# DEBT

Known defects, deferred obligations, and unverified claims. Stable IDs so a
session can reference an item without restating it. Nothing is removed from this
file; resolved items keep their row and gain a resolution.

Severity: `defect` (shipped and wrong), `gap` (missing and known), `drift`
(two sources disagree), `unverified` (claimed but not independently established),
`design` (a decision deliberately deferred).

| ID | Sev | Found | Item | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| D-45 | gap | 2026-07-26 | **An unbacked register entry is refused at RATIFY, not at promote, so one can sit in the working corpus until publication.** REWRITTEN 2026-07-26; the first version of this entry said "nothing anywhere refuses it", which was wrong and was written before reading `src/gate.mjs`. `runGate` carries the invariant explicitly, under the comment "The plane's own remaining duty: bytes the register claims must exist": it probes R2 per register row and refuses with `PLANE_MISSING_BYTES`, or `PLANE_SIZE` when the register and the stored object disagree. That runs on ratify, which is the publication boundary and the two-bucket fence, and is arguably exactly the right place for it. What remains true is narrower: `promote` verifies nothing, so a provenance claim about bytes that exist nowhere is accepted into the WORKING corpus and reported as success, and stays undetected until someone ratifies or runs `op=registeraudit`. Whether to close that at promote is a design question, not an obvious fix. The obvious fix is wrong and was tried and reverted: requiring every entry to name a file in the same payload refuses `migrate.mjs`'s deliberate and correct pattern, and `migrate.test.mjs` failed on it immediately. A real promote-time guard would need R2 and therefore could not sit inside promote's `transactionSync`. DEFAULT POSITION: leave it, because the fence is where the fence should be, and `op=registeraudit` now reports the condition on demand. | ACCEPTED · open, default is to leave it |
| D-50 | gap | 2026-07-26 | **Project name uniqueness is enforced at the write path but not in the check catalog.** v2 section 11 item 8 asks for both. `promote` refuses a colliding name, so nothing can be written wrong, but `bio-checks` cannot report the condition on a corpus it is handed from elsewhere, which is what the conformance path is for. Lower priority than the write path, which is the one that prevents the damage. | M7 · open |
| D-52 | gap | 2026-07-26 | **An export is recorded but no administrator is NOTIFIED.** Section 8.1 says an export is recorded in the append-only history AND every administrator is notified. `export_log` delivers the first half and is readable by in-app administrators through `op=exportlog`, so an export is discoverable. There is no notification channel in this system at all, so the second half is not implemented and an administrator who never looks will not learn of an export. Closing this needs a channel decision (in-app banner on next sign-in, or the communications surface the Roadmap describes) rather than more code here. | M7 · open |
| D-53 | gap | 2026-07-26 | **BIO has no reputation or credence mechanism, and S-11 step 5 is blocked on deciding whether it needs one.** Grepped, not assumed: no credence, reputation, trust or confidence field, table or check exists in `checks/` or `src/`. Bob's framing is that information from a credible source can be ratified with trust inherited from that source, that member ratification adds that member's trust, and that trust never decreases. Two existing doctrine statements bear on it and one partly contradicts it: `BIO_Technical_Architecture_Decisions_v10` **No transitive trust** says trust is never inherited but re-established locally at every hop and credibility is never demonstrated by the producer's reputation, scoped to PEER BIO GROUPS and the R13 infiltration threat rather than to external primary sources; and `BIO_Design_Requirements_v2` section 4 says reputation accrues through consistent quality with no authority managing the process, so any mechanism must be a group's own ledger and not a shared score. Blocking finding for any design: **references target a bundle id and nothing finer** (`BUNDLE_ID_RE`), with no anchor, span or claim identifier anywhere in the catalog, so credence attached to an edge is still document-granular and cannot distinguish an authoritative table of figures from a self-interested assertion in the same document. See SESSION-KICKOFF.md for the full parked analysis. | DOCTRINE (Bob) · open, blocks S-11 step 5 |

Either the plan joins docs/architecture or the references should stop implying a document a reader can find. | ACCEPTED · open; probe 1 (RETRIEVAL-PROBE.md) recorded actuals with no prediction table to check against, as this gap predicts |
| D-19 | gap | 2026-07-24 | **Migrated manifest entries carry the migration's wall clock** as their `created`, not the original promotion time, so C-12.1 reports 18 bundles as having a live bundle.md older than a superseded version. | D-32 | gap | 2026-07-25 | **The shipped retrieval path is 3.5x slower than the probe it inherited its numbers from.** MEASURED, 2026-07-25, by `npm run bench:retrieval` (test/retrieval-scale.mjs), which loads a corpus through the real `promote` and drives the real `op=search`. At 20,000 bundles the shipped path runs 5ms to 163ms; probe 2 recorded nothing above ~46ms. The gap is real work the probe did not do: a 26-column provenance projection per hit, the viewer gate, bm25 with snippets, and a facet pass. Two structural causes were found and fixed in 0.17.0, halving the worst shape from 305ms: the gate was a CTE intersected into every statement, which cost a full table scan per statement, and is now a WHERE predicate on rows already selected; and the facet pass ran one statement per field, rebuilding the same scope six times, and is now batched. What remains is inherent to the contract rather than accidental, so closing this further means a covering index for the provenance columns, or counting facets from one scan in JS instead of a GROUP BY per field. Bob's decision, 2026-07-25: ship in the measured condition and stop quoting the probe's numbers as if they described this code. | M5 · measured 0.17.0; further optimisation open |
| D-33 | gap | 2026-07-25 | **The id tiebreak is required by argument, not by a failing test.** Every sort compiles to `ORDER BY <field> <dir>, bundle_id ASC` because without a total order a row can appear on two pages or on none. `test/search.test.mjs` builds 600 bundles that all carry one value on the sort field and pages the whole set. Removing the tiebreak from the compiler and re-running it STILL PASSES: at that size SQLite returns tied rows in a deterministic order. So the order is deterministic but undeclared, which is the real hazard, since the delivery order is a property of the plan and the plan changes with corpus size, with an added index, and with an engine upgrade. The guard that actually holds is at compile time, in `test/query.test.mjs`, which asserts every sortable field in both directions ends in a unique column. Demonstrating the runtime failure needs a corpus large enough to spill the sorter, which belongs with D-32 and not in a suite. | M0 · open |

| D-36 | defect | 2026-07-25 | **workerd's SQL limits are orders of magnitude below SQLite's documented defaults, and a bench found them rather than the suite.** Two ceilings, both binary-searched through the real code path on 2026-07-25: a statement may bind about **100 variables** (SQLite's default is 32,766) and a compound SELECT may have **five terms** (SQLite's default is 500). The first broke a chunked id list at 400 per chunk; the second broke a six-arm facet statement AND, more seriously, would have broken the query compiler itself, since six metadata filters is one ordinary pass over a filter sidebar and compiles to six INTERSECT arms. Both are now guarded, with the id chunk at 64 and compound chains nesting through subqueries at 4 per compound, and `test/query.test.mjs` asserts the nesting across four query shapes. The open item is the CLASS: these limits are undocumented, they are not the platform's published behaviour, and any new statement shape can meet another one. The lesson is the one that keeps recurring in this repository, which is that a bench that runs the real path at real size finds what a suite at small scale cannot. | M5 · guarded 0.17.0; the class is open |
| D-38 | design | 2026-07-25 | **The number of Information records a Project can cite is bounded by INLINE_MAX, not by SELECTION_MAX_ITEMS, and the two were set independently.** Citing writes one frontmatter entry per cited record into a single `bundle.md`, measured at 83 bytes per edge at a 25-character bundle id. The enumeration cap is 10,000 and the inline file limit is 1MB, so a MAXIMUM LEGAL SELECTION originally produced a 1,070,846-byte document and was refused by `promote` with `OVERSIZE_INLINE`: an error about inline byte storage, in answer to an operator who selected a legal number of records and pressed cite. Found by `test/cite-scale.mjs`, not by the suite. Two changes in 0.18.0 make it survivable rather than resolved: the Session Log entry is bounded at 20 named ids plus a count (it had listed every id, costing a further 24 bytes per edge), which took an edge from 107 to 84 bytes and lets a maximum 10,000 selection fit at 841KB; and `CITATION_TOO_LARGE` now refuses before anything is written, naming the real limit and the room remaining. The ceiling is now reachable only CUMULATIVELY, and is: at 12,000 edges a further 2,000 refuses and leaves the document byte-identical. `bundle.md` cannot spill to R2 to escape this, because the gate compares it byte-wise against history (C-5, C-12) and `schema.mjs` keeps every file the gate reads inline by rule. Bob's decision, 2026-07-25: leave the ceiling where it is. The tested ceiling is far above any plausible group, lowering the selection cap would penalise selections that are not for citing, and there may be Cloudflare configuration raising these limits that has not been explored. Revisit when a real corpus approaches it. | ACCEPTED · open, by decision |
| D-40 | defect | 2026-07-25 | **Test and bench fixtures write `criticality: notable`, which is not a legal value.** The catalog's enum is `crucial` or `supporting` (C-2.7), so every Information bundle these fixtures build is non-conformant, and a conformance assertion run over one would fail for a reason unrelated to what it was testing. Found on the deployed 0.19.0 by auditing the scratch namespace after a live check, not by any suite, because the suites conformance-check the PROJECT they cite into and never the Information they cite. Fixed in the fixtures written this session (`cite`, `edges`, `cite-scale`, `limits-probe`). NOT fixed in `retrieval-scale.mjs`, `search.test.mjs` and `selection.test.mjs`, where `notable` is deliberately used as facet and filter test DATA (`q=criticality:notable` is a bench shape measuring the dominant-value case) and changing it would rewrite assertions to fix a fixture rather than a defect. The real risk is a future session copying one of those fixtures as a template. | M0 · open, narrowed |
| D-42 | gap | 2026-07-25 | **Burner-URL invitations are specified but only half built.** Section 6 wants a URL carrying a one-time code that is SPENT ON USE and afterwards resolves to nothing, carrying no record of what it formerly addressed, so a leaked or archived link is inert and reveals neither the group nor the invitee. What exists is an invite code the administrator hands over, reached through a URL fragment (the D-14 fix), and the code is correctly cleared at enrolment so it cannot be replayed. What is missing is the URL itself being the credential and resolving to nothing afterwards rather than to an enrolment form that says the invite is spent. Unblocked. | M7 · open |

| D-376 | gap | 2026-09-15 | **THE FOURTH DOCUMENT CLASS IN M0-32's MEASURED ORDER — THE STAFF DIRECTORY — HAS NO CONTENT TYPE, AND THE REASON IS THAT NO DOCUMENT OF THE CLASS IN THIS CORPUS CAN BE READ AT ALL.** Found by FW-18 while landing `EXTRACTION-BREADTH-DESIGN.md` §7 row 2 (the other three classes of the order landed: `meeting_minutes`, `staff_report`, `regulation`). **MEASURED 2026-09-15 rather than inferred, two ways** (`MEASUREMENTS.md` M-21 §5): every one of **30 name-matched directory PDFs** in `s3://cao-94612` decoded **0–100 characters against 1,204–33,189 undetermined markers** — 0 of 30 readable, all single-page scans and scanned booklets; and a **fixed-seed walk of 300 bucket PDFs** decoded 45 (15%) and found **none** of the class. The second figure is NOT evidence of absence and is not read as one: at M0-32's own rate (~395 directories in a 29,626-item text-bearing stratum, 1.3%) the expectation over 45 decoded documents is ~0.6, so the walk is consistent with rarity. **WHY THIS IS NOT A CONTENT-TYPE GAP WEARING ITS OWN CLOTHES:** `readText` refuses a document whose undetermined count exceeds its decoded characters — the plane's own measured essentially-nothing line — so for these documents **no reading is attempted and no content type is ever consulted**. A better matcher would never run. The class is invisible to the content axis because it is invisible to the text tier. **WHAT WAS NOT DONE, and deliberately:** a directory reader was NOT written from a document nobody could read. `docprofile/doctypes/registry.mjs`'s own standing rule is that an unmeasured content type is not written, because a type invented from what a document probably looks like reassures people about things it has not understood — and this row exists so that restraint is a recorded gap rather than a silence. **COSTS WHILE OPEN:** the smallest class of the four (~395 items) is unread, so a member asking who holds an office, or which offices a body has, gets nothing from the store and cannot tell *nobody extracted it* from *nobody captured it* — `CLAUDE.md`'s sparse-at-every-level obligation, unmet for one class. **CLOSING IT TAKES**, in order: (1) a tier-3 (OCR) path reaching this class, which EXISTS as a fleet member (`ocr-worker`, tesseract-wasm, cap C, CPDF-10) but is not reached on the read path for an already-captured document — that is D-319 / `EXTRACTION-BREADTH-DESIGN.md` §5.1 and BREADTH §7 row 5, which is CONTENT-PDF's and is not this row's remedy to build; (2) then one FRAMEWORK item to write the type from a directory that has been read, which is one register() call and one file, on the pattern the other three establish. **It is sequenced behind (1) and cannot be started before it**, which is why it is a row rather than a queue item. | M2 · FRAMEWORK · **open, and its BLOCK IS LIFTED 2026-09-18: CPDF-19 closed D-319**, so an already-captured scan can now be re-read to tier 3 through `op=pdfstructure&ocr=1` and the reading it produces is run through the content-type registry (`readText` → `identify`/`doctypeFor`) and persisted — FW-20 re-takes the decode census over re-read directories and writes the type if they now decode. *Prior disposition, kept:* **open, BLOCKED ON D-319 / BREADTH §7 row 5, and the block is VERIFIED rather than inherited**: grepped on this tree 2026-09-15 — `needsTier3`/`tier3Pages` exist and `op=acquire` reaches tier 3, and the read-time structure op does not, which is exactly what D-319 names. Disposition is OPEN-WITH-A-NAMED-SEQUENCE: nothing to decide, one dependency to land, then one small item. |
| D-377 | gap | 2026-09-15 | **THE PLANE'S COMMITTED BUNDLE IS A GENERATED ARTIFACT OF `docprofile/`, AND THAT RULE IS WRITTEN DOWN FOR THE UI'S FLATTENED COPY AND NOWHERE FOR THE PLANE'S.** Found by FW-18 the only way it can be found today — by paying for it. A pristine baseline on this worktree read **201/201 green · 12,467 assertions**; the first full run on the branch read **200/201, `fleetbundles.test.mjs` 83 pass / 4 FAIL**, naming *a fresh build of `src/index.mjs` is byte-identical to the committed `dist/bio-plane.bundled.mjs`* — **with no plane source touched by the item at all.** The cause is structural and correct: `src/index.mjs` imports `docprofile/registry.mjs`, so `docprofile/` is among the bundle's 50 first-party inputs (the manifest the rebuild wrote says so), and D-298's gate did exactly its job. `node bio-plane/scripts/build-plane.mjs` restored it to **87 pass / 0 fail** — no version bumped, nothing signed, nothing tagged, and `embed:sign` deliberately not run, per that script's own header. **THE GAP IS THE RECORD, NOT THE MECHANISM.** CONDUCT ruled at FW-17's integration that `civicos-ui/app.html`'s flattened docprofile embed is a generated artifact of `docprofile/` and *whoever changes `docprofile/` regenerates it in the same commit*; that ruling is in `INTERFACE-CHANGES.md` and in `QUEUE.md`'s FW-17 `landed:` line, and it names ONE of the two generated copies. `DOCUMENT-PROFILES.md`'s *What depends on it* names `app.html`'s flattened copy AND its drift check and does NOT name the plane's committed bundle. `kickoffs/FRAMEWORK.md` — the file a FRAMEWORK worker actually reads before touching `docprofile/**` — names neither. **COSTS WHILE OPEN:** every future `docprofile` worker meets a green-to-red battery on a suite it did not touch, on a file it did not claim, and must diagnose from the failure text that it is holding a build step rather than a defect; FW-17 changed four files under `docprofile/` on 2026-09-14 and its release line records the embed regeneration and not this one, so the cost is already once-realised and once-unrecorded. It is `CLAUDE.md`'s own rule — *a mechanism that is not in the loop the reader actually runs is not a mechanism* — with the loop being a kickoff rather than a gate. **CLOSING IT TAKES** one sentence in TWO files and no code: `kickoffs/FRAMEWORK.md`, beside the claim instruction, saying that `docprofile/**` has TWO generated copies (`civicos-ui/app.html`'s embed via `tools/bundle-docprofile.mjs`, and `bio-plane/dist/bio-plane.bundled.mjs` + its manifest via `bio-plane/scripts/build-plane.mjs`) and both are regenerated in the same commit; and `DOCUMENT-PROFILES.md`'s *What depends on it*, which should name the plane's bundle beside `app.html`. **It is CONDUCT's to place** — `kickoffs/FRAMEWORK.md` is rewritten by a FRAMEWORK session at its close and `DOCUMENT-PROFILES.md` is FRAMEWORK's own, but the RULING that made the first copy an author's obligation was CONDUCT's, and the second copy should be added by the same hand rather than asserted by a worker. | M2 · CONDUCT · **open, and the disposition is OPEN-WITH-A-NAMED-EDIT rather than accepted**: nothing to build, nothing to decide, two sentences to place in the two files named above; FW-18 rebuilt the artifact and claimed the path in the same commit, so `main` is not stale — what is open is only whether the next worker will know to. |
| D-378 | gap | 2026-09-15 | **THE TRUNCATION GRADER READS ONE SPELLING OF THE CLAIM, `X.length > Y`, SO SEVEN `truncated` FIGURES ARE IN NO ROSTER IT PRINTS AT ALL — NOT GRADED, NOT UNGRADED, NOT IN VIOLATION.** Found by M0-38 while closing D-369's reachable half, and the proof that the finding is owed rather than pedantic is inside D-369 itself: **that row NAMES `biasManifest` as one of the four methods it is about, and `biasManifest` publishes `truncated: from + page.length < all.length` — an OFFSET form `TRUNC_RE` cannot match — so a debt row named it and no instrument ever did.** MEASURED 2026-09-15 on `origin/main` `58b77ea` with a reader shipped in `derivation-bounds.test.mjs`, which now PRINTS and PINS the roster so a new one must be declared: three OFFSET forms (`biasManifest`, `versionChain`, `basisVersions` — `from + page.length < all.length`), two `>=` forms where the grader reads only `>` (`search: ids.length >= IDS_MAX`, `extractProposals: listed.length >= n`), one disjunction whose FIRST term is not a length comparison (`deriveConnections: rowsCut || distinct.length > endsCap`), and one claim that is not a length comparison at all (`suggestVersion: rc ? !rc.legs_complete : false`). Two further sites REPUBLISH a figure computed elsewhere and are counted separately rather than folded in. **THIS IS THE LIST-OF-SPELLINGS FAILURE `derivation-bounds.test.mjs`'s own header was written against, met inside the instrument that carries that header** — and it is the same shape as REC-88's finding one screen up (M0-40's row), where a hoisted loop iterable takes a method off the class roster: an instrument defeated by a spelling. **A `gap` AND NOT A `defect`, measured rather than judged:** every one of the seven is a correct read today, each was inspected at its site, and the `>=` spellings fail in the SAFE direction (they announce truncation when exactly the cap was returned, which over-reports rather than under-reports). **COST WHILE OPEN:** any of the seven can have its cut moved away from its published cap and nothing will say so — which is exactly the defect D-365 measured for SQL bounds and D-369 for in-memory ones, in the one population neither instrument enumerates. It is bounded by the pin: a NEW unread form must be declared in the suite before it can pass, so the population cannot grow in silence. **CLOSING IT TAKES** widening `TRUNC_RE` to read the offset and `>=` forms and re-deriving REC-99's `graded`/`ungraded` rosters and M0-38's in-memory rosters from the wider reader — one edit, four pinned rosters re-measured from the instrument's own printed output, and an over-strictness arm proving the wider reader does not enrol a claim that is not a bound. **M0-38 did NOT do it**, and the reason is on this row rather than left to be guessed: widening the regex enlarges REC-99's two rosters and M0-38's three in the same breath, and that item's claim on the file was ADDITIVE — an item that rewrites a neighbouring item's pins inside its own landing is the scope creep `PARALLELISM.md` forbids, and the population is fully declared in the meantime. | M0 · **open, disposition OPEN-WITH-A-NAMED-EDIT rather than accepted** — the remedy is one regex and five re-measured rosters, the population is PINNED by name today so it cannot grow unseen, and the owner is whoever next holds `bio-plane/test/derivation-bounds.test.mjs`. **It should be taken WITH or AFTER M0-40**, which edits the same file for the sibling spelling defect in the class walk; two workers in that file at once is the collision the 2026-09-15 wave deliberately avoided. |

| D-380 | defect | 2026-09-16 | **THE BATTERY'S FLEET SKIP MESSAGE TELLS `agent-worker` TO RUN A COMMAND THAT EXITS `EUSAGE`, AND TELLS IT TO ADOPT THE IDIOM IT ALREADY IMPLEMENTS — AND UNDERNEATH IT THE FLEET IS RUNNING TWO RESOLUTION IDIOMS, ONE OF WHICH GOES DARK ON A FRESH WORKTREE.** Found by FLEET #1's stand-up, driving the named measurement in its brief rather than reasoning about it; every figure is **M-31**. **THE HALF THAT IS A DEFECT TODAY.** `bio-plane/scripts/battery.mjs`'s `fleetDepSkip` composes its remediation from the member's OWN directory plus a hardcoded exemplar: *run `npm ci` in <member>/, or have the suite resolve it from the plane's install as agent-worker's does*. For `agent-worker` **both halves are wrong**: `npm ci` there exits **`npm error code EUSAGE`** and creates nothing, because a package with no dependencies has no `package-lock.json` (the four-manifest table in M-31 §1) — and the second half names that member's own file as the exemplar to copy, which it has been since FL-2. **The instruction that works is `npm ci` in `bio-plane/`, which no version of the message names.** The same message is CORRECT for `ocr-worker`, so this is a per-member question rather than a wording fix. **THE HALF THAT IS THE REAL FINDING.** `agent-worker` and `pdf-worker` resolve `miniflare` through an explicit two-step loader (bare `import`, then `createRequire(bio-plane/package.json).resolve`) and therefore RUN with their own directory empty — MEASURED at **5/5 green, 628 assertions, `agent-worker/node_modules` absent, plane installed**. `ocr-worker/test/ocr-worker.test.mjs:46` imports `miniflare` bare with no fallback and is **DARK** in the same condition (`0 member(s) actually RAN · DARK: ocr-worker`). **The comment above `fleetDepSkip` asserts "THIS PATH IS NOW UNREACHABLE FOR EVERY MEMBER THAT EXISTS" on the premise that "the plane's install is present wherever this battery can run at all" — REPRODUCED FALSE 2026-09-16** on a pristine worktree: the battery carries no dependencies, so it runs with nothing installed, and four `agent-worker` suites went dark at **exit 0**. That comment PREDICTED its own falsification (it keeps the branch as *the landing pad for the NEXT member, which will be written before it has adopted the idiom*) and `ocr-worker` is that member; the landing pad worked, and nobody adopted the idiom afterwards. **COSTS WHILE OPEN:** on any fresh worktree `ocr-worker` is silently unrun while the battery reports **green and exits 0** — the residual the comment itself names as *the fleet's to weigh*, now with a receipt; and a session that meets the skip and obeys the message gets an error for one member, or copies a file that needs no copying. It is the trap `CLAUDE.md` already carries one package further out (*a baseline measured before `npm ci` is a wrong number carrying full confidence*), arriving as a REMEDIATION that cannot be performed. **CLOSING IT TAKES** two edits in two areas, NEITHER taken by the finder and both routed rather than left as prose: (1) **FLEET's ground** — give `ocr-worker`'s suite the same two-step loader, which M-31 §6 establishes is sufficient because its only bare import is `miniflare` (the engine's wasm and language model are COMMITTED ASSETS, not `node_modules` reads), leaving the member's own install necessary for its BUILD alone; (2) **the battery owner's ground** — correct the remediation to name the resolution path per member and correct the unreachability comment, DELEGATED in `CLAIMS.md` 2026-09-16 (FLEET → whoever owns `battery.mjs`), because FL-9 set FLEET's own precedent that the runner is not this area's ground. The two are COUPLED and the delegation says so: if (1) lands, the exemplar becomes accurate for all three members; if it does not, the message must name the path per member. | M0 · FLEET + the battery owner · **open, disposition OPEN-WITH-A-NAMED-EDIT rather than accepted** — nothing to decide and nothing to design; one loader to copy and one message to correct, both measured, neither taken because activation and routing are the LEAD's and this was a stand-up. **The condition is bounded meanwhile**: the `fleet:` line names a dark member on every run (`DARK: <name>`), so the hole is reported — what is not bounded is that the headline and the exit status both still read healthy. |

## How to use this file

> **110 CLOSED debt rows were rolled to `docs/archive/ledgers/DEBT-closed-2026-08.md` on
> 2026-08-10**, unedited — 217 KB of the 532 KB this file had reached. Every OPEN row
> stayed. `planning-hygiene` asserts a disposition token per row and names each one, so
> it now checks 171 rows rather than 281 and checks them all; `mintid` reads the D floor
> from both files, and the floor is unchanged. A closed row's reasoning is still indexed
> — `node tools/decided.mjs "<subject>"`.


Reference items by ID in commit messages and in session notes. When a step in
`PLAN.md` resolves an item, change its status to the commit that did it rather
than deleting the row: the history of what was wrong is worth as much as the
record of what was fixed, and a ledger that only shows current problems teaches
nobody anything.

Items marked `accepted` are known, judged tolerable, and carry the reasoning in
the row. They are not resolved and should be re-read when circumstances change.
| D-54 | gap | 2026-07-29 | **The installer does not check or enforce the Workers plan.** BIO now depends on platform limits that differ by plan, and a group installing on Workers Free gets a working instance that behaves differently from every description of it: 50 external subrequests per invocation instead of 10,000, 10ms of CPU per request instead of 30s, 10 minutes of browser time per DAY instead of 10 hours per month. All of that is survivable (the plane calibrates its own ceiling and captures across ticks) and none of it is visible to the person installing. The installer must detect the plan, state plainly what capture will and will not do on it, and record the answer where the instance can report it. Enforcement is the open question: REFUSING to install on Free would contradict the ruling that Workers Paid is an optimisation and never a requirement, so the likely shape is inform-and-record rather than refuse. Wrangler's `limits.subrequests` is also unset in this repo and should be set explicitly once a plan is known, because the default moved from 1,000 to 10,000 in February 2026 and a default that moves is not a decision. | M2 · open **CHANGED IN CHARACTER 2026-08-04 by DEC-42: the installer no longer merely DETECTS the plan — it REQUIRES Workers Paid, VERIFIES it, and refuses to complete honestly rather than installing something quietly degraded.** That refusal IS this row's fix now: the D-106 failure this guards against (a group installing today getting something quietly different from every description of it) is closed by refusing, not by warning. Free is no longer a supported configuration. |
| D-55 | design | 2026-07-29 | **A rendered capture may contain content whose authority is not the hosting site, and `capture.authority` holds exactly one value.** Bob's ruling: if content produced by a third-party script is ever evidence, it must be recorded as produced by that third party and not by the hosting site. The provenance document has one `authority` per capture, so a rendered document carrying an advertiser's, an analytics vendor's, or a CDN-hosted widget's output cannot currently attribute it. Attribution has to be per-origin and sub-document, which is a shape the register has never held: `references[]` targets a bundle and nothing finer, the same granularity problem D-53 is blocked on. Deciding this is a precondition for treating any rendered capture as evidence, not a follow-on. | DOCTRINE (Bob) · BLOCKS D-64 · open, blocks CLIENT-RENDERED.md |
| D-56 | unverified | 2026-07-29 | **CPU headroom: MEASURED 2026-07-29, and the worry was unfounded on the numbers available. HELD OPEN DELIBERATELY as a watch item with no task attached.** The entry asked whether capture was near the free plan's documented 10ms CPU ceiling. Measured on biosmoke7 (Workers Free) at 0.44.0: `op=cpuprobe` completed 20 steps of 2,000,000 reference iterations, 40,000,000 in total, and the isolate was killed during step 21 with HTTP 503 `error code: 1102`. Forty million modular multiplications is not 10ms of anything, so whatever is enforced on this account is not the documented figure. Real captures are nowhere near it: the heaviest measured, a news front page, does 49 compute calls over 16.97MB of hashing and parsing. **A SEPARATE AND MORE IMPORTANT FINDING came out of trying: a Worker CANNOT TIME ITSELF.** Cloudflare freezes `Date.now()` during synchronous execution as a timing-attack defence, so the first meter reported zero for every segment of every real capture. Any millisecond figure reported from inside a Worker is a fabrication. Consumption is therefore counted in WORK (calls, bytes) and the ceiling in REFERENCE ITERATIONS; see `src/cpu.mjs`. **WHY THIS STAYS OPEN.** A CPU overrun terminates the isolate: there is no catchable error, no chance to record anything, and the caller sees an opaque 1102. Unlike the subrequest ceiling, this failure mode can never announce itself, so a future scope of work can begin silently overrunning it and the only symptom will be requests that stop working. Nothing is asked of any session by this row. It exists so that an unexplained 1102, or a capture that fails only on large inputs, is read as a CPU overrun on the first guess instead of the fifth. If work volume per capture ever grows by an order of magnitude, re-run `op=cpuprobe` and compare. | WATCH, no task · open as a watch item, no task |
| D-57 | defect | 2026-07-30 | **`resolveLinks` describes a self-reference as a target that CHANGED, and the UI prints that sentence to a member verbatim.** Found on live data during the U8 exercise. The bracket arm needs `observations > 1` and does not fire; the `before && after` arm then matches THE SAME ROW on both sides and returns `undetermined` with the basis "the target changed somewhere between the captures bracketing this document's retrieval", plus a detail naming one capture hash twice: `bracketing captures differ: fc7f4bb39281 last seen 2026-07-30T00:18:02Z, fc7f4bb39281 first seen 2026-07-30T00:18:02Z`. Nothing changed and the two captures are one capture. The verdict value is defensible; the BASIS is false, and because the UI shows the plane's basis in the plane's own words rather than paraphrasing it, a member reads a fabricated claim about a source. The fix belongs in the plane, not the viewer: `resolveLinks` should recognise `pick.capture_sha === sourceCapture` and report a self-reference, which is a fourth basis rather than a fourth verdict. The UI already counts self-references apart from connections (see below), so the count is right while the sentence is wrong. | M3 · open |
| D-59 | unverified | 2026-07-30 | **`contemporaneous` has never been observed on real data, and the strongest verdict arm may be unreachable for the sources BIO actually captures.** The arm requires one set of bytes observed on both sides of a document's retrieval, `observations > 1` on a single `captured_locators` row. Measured 2026-07-30 on `oakland.legistar.com`: two captures of `Legislation.aspx` twelve minutes apart produced two DIFFERENT hashes (`7f1083d74f30`, `8691808a30ab`), because ASP.NET pages carry per-response viewstate and timestamps. A page whose bytes differ on every request can never produce an `observations > 1` row, so identical-byte bracketing cannot fire for it regardless of whether the content changed. That makes `undetermined` not merely the common case but the ONLY reachable verdict for a large class of municipal sources, which is honest and is also weaker than the design assumed. The other establishing routes named in LINK-FIDELITY.md (a timestamp token over the target's hash, a third-party archive, monitoring across the interval with no change detected) do not depend on byte identity and are now the load-bearing ones rather than the fallbacks. Worth measuring across more hosts before deciding whether the bracket arm earns its complexity. | M3 · open |
| D-60 | design | 2026-07-30 | **A capture has no STABLE DIGEST, so change detection, duplicate detection and contemporaneity all run on a hash that moves for reasons that are not changes.** MEASURED 2026-07-30, two fetches of `oakland.legistar.com/Calendar.aspx` three seconds apart: identical length, **114,177 differing bytes, 31% of the document**, every differing byte inside exactly two hidden fields, `__VIEWSTATE` (115,096 bytes) and `__EVENTVALIDATION` (876 bytes). Normalise those two and the remaining **252,948 bytes, 68.6%, are byte-identical**. Nothing about the calendar changed. ASP.NET reserialises its control tree and reissues its anti-forgery list on every response, and municipal publishing runs on this class of software. Three separate mechanisms are wrong because of it: **monitoring** reports a change on every tick and therefore reports nothing; **duplicate detection** does not fire on re-captures of the pages that get re-captured most, so the record grows a second bundle for one document while C-18.3 and `op=audit` both stay silent because the hashes genuinely differ; and **contemporaneity's strongest arm** can never fire (see D-59). The fix is one thing: beside its raw identity hash, a capture carries a derived digest computed with known-volatile regions normalised to a placeholder. Identity stays raw and raw bytes are never rewritten; comparison uses the stable digest. Volatile regions are RECORDED with their field names, offsets and lengths, and the fact that two fetches differed only there is itself a dated observation worth keeping. Classification is per-family, heuristic, and reclassifiable exactly as chrome detection is, discovered by measurement rather than declared. A volatile region is never treated as evidence. Design written into LINK-FIDELITY.md. **PARTLY DONE 2026-07-30:** the volatile machinery is NOT at `civicos-ui/volatile.mjs` (that path does not exist; the row was wrong). It lives in `docprofile/index.mjs` (the `digests` and `compare` exports) with per-family handlers in `docprofile/handlers/` (`aspnet-webforms.mjs` is the Legistar/viewstate case), validated both directions against two live captures of one Legistar page. The PLANE has not adopted it: monitoring, `op=audit`'s duplicate sweep, and `resolveLinks`' bracket arm all still compare raw hashes, so the plane should import `digests`/`compare` rather than grow a second copy. **ADOPTION FEASIBILITY MEASURED 2026-07-30:** docprofile is 37KB of pure JS, zero external deps, zero node builtins, only internal cross-imports. Bundled through the plane's own esbuild target it tree-shakes to **5.3KB** against 2.4MB of headroom under the 3MB Free limit, so the bundle-size concern that applies to unpdf (D-91) does NOT apply here. docprofile sits at repo root OUTSIDE `bio-plane/`; the import path from `src/` is `../../docprofile/index.mjs`, and because `build` inlines all imports into one self-contained `dist/bio-plane.bundled.mjs`, the deployed artifact and the installer are unaffected by the cross-directory location as long as `docprofile/` is present at BUILD time (it is, in a clean checkout). Adoption is a build task, not an investigation. | M3 · open — **PARTLY STALE, corrected 2026-08-06: the PLANE HAS ADOPTED docprofile** (`bio-plane/src/index.mjs:70` imports `identify, doctypeFor, profileRecord, digests, CONFIDENCE, readText` from `docprofile/registry.mjs`), so "the PLANE has not adopted it" is no longer true as written. **CHECKED 2026-08-06 (sub-session). THE ROW IS ONE-THIRD DISCHARGED, and my own 2026-08-06 correction above was half-right — it is true of the WRITE path only.** `compare()` is **never called anywhere in `bio-plane/src/`**; the only caller in the repo outside docprofile itself is the browser (`app.html:13676`). The plane calls `identify()` and `digests()` in exactly one place, `op=acquire` (`index.mjs:3251`, `:3314`). Site by site: **(a) MONITORING — STILL RAW.** `index.mjs:3834-3838` digests the fetched bytes with `crypto.subtle` and compares `seen === baseline` against `provenance.json`'s recorded sha. No digest, no compare. On a Legistar page this reports `modified` every tick, which is this row's original measurement, undischarged. **(b) AUDIT'S DUPLICATE DETECTION — DISCHARGED.** `checkRegisterIntegrity` (`bio-checks.mjs:3553-3558`) buckets on `docs[i].profile.digests.evidentiary` when `determined === true`, beside the raw arm, firing only when ≥2 different raw shas share a digest. **So this row's claim that the duplicate sweep compares raw hashes is STALE, and `DOCUMENT-PROFILES.md:309-310` repeats the stale claim** — delegated to FRAMEWORK in `CLAIMS.md` *[DISCHARGED 2026-09-14: FW-16's front matter marks that §Known gaps bullet stale in the document's own Incomplete list; no live delegation existed in `CLAIMS.md` — the owed act had survived only inside this row; the body correction is M0-27]*. But note the scope: it is INTRA-BUNDLE, and no cross-bundle sweep exists at all. **(c) `resolveLinks`' CONTEMPORANEITY BRACKET — STILL RAW.** `store.mjs:14859-14873` brackets over `captured_locators` rows whose identity is raw `capture_sha`, so on a viewstate-churning page every fetch mints a fresh row with `observations = 1`, `bracket` is always null, and the verdict degrades to undetermined. **This is D-59's "strongest arm can never fire", still true.** So what remains on this row is (a) and (c) |
| D-63 | gap | 2026-07-30 | **Only three stacks have been measured, and every unmeasured one is a handler waiting for evidence.** `docprofile/` now has a structure for recognising a document's stack and kind and deciding what is evidentiary, what is furniture and what is per-render machinery, and it is validated against ASP.NET WebForms (Legistar), WordPress (Oaklandside) and a client-rendered app (OpenGov). Everything else in Oakland's orbit falls to the conservative handler, which is safe and noisy: Drupal, plain static HTML, Granicus, CivicPlus, NextRequest, Squarespace, and the county's own sites. The work per stack is small and specific: fetch the same page twice, diff, attribute the differences, decide whether the document has an identifiable boundary, and write the handler with the measurement in its comments. The noise the conservative handler makes on an unmeasured source is the signal that the source needs a handler, so this row is a standing invitation rather than a blocker. Do not add a rule without a measurement: a careless rule hides a real change, which is the only failure mode here that matters. | M2 · open, ongoing |
| D-64 | gap | 2026-07-30 | **A client-rendered page is recognised as a shell and still cannot be captured as evidence.** `docprofile`'s `client_rendered` handler detects the case structurally (a body with no links and no prose) and carries a member-facing warning that what was collected is the frame and not the figures. That is the honest interim and not the requirement. The standing ruling is that JS-rendered content IS the content and must be captured at the SAME GRADE as the rest of the document, because the render happened in the site's own execution environment at capture time while the HTML/CSS rendition is rendered later in the reader's. That needs the Browser Rendering path, which is available on Workers Free at 10 minutes a day. Until it lands, `oaklandca.opengov.com` and every portal like it can be captured and cannot be cited, and the record should say so on the document page rather than only at capture time. | D-74 | gap | 2026-07-30 | **Oakland's shared identifier spaces have not been measured, and they are the cheapest large win available.** Framework §8.3: a progression inside one system can reach Grade A because that system links its own stages, but one crossing systems collapses to Grade C, since a procurement portal, a finance system and a legislative record each keep their own identifier space and none links to the others. That is precisely where the most consequential progressions live. The lever is that institutions DO reuse certain identifiers across systems, and finding which ones is empirical work exactly like measuring a host stack: a contract or purchase order number, a project or CIP number, a resolution or ordinance number, an APN, a fund or account code. Each identifier found in two systems converts an entire progression from Grade C to Grade B. | D-75 | design | 2026-07-30 | **The content framework and the object catalogue have been developed independently and have never been connected.** Framework §12, added at Bob's direction that the system must support goals, objectives and aspirations: the record already models intent as `project` (which carries an `objective` field, states `forming → investigating → matured → closed`), `focus` and `problem` (open questions and obstacles), and `action` (steps). The framework was written through eleven sections without touching any of it. Two object types genuinely do not exist and are needed: ASPIRATION, a standing commitment that never closes and sets priority, and GOAL, a bounded pursuit that eventually does. Conflating the three is the ordinary failure: an aspiration written as an objective is never finished and demoralises, and an objective written as an aspiration is never checked and quietly abandons itself. | M4 · open |
| D-76 | design | 2026-07-30 | **Progress on an objective is reported rather than derived, and it should be computed from the record.** Framework §12's load-bearing idea: an objective states its SATISFACTION CONDITION in the framework's own vocabulary, e.g. "hold a Grade B or better procurement progression instance for every contract over $250k on fund 3100 since FY2019". Three things follow and each is worth the work on its own. Progress becomes derived: "41 of 58 at Grade B, 12 Grade C for want of a shared identifier, 5 with no solicitation and no exception document" is computed, so nobody has to be trusted about how it is going. The gaps become the WORK LIST, with the 12 naming exactly what measurement would raise them and the 5 being records requests with the request already specified. And an AI assistant working the objective becomes auditable in the same terms as anyone else, since everything it produces carries provenance and grade. | M4 · open |
| D-77 | doctrine | 2026-07-30 | **Goal-directed work is where a case turns into a brief, and nothing guards against it.** Now invariant 7 of the framework: a goal may direct what is SOUGHT but must never filter what is recorded, retained or shown about what was found, and a finding that cuts against a goal must be surfaced at least as prominently as one that supports it. This is the invariant most likely to be violated BY ACCIDENT, because helpfulness looks exactly like it from the inside: an assistant pursuing an objective, ranking findings by relevance to that objective, is one short step from an assistant that quietly does not mention the inconvenient one. `BIO_Declared_Bias_v0_1.md` is the doctrine this connects to and neither is implemented. The concrete mechanism needed is in Step 8b: every finding takes the same path to a member regardless of which way it cuts, and a deferral is recorded with its reason. | DOCTRINE (Bob) · open |
| D-79 | design | 2026-07-30 | **Unattended surfacing needs aggregation and ageing, neither of which exists.** An assistant that can open a focus without a member can produce hundreds where a person produces one, and framework §12 states the two disciplines that requires. AGGREGATE, DO NOT MULTIPLY: one junction check firing across 58 contracts is one focus with 58 instances, not 58 focuses, because "why do these 58 awards have no solicitation" is one question; getting this wrong does not corrupt the record, it drowns it, which for an advisory object is the same failure. AGE RATHER THAN VANISH: a machine-surfaced focus nobody has acted on after some interval moves to `deferred` with the reason recorded, never silently disappearing, because a finding that disappears is indistinguishable from one that was never made and that rule does not relax because the finder was a machine. Neither the aggregation key nor the ageing job exists. | M4 · open |
| D-80 | design | 2026-07-30 | **Contact between aspirations is not detected, and contradiction is a thing to FIND rather than prevent.** RULED by Bob: contradicting aspirations are welcomed, because we may not realise that they contradict and because we learn from trying to achieve aspirations whether they are achieved or not. That removes the arbiter from the design deliberately rather than leaving it unfinished: no precedence between group, project and member scopes, and no forced resolution, since forcing one would suppress exactly the discovery the ruling is pointing at. What remains to build is detection of CONTACT, not of contradiction, because judging whether two prose commitments contradict is not something this framework can establish and inventing that verdict would violate invariant 5. Contact IS establishable: two aspirations directing work at the same entities, touching the same progressions, or ordering the same queue differently. What counts as contact is named in framework §12.1 and not specified, and the surface that shows it does not exist. | M4 · open |
| D-81 | design | 2026-07-30 | **An aspiration accumulates learning and has nowhere to put it.** Follows from Bob's ruling that we learn from pursuing aspirations whether or not they are achieved. An object that never closes has no natural moment at which anyone writes down what it taught, so framework §12.2 adds a pursuit record: the objectives opened under it, the findings adopted and deferred, the records requests and their answers, the measurements, and the dead ends. Three properties are the whole value and each is easy to lose. A DEAD END IS KEPT, since "we assumed the fund code would appear in the procurement portal and it does not" is the kind of thing every member relearns individually and nobody writes down, and it is exactly what makes the next member's work cheaper. It is NOT A PROGRESS BAR, since an aspiration has no completion; objectives beneath it carry satisfaction conditions and derived progress, the aspiration itself does not. And it SURVIVES ABANDONMENT: retiring an aspiration after two years should be an act that captures what it taught rather than one that archives a folder. | M4 · open |

| D-84 | defect | 2026-07-30 | **`object_type: bias` does not exist in the check catalogue, so a bias bundle cannot be written.** The catalogue carries information, focus, project, action and problem. `BIO_Declared_Bias_v0_1.md` specifies bias sets as bundles of `object_type: bias` precisely so they inherit append-only history, member-authored transitions, convergent promotion and conformance checks without inventing governance. The evidence is in `research/SEARCH-COMPLETENESS.md`: the extractable rules are the countable ones, and in four of five documented verification failures **the countable rules were formally satisfied while the uncountable properties failed** — so enforcing the extractable half silently would deliver enforcement of precisely the part that does not protect, wearing the authority of the whole policy. (c) **INHALE MEANS PROPOSE FOR ADOPTION, NEVER INSTALL** — adoption is an authored, attributed act (DEC-46, D-90, D-82), because otherwise adopting a policy LAUNDERS a standard: "we follow BBC standards" with nobody in the group having authored anything, which is the never-prefill violation wearing a compliance badge. (d) **The adopted policy is PINNED — source, retrieval date, content hash — and a published case names the VERSION it was held to.** An external policy moves; a case published under it must stay checkable after it moves. That is DEC-12's edition pattern and D-183's calibration pattern at a third altitude, and it is what makes REGRADE meaningful across organisations: two lenses, both pinned, both re-runnable. | M4 · open |

| D-86 | design | 2026-07-30 | **Bias debt and temporal expectations are the same shape and should share one mechanism.** Bias debt: the lens changed, so this analysis owes a re-run, and it may not advance its workproduct_state or be ratified until the debt is settled; tracking continuous, settlement batchable.
