| D-87 | design | 2026-07-30 | **A pattern statement's evidence is cited once and never revisited, so a bias can outlive its justification unremarked.** Bob's reframing: a side-effect of this framework's work should be that evidentiary comments accrue to bias records, measuring the extent to which the bias is JUSTIFIED. The doctrine requires a `pattern` statement to cite evidence and refuses to let one leave draft without a citation; what it lacks is TIME. A statement declared in March citing three instances is a different object by August, either because those three became forty or because they stayed three while sixty went the other way, and nothing notices either. This is the INVERSE of bias debt: bias debt says the lens changed so the analysis owes a re-run, this says the evidence changed so the lens owes a re-examination. The second is the one nobody builds because it is uncomfortable, and a group may keep a statement whose grounds have evaporated provided it says why, but the decay must not go unremarked, since a bias whose grounds have quietly evaporated is undeclared in the way that matters. | M4 · open |
| D-88 | design | 2026-07-30 | **A bias statement should be able to carry a MEASURABLE FORM, and it is the same construct as an objective's satisfaction condition.** Framework §13.1. Most pattern statements are not mechanically measurable and must stay analysis; some are, and those should say so, with the measure expressed in the framework's own vocabulary. Three properties keep it honest and each is easy to lose. The measure NEVER EDITS the statement: text and justification stay a member's authored act and the standing measure is a derived dated attachment, because a machine that could rewrite doctrine would be worse than the problem it solves. The scope is REGISTRY-DEFINED and never hand-picked, since a cherry-picked denominator would let a group manufacture authority for a bias, which is the distortion the malformedness rule fights arriving by the back door. And it is reported WHICHEVER WAY IT CUTS, which is the case where invariant 7 actually costs something, because the value of the feature is entirely in the measures that refute. Build it once with §12's satisfaction-condition evaluator. | M4 · open |
| D-89 | doctrine | 2026-07-30 | **Bias must never shape what is captured or monitored, only how conclusions are weighed.** Framework §13.1 settles a question v0.8 asked badly. A pattern statement's evidence comes from monitoring; if monitoring configuration were itself shaped by a lens, a measure computed over that collection would prove only that the bias had been thorough. The fix is to forbid the coupling rather than document it: a scrutiny statement must not cause the system to collect less from a source, or more. This is invariant 7 applied to the bias doctrine itself, and it is what keeps the whole of §13.1 from being circular. Nothing enforces it today because neither bias binding nor per-source monitoring configuration exists, which makes now the cheap moment to write the constraint down rather than discover it later. | DOCTRINE (Bob) · open |
| D-90 | doctrine | 2026-07-30 | **Measure decay is loud and never blocking, which is the general rule that derived things inform and authored acts bind.** RULED by Bob, by pointing at the principle already written: the measure never edits the statement, so a measure that may not edit a statement certainly may not block work resting on one. Now invariant 8 of the framework, and it runs through more than bias: an assistant's proposal, a connection grade, a recogniser's confidence all report and none decides. The asymmetry with bias debt is exact rather than arbitrary. Bias debt blocks because the GROUP changed the lens by an authored act and the block is what makes that amendment mean something; a decayed measure means the WORLD changed and nobody decided anything, and an institution improving its behaviour must not be able to freeze a group's in-flight work. The path from decay to a block runs through a person: the measure reports, a member amends or retires the statement, that amendment is authored, and it generates bias debt in the ordinary way. A group that reads a collapsed measure and keeps the statement anyway has done something legitimate and recorded. | DOCTRINE (Bob) · open |
| D-91 | gap | 2026-07-30 | **A PDF is captured as bytes only: no content, no links, no structure, so the document class this project exists for cannot participate in the citation graph.** RULED by Bob: content should be identified and processed in other document types exactly as it is in HTML. Today `op=acquire` hits the `NOT_HTML` guard at `index.mjs` and a PDF yields a hash and nothing else. Agenda packets, staff reports, budget exhibits and ACFRs are PDFs, and an agenda PDF linking to a dozen item PDFs is precisely the graph CAPTURE exists to build. Mapping onto machinery that already exists: `/Annots` with `/Subtype /Link` and a `/URI` action are the `<a href>` equivalent; named destinations and `/Dest` are the `anchor` partition; embedded files are `intra`; page number plus annotation rectangle is the element reference. Link annotations are cheap and need no font handling; Workers has `DecompressionStream("deflate")` natively so FlateDecode costs no bundled code. Text extraction is the expensive half (glyph-to-Unicode through embedded CMaps) and Bob is right that libraries solve it: `unpdf` is a serverless pdf.js build targeting Workers. The real constraint is bundle size against the 3MB Free worker limit and CPU against the measured ceiling, both MEASURABLE rather than arguable, and an earlier claim here that it was "expensive and failure-prone" was a guess and is withdrawn. DECIDED: CAPTURE extracts structure and hands FRAMEWORK a parsed object tree; `docprofile` decides what the content is, because content identification is FRAMEWORK's subject regardless of container. **PHASE 1 LANDED 2026-07-31** (area CONTENT-PDF, commit 0b1c6ce): `bio-plane/src/pdfstructure.mjs` extracts the outbound-link graph — `/Annots` link annotations classified into the SAME anchor/intra/deferred/refused partitions HTML uses (wrappers byte-identical to subresources.mjs, asserted; plus a first-class `undetermined`), resolving classic objects, FlateDecode, and object streams, and reading a page's `/Rect` + index as the element reference. 46-assertion suite with a run negative control; producer-side shape registered as I2 (provisional) in INTERFACES.md. NOT DONE: text extraction (glyph→Unicode via `unpdf`) is phase 2 and gated on measuring bundle size vs the 3MB limit and CPU vs the ceiling; and live-verify awaits `op=pdfstructure`, a recorded DELEGATION to CAPTURE. **OP WIRED 2026-07-31** (CAP-1, commit 2ab62f4): `op=pdfstructure` serves the structure over the `op=capture` byte path, read-only, same auth. **PHASE-2 MEASURED → GO 2026-07-31** (CPDF-1, MEASUREMENTS.md): `unpdf` bundles to ~2.9MB raw / 0.71MB gzip — FITS the 3MB Free limit with 2.29MB gzip headroom; node-proxy extraction ~0.7–0.9 ms/page. Text extraction (CPDF-2) now in progress. STILL OPEN: unpdf/pdf.js wants the whole document in memory — exactly what I1's range reads avoid for large PDFs — so the authoritative large-PDF Worker-CPU/memory ceiling needs a deployed probe; oversized PDFs are recorded text-undetermined. | M2 · open; phase-1 + op wiring landed, phase-2 GO, text extraction in progress |
| D-92 | defect | 2026-07-30 | **`op=file` with a member token returns intermittent 403 under sequential load.** Found reading the live instance: three bundles failed in one pass and succeeded in the next, with different bundles failing each time, so it is transient rather than a permission boundary. A 403 rather than a 429 makes it read as a refusal instead of a rate limit, which cost a full re-run and nearly produced a false finding that eleven bundles had no recorded source. Whatever the cause, the response should name it. | M7 · open |
| D-120 | design | 2026-07-31 | **Member-driven capture for egress diversity is named nowhere in the ledger.** Raised by DEC-1's enactment: with the allowlist arm closed (D-94), admission on the plane's own egress rests on the CivicOS UA not yet being recognised — not a durable position. The mitigation that SCALES with the project (unlike an allowlist request, one ask per instance forever) is capturing through MEMBERS' own networks: diverse egress that does not all present as one recognisable origin. Needs a design for how a member contributes a capture and how its provenance/grade is recorded (a member's first-party fetch is still evidence at capture time), and how it composes with the archive fallback (CAP-3) as the two scaling resilience mechanisms. | M1 · open (DEC-1) — member-driven egress diversity; placed under M1 in MILESTONES, sibling to CAP-3 as a scaling capture-resilience mechanism |
| D-121 | defect | 2026-07-31 | **A revision can carry a stale `surfaced_by` forward, so an agent-opened focus could later be relabelled `human`.** Raised by REC-3's D-78 fix: `surfaced_by` is now server-stamped at the CREATION act (op=promote, actor class → `agent`/`human`), which is right for the surfacing act. But a later REVISION carries the document's existing `surfaced_by` forward unchanged — the store byte-trusts bundle.md by contract (40+ suites depend on it) — so a reviser could in principle overwrite an agent focus's honest `agent` with `human`. Narrow: outside the surfacing act, requires a revision, and enforcing revision-immutability of the field is a separate store-side change that touches the byte-trust contract. Sibling to D-82 (the DISPLAY half of `surfaced_by`). Not queued; recorded so the next session on the surfacing chain sees it. | M7 · open — revision-integrity of surfaced_by; store-side, sibling to D-82 |
| D-99 | gap | 2026-07-30 | **BIO speaks none of the standard web-archive formats, and discards provenance it can never recover.** The archives use WARC (ISO 28500), CDX, SURT and Memento (RFC 7089); BIO uses home-grown equivalents for all four and has no Memento interface, so nothing can query BIO the way BIO wants to query an archive. PARTLY FIXED 2026-07-30: the capture now records every response header in order with duplicates preserved, the requested and resolved locators, the redirect flag and the status. Previously only `content-type` survived, so `Last-Modified` and `ETag` were discarded at the only moment they existed despite both being named in `LINK-FIDELITY.md` as recordable evidence. `peer_address` is recorded as null with a named reason, because the Workers runtime does not expose the peer address of an outbound fetch and an unobtainable value differs from a missing one. STILL OPEN: WARC import and export as INTERCHANGE (the internal store stays content-addressed), and a Memento interface. Note the record is now non-uniform, since captures taken before this carry less, and provenance documents are first-party material and are never reshaped. | M6 · open |
| D-100 | drift | 2026-07-30 | **The Apps Script data plane was deleted, and it was the only component that could reach `www.oaklandca.gov`.** Recorded as a lesson rather than a defect, because the cause is now known and is D-94 rather than the substrate. Six documents exist in the record ONLY because that plane ran on 2026-07-19; nothing else could have taken them, and the loss stayed invisible for five months until a capture was attempted. Two things to carry forward: a migration can remove an access path nobody has identified as load-bearing, and the natural experiment it left behind (Google egress succeeding where Cloudflare egress failed) was read for three sessions as evidence for a network theory that the 2026-07-30 measurement disproved. Two clients differing in TWO variables is not evidence about either one. | WATCH, no task · open as a lesson, no task |
| D-107 | gap | 2026-07-31 | **The installer has no scripted deploy, and that is part of why D-106 went unnoticed for thirteen releases.** `newgroup/DEPLOY.md` documents deployment as: open the dashboard, select all in the editor, delete, paste `dist/newgroup.bundled.mjs`, Deploy. A manual paste produces no record of what was deployed, no read-back verification, and no place for a version assertion to live, which is exactly the blind spot the plane does NOT have: `bio-plane/scripts/deploy.mjs` refuses to report success until it reads the script back from the account and hashes it against the signed asset. This session deployed the installer with `wrangler deploy` and verified by reading the script back through the API (version, absence of the old version, and `bindings: []`), which worked but is not written down anywhere. The installer deserves the plane's discipline: a script that deploys, reads back, and asserts both the embedded `RELEASE_VERSION` and the empty binding set, since the no-bindings property is a structural security guarantee that a hand-paste into the wrong Worker could silently break. Not urgent while installer releases are rare, but the cost of the manual path is now measured rather than assumed.  REINFORCED 2026-07-31 by a finding that makes this sharper than when it was written: a fresh checkout on Bob's machine was authenticated to a DIFFERENT Cloudflare account (`neologic`, `2f4cabcc...`) than the project's, because `wrangler` takes its identity from an OAuth session stored on the machine and a machine is often logged in for unrelated work. An installer deploy from that state would have SUCCEEDED and put the installer in the wrong account with no error to notice, which is precisely the class of silent wrong outcome the plane's own deploy path refuses to permit. PARTIAL FIX SHIPPED: both wrangler.jsonc files now pin `account_id`, so the repository rather than the machine decides where a Worker goes. `scripts/deploy.mjs` was never exposed, because it takes the account from CF_ACCT and talks to the REST API directly; the gap was always the installer's `wrangler deploy`. The rest of this item still stands, and the account discovery is an argument for it: the installer deserves the plane's discipline, meaning a script that deploys, reads the Worker back, and asserts the embedded RELEASE_VERSION, the empty binding set, AND the account it actually landed in. | M7 · open |
| D-111 | unverified | 2026-07-31 | **The Wayback rate ceilings are still third-party description and should stay that way until ordinary use tests them.** The 24-to-60 requests per minute figures, the 429 behaviour, and the claim that ignored 429s escalate to an hour-long firewall block are the remaining unverified half of D-105. They were NOT measured on 2026-07-31 and the decision not to measure them is the point of this entry rather than an omission from it: the only way to establish a wall is to hit it, and the documented consequence lands on Cloudflare's shared egress, which is to say on people who have never heard of this project and get no say. Our appetite stays the conservative 24/min because it is OURS to choose; their capacity gets discovered the way D-95 already discovers capacity, by a refusal arriving in the course of polite use and being recorded with its date and instrument. If a figure is ever needed sooner, the honest route is to ask the Internet Archive, not to probe them. Anyone tempted to close this by testing should read the outreach note at the foot of ARCHIVE-FALLBACK.md first. | NOT OURS TO MEASURE · open, and deliberately not measurable by us |
| D-113 | defect | 2026-07-31 | **`op=purge` reported scope ALL while leaving the tables added since it was written.** Found during close-out: a scratch purge cleared five bundles and answered `scope: "ALL"`, and an open task pointing at one of the purged bundles survived it. That task then referred to a bundle that no longer existed, which C-19.1 refuses as unresolvable, so the purge had produced exactly the inconsistent state the checker exists to catch. A purge that reports ALL and leaves rows behind is worse than one reporting a narrower scope, because the caller believes the store is empty. FIXED in 0.53.0: the whole-store arm now takes `tasks`, `task_queue` and `source_reachability`, `stats()` reports all three so the removal counts can be proved rather than asserted, and the per-bundle arm deliberately touches none of them for the same reason it leaves selections alone. Verified live: the second sweep removed 1 task and 3 reachability rows and left scratch genuinely empty. THE STANDING HAZARD, which is the part worth carrying forward: this list is maintained by hand and there is nothing that fails when a new derived table is added and not added here. D-98's tables were three releases old before anyone noticed. A hygiene-style check that every CREATE TABLE in schema.mjs is either named in purge or explicitly exempted would close the class rather than this instance. | M0 · open as a class; the instance is fixed |
| D-115 | gap | 2026-07-31 | **The installer installs ONE Worker, and the topology now has a fleet.** Bob's 2026-07-31 decision moves heavy, dependency-laden functions into dedicated single-purpose Workers the plane calls over service bindings, `pdf-worker` first. `newgroup` installs the plane and nothing else, so a group installing after the fleet lands gets an instance whose PDFs silently do less than every description of it — which is D-106's failure exactly, a quietly different install that nobody notices because nothing errors. The installer must install the fleet, bind the services, and verify each member's version on read-back, and the wizard must say what a member being absent costs. | M7 · open |
| D-116 | design | 2026-07-31 | **Version authority must span the fleet, or D-106's drift class returns multiplied.** A version lived in four places and had rotted in three; the fix made `bio-plane/package.json` the single declared authority with `resolveVersion` refusing on ANY disagreement in either direction. A fleet member is a fifth, sixth and seventh place for a version to live, each with its own deploy and its own rollout, and a plane calling a stale sibling over a service binding is a NEW face of D-108: the plane can verify itself current while the member still answers from the previous build, invisibly to both. Needs the authority rule extended to every member and a post-deploy probe that establishes which build ANSWERED on the far side of the binding, not just on this one. | M7 · open |
| D-121 | gap | 2026-07-31 | **Spreadsheets, word-processing and presentation documents are captured as bytes only, and the format dispatch cannot absorb them.** Bob's direction: BIO must inspect these as it inspects HTML and PDF, cleanly. Research in `OFFICE-FORMATS.md`. The shape of the finding: `.docx`/`.xlsx`/`.pptx` are ONE container (OOXML = ZIP of XML parts) with three part-maps, and `.odt`/`.ods`/`.odp` are the same shape — so this is not three parsers. MEASURED 2026-07-31 in workerd via miniflare: `DecompressionStream` supports `deflate-raw` and round-trips it (480 raw / 29 compressed / intact), so the container is readable in the plane with ZERO dependency, exactly as `FlateDecode` made PDF phase 1 dependency-free. Outbound links live uniformly in `_rels/*.rels` with `TargetMode="External"` and map onto I2's existing partitions unchanged; embedded files are `intra`. Text is EASIER than PDF, not harder — XML text nodes, no glyph/CMap problem, so there is no Tier 2 and no fleet member for office text. Two element references are BETTER than PDF's page+rect: `Sheet1!B14` and slide+shape are stable and human-meaningful, the first time the record can cite finer than a document without inventing an anchor scheme. THE ARCHITECTURAL POINT: today's dispatch is already TWO mechanisms that do not scale — a hardcoded `HTML_CT` array guarding the acquire-time subresource branch (everything else refused `NOT_HTML`) and a separate read-time `op=pdfstructure`. Three more formats makes five special cases across two mechanisms. The framework already specifies the fix and has never exercised it: §4's uniform recogniser plus a registry per axis, with FORMAT named outright as a candidate axis. So the work is stand up the FORMAT registry and MOVE HTML AND PDF ONTO IT, then add formats as registry entries. | M2 · open |
| D-124 | design | 2026-07-31 | (renumbered from a colliding D-122 by CONDUCT 2026-07-31) **Office formats carry latent evidence HTML and PDF do not, and the same artefacts carry personal data the publisher did not knowingly publish.** From `OFFICE-FORMATS.md`. The evidentiary half is substantial and is why this is more than parity: an XLSX stores the FORMULA (`<f>`) beside its cached value (`<v>`), and for accountability work the derivation is frequently the finding — a PDF or printed copy destroys it permanently; DOCX tracked changes and comments record who changed what and what a reviewer said, which a published PDF is designed to remove; PPTX speaker notes are routinely more candid than the slide; hidden rows, columns and sheets are first-class findings invisible in every rendered form; and `docProps/core.xml` carries creator, `lastModifiedBy`, revision count and instants. The risk is the same artefacts: `lastModifiedBy` NAMES A MEMBER OF STAFF, a tracked change attributes an edit to a person, a comment may be candid about a named individual — none knowingly published, all inside a document the body did knowingly publish. CAPTURING is not in question (the record holds what was served and stripping bytes would break the hash and the premise). SURFACING is a different act with effects on people outside the project, which is the D-77 / invariant-7 neighbourhood and Bob's to rule. RULED 2026-08-01 (DEC-5), and the ruling INVERTS the framing: these are public documents, there is no reason to redact anything from a public record, and who edited a document and when IS evidence — it demonstrates the actions of people and departments. So this is not a disclosure risk to be managed but an EVIDENCE SOURCE to be extracted: creator, lastModifiedBy, revision count and instants, tracked changes with author/date/superseded wording, comments with author and date, speaker notes, hidden sheets — structured, projected, indexed and searchable like any other content. The recommendation this row originally carried ("present and not promoted", kept off indexes and facets) was self-defeating: an analyst can only decide what matters if they can find it, and the patterns that make this material valuable are only visible ACROSS documents. Scope is public records; restricted material is D-124. | M2 · open, ruled |
<!-- D-124's SECOND row is kept LIVE beside its twin deliberately: D-124 is a REGISTERED PRE-EXISTING ID COLLISION (tools/mintid.mjs KNOWN_COLLISIONS). Rolling the closed half to the archive on 2026-08-10 split the pair across two files and mintid read the collision as RESOLVED — a defect made invisible by relocating half of it, which is the laundering shape this record refuses. Both rows stay here until the renumber happens. -->
| D-124 | design | 2026-08-01 | **DEC-5 settles public records and deliberately does not settle restricted material.** Bob's ruling — surface everything, internal metadata included, because who edited a document and when is evidence — was given for PUBLIC DOCUMENTS, and he scoped it himself: "there may be other situations where they're not and there are restrictions on what should be on the record or redacted." Three classes are visibly outside it and none is answered: a public-records-request response carrying STATUTORY redactions (where the redaction is itself a fact about the body, and re-deriving redacted content from document metadata would defeat a legal protection the record has no standing to override); MEMBER-ORIGIN or confidential-source material, which C-18.1's custody block already treats differently from a public fetch; and material a body published in error and withdrew, where holding it is settled but surfacing its authorship is not. Recorded now, with a TRIGGER rather than a date, so nobody over-applies DEC-5 to a class it never covered and nobody re-raises it before there is anything to reason about. | DOCTRINE (Bob) · deferred — trigger: the first capture that is NOT a straightforward public publication (a records-request response, member-origin material, or a withdrawn document) |
| D-123 | gap | 2026-07-31 | **I2's element reference is PDF-shaped and office formats need a per-container form, which is `INTERFACE-CHANGES.md`'s first real use.** I2 records `source: {page, rect} | null`. A spreadsheet's reference is `{sheet, cell}` and a deck's is `{slide, shape}`. That is a change to a shape FRAMEWORK builds against, so it goes through the change protocol — and `INTERFACE-CHANGES.md` DOES NOT EXIST, deliberately, because `PARALLELISM.md` says writing it before it is needed gets it wrong. This is the moment it is needed, and a session should expect to write the protocol file as part of the work rather than discovering halfway that there is nowhere to file the proposal. CONCRETELY, today's shape is `source: { page: 3, rect: [x0,y0,x1,y1] } | null`; a sheet needs `{ sheet: "Sheet1", cell: "B14" }` and a slide `{ slide: 7, shape: 3 }`. **AND IT IS NOT SAFELY ADDITIVE, which the first reading of this got wrong:** a consumer written as `if (source) usePage(source.page)` does not break loudly on a sheet-cell source, it reads `undefined` and carries on — so the change REQUIRES consumers to discriminate on a kind tag, which is precisely the "shape another area builds against" case the protocol exists for. A genuine fork the proposal must settle: whether the canonical reference is a STRUCTURED union or a per-container STRING (`"Sheet1!B14"`, `"slide7#shape3"`, `"p7#rect(...)"`) with structured fields beside it. The string has a real argument — `Sheet1!B14` is already the form a person recognises, and the citation surface will want to show it — while the structure is easier to validate. ONE WRINKLE WORTH KNOWING BEFORE PROPOSING: FRAMEWORK owns I2, is dormant, and has never consumed it, so nothing breaks TODAY; the whole reason to use the protocol is that registering an interface means not changing it casually later. CONDUCT answers on FRAMEWORK's behalf IN WRITING (protocol step 3), and that answer must be recorded as CONDUCT answering for a dormant area rather than as FRAMEWORK agreeing — the distinction matters when FRAMEWORK wakes and disagrees. AND A REAL RISK OF DECIDING EARLY: M4 brings readings and the entity axis (D-71, D-83), and an element reference may want to BE the same construct as an entity locator rather than a sibling of it. RESOLVED 2026-08-01 as a PROPOSAL, not a unilateral edit, after Bob delegated it as plumbing. THE FACTS CHANGED WHILE IT WAS BEING REASONED, which is why the answer is not the one drafted an hour earlier: I2 went from PROVISIONAL-and-unconsumed to 1.0.0 STABLE, confirmed by its owner FRAMEWORK (FW-1, framework-agent-2, 2026-07-31). A draft decision that it was provisional and unconsumed, so producer-side revision rather than protocol, was obsolete before it was written -- and this row's original claim, that this is INTERFACE-CHANGES.md's first use, turns out RIGHT for a different reason than it gave. So the protocol applies, INTERFACE-CHANGES.md is written (it deliberately did not exist), and the shape is filed as IC-1 PROPOSED rather than edited into a stable interface by a non-owner: `source` becomes a tagged union with a REQUIRED `kind` discriminator and a REQUIRED human-readable `ref`, with `pdf-page` keeping `page`/`rect` byte-for-byte. Not safely additive, and that is exactly why it is a protocol case: `if (source) usePage(source.page)` reads undefined on a sheet-cell source and carries on. Pure-string was rejected (pushes per-container parsing onto every consumer, where silent misreads live) and pure-structure was rejected (forces every display surface to rebuild the human form). D-121 steps 1-2 proceed meanwhile; neither emits a `source`. | M2 - PROPOSED as IC-1, awaiting FRAMEWORK | M2 · open |
| D-125 | gap | 2026-08-01 | **A member has no notification preferences, and DEC-10's ruling requires them.** RULED 2026-08-01: an overdue progression stage notifies the member who AUTHORED the connection between that instance and a Focus or Project, and the notification must offer three things -- remind me again at a further increment overdue, stop notifying me about this one, stop notifying me about that GROUP. The plane has no state for any of it: `tasks` carries `open`/`forwarded`/`resolved` and nothing per-member and nothing with a snooze clock. Needed: a per-member preference keyed on the thing muted, where the mute key is expressible on the axes the CONNECTION was made on (progression type, body, or Focus/Project) so a member mutes what they mean rather than a bucket the system chose; a snooze whose increment is THE STAGE'S OWN DECLARED INTERVAL rather than a new constant, since the expectation already carries the number and a second one would drift from it. **THE HAZARD, and it is the one to build against first: MUTING IS PERSONAL, DISMISSING IS A RECORD ACT, and they must never be one control.** If 'stop notifying me' also dismisses the finding, one member's inbox hygiene erases a finding for the whole group -- which is the silent disappearance the discovery-loop doctrine forbids (D-79, Step 8b). The finding stands; only that member's notification stops. NOTE the channel itself already exists: D-98's task inbox is the in-app notification channel, so D-52 ('there is no notification channel in this system at all', raised for export notification) is substantially answered by the same mechanism -- one channel, three consumers, which is the D-86 lesson applied rather than a second scheduler's worth of bugs. AMENDED 2026-08-01 (DEC-10 amendment): notifications AGGREGATE PER CASE, so the preference state is needed at TWO levels, not one -- per item and per (member, Focus/Project) group. The group entry is STANDING and ACCUMULATING rather than a stream, which removes any digest-cadence machinery: one live entry per (member, case) while it holds unhandled events, re-notifying only on a snooze increment or when something new lands after the member last looked. AND THE GROUP MUTE CARRIES ITS OWN HAZARD: it must be scoped to the KINDS PRESENT WHEN IT WAS MADE, so a genuinely new kind of event on that case surfaces again -- otherwise 'mute this case' is a permanent blindfold, which is the same silent-disappearance failure as conflating mute with dismiss, one level up. Grouping itself needs no new stored relation: `tasks.refers_to` names a bundle and the case comes from the citation edge that already exists, so the group is DERIVED from the authored connection rather than duplicated beside it. | M4 - open; the inbox half M8 |
| D-126 | design | 2026-08-01 | **About thirty notification generators exist or are designed, with no catalogue, no classes and no item contract.** Inventoried 2026-08-01 in `NOTIFICATIONS.md` at Bob's direction: clock-driven (overdue successor, temporal expectation, bias debt, recheck sweeps, fallback eligibility, capture-session TTL), data-flow driven (undetermined authority, source modified/removed, governed-vs-unreachable, duplicates, link verdicts, post-hoc reuse divergence, completed walked-away captures, partial captures, text-undetermined, client-rendered shells), governance (endorsements, expertise confirmations, the doorbell, export notification, owner rescue, spent invitations) and analysis (assistant-surfaced focuses, missing predecessors, improvable connection grades, satisfaction gaps, measure decay). THE SIZE IS THE ARGUMENT: thirty ad-hoc sites means thirty invented wordings, severities and button sets, and the drift is guaranteed rather than likely. This is the lesson `CONSTRUCTS.md` item 5 already recorded — ad hoc event strings with no registry, 'the same lesson the check catalogue already taught the plane' — so kinds take stable `N-` ids beside the `C-` ids, and this closes that half of D-68. CLASSES ARE THREE, NOT FOUR SEVERITIES: FINDING (true about the world/record, may become evidence), OBLIGATION (a named person must act for the record to proceed), CONDITION (a fact about our own machinery). A severity ladder was rejected because it encodes how LOUD rather than what it MEANS and rots in one direction — everything becomes a warning; warning/error are severity, which is orthogonal and mostly derivable. FINDING and CONDITION must not merge, or our plumbing dilutes the record's findings and the standing rule that a technical complication is never surfaced as a choice is broken. NEEDED: the catalogue with ids, the item contract (summary, detail, BASIS, producer-published options — never a surface-side map, which is the `op=searchfields` drift), and the `per-item` application weight beside `refuse`/`report`, where a retained item carries its reason. | M4 - open; the surface half M8 |
| D-127 | design | 2026-08-01 | **CASE-MAKING is undesigned, and it is what the whole system is for.** Bob, 2026-08-01: "the CivicOS system is about developing grounded, justified, and meaningful analysis and conclusions. These are built upon evidence and findings from that evidence. We haven't dove into that process of case making yet. But it will certainly involve organizing and building upon this substrate and their derivatives." Everything built to date is SUBSTRATE for this: capture produces bytes and provenance, content produces structure, the framework produces meaning, retrieval finds things, and findings accumulate — and nothing composes them into a justified conclusion a reader can check. The pieces that exist and are unconnected to it: `project` carries an `objective` and a state machine; `focus` holds an open question; citations link Information into a Project with `cites`/`corroborates`; the published surface (UI-PLAN U12) describes a case file as a NARRATIVE with print as first-class, and names the sewer case as the proof piece — but that is a RENDERING of a case, not the process of making one. Missing entirely: how a finding is adopted into an argument, how competing or contradicting findings are held together (D-80 rules that contradiction is a thing to FIND rather than prevent), how a conclusion states what it rests on and what would falsify it, how the grade of a case is derived from the weakest link in its chain (D-72's connection grades are the input and nothing consumes them), and how invariant 7 is enforced in practice — a finding that cuts against the case must take the same path as one that supports it. This is not a milestone gap to be filled quickly; it is the layer the roadmap calls ANALYSIS and it deserves its own design pass with Bob, not a session's guess. **RESTATED BY BOB 2026-08-01 AS THE SYSTEM'S RAISON D'ETRE, which makes it the most important open item in the repository:** CivicOS is about telling a story, or equivalently making a case — capturing the data, rigorously extracting meaning, understanding the legal/regulatory/policy framework, making connections, and figuring out how {information, money, responsibility, relationships, decisions, power} is SUPPOSED to flow and how it ACTUALLY does, where it works and where it does not; and a meta-level above all of that, answering questions, making a case, telling a story and TAKING ACTION to affect the living civic system. "There's nothing about CivicOS and how it works that's more important than supporting users traverse that winding path of questioning, exploring, discovering, documenting, and impacting the civic system." **A STRUCTURAL CONSEQUENCE IT EXPOSES IMMEDIATELY: the milestone ladder has no rung for the thing the system is for.** M0-M8 are substrate and surfaces — running unattended, every document class as evidence, knowing and connecting what it holds, search, leaving, installing, reaching. Not one is "a member can make a case." Read against this statement the ladder is upside down: case-making is not M9 appended after the substrate, it is what the other eight SERVE, and the ladder should say so. Fixing that belongs INSIDE the design pass rather than before it, because the rung's shape is what the pass produces. **And note the fifth verb, which no milestone and no construct touches at all: IMPACTING.** Questioning, exploring, discovering and documenting all have substrate; taking action to affect the civic system has none. **THE PASS IS OPEN as `architecture/BIO_Case_Making_v0_1.md`** (2026-08-01), recording Bob's framing as he gives it plus the observations it raises; nothing in it is settled and nothing builds from it yet. FOUR THINGS ALREADY SURFACED THERE. (a) The constructs CONFLATE WORK WITH OUTPUT while the substrate already draws that line: focus / project / action describe work, a case file is an output, and the two-bucket fence plus ratification is exactly that distinction load-bearing in the plane and unexpressed in the member vocabulary. (b) **A PREVIOUS CLAIM HERE THAT IMPACTING HAS NO SUBSTRATE WAS WRONG and is corrected: `action` IS it** — states planned/active/awaiting_response/resolved/abandoned, headings including `## Correspondence`, fields `action_kind`, `risk_tier`, `counterparty`. Unmistakably outward-facing and in the catalogue throughout. What is missing is not the object but its CONNECTIONS: nothing links an action to the findings that justified it, the case it advances, or the outcome it produced, so the record can hold 'we wrote to the city' and cannot say 'because of these findings, and here is what came back'. The loop from evidence to action to CONSEQUENCE is unbuilt, and the consequence half is itself new evidence about how the system responds — D-128's declared-versus-actual flow measured on our own intervention. (c) Audience difference is probably RENDERING rather than four case types, and the sharp form of it is CLAIM STRENGTH: a journalist can publish 'records suggest', a lawyer needs 'the record establishes', so a case carries claims with derived strength and a rendering selects what clears the audience's threshold and says what it excluded. That gives D-72's unconsumed connection grades a consumer and gives invariant 7 a MECHANISM rather than an exhortation. (d) **~~The government-administrator archetype INVERTS the threat model~~ — STRUCK 2026-08-01, and the row's own correction is recorded here because this is the row a case-making session reads first.** Bob ruled the same day that BIO's doctrine contains nothing resembling "stick it to the man", the objective is BETTER GOVERNMENT, and all stakeholders are assumed to want better outcomes in the public interest — bad actors being real and identified BY EVIDENCE rather than assumed by role. So an administrator is a stakeholder like any other and the question was malformed. THE THREAT MODEL IS SYMMETRIC AND ITS MORE DANGEROUS HALF IS OURS: someone may damage a publisher, and someone may claim far beyond what the evidence supports — and almost every hard-won rule in this repository defends against the second. What remains open is narrower: in an administrator's instance, what does the two-bucket fence protect and from whom? Full reasoning in `BIO_Case_Making_v0_1.md` section 4. The stale claim was — every doctrinal assumption here is outsider-looking-in, and for an administrator the subject and the user are the same institution, so the adversarial fence protects nothing while the impact available is larger. Raised, not answered; it is doctrine. | DOCTRINE (Bob) · open AS DOCTRINE, **and the DESIGN IT ASKED FOR HAS LANDED — recorded 2026-09-17 by the corpus single-authority sweep, because this row's headline was being read as a live statement of fact and had propagated into three other governed documents.** The design is `docs/architecture/BIO_Case_Making_v0_1.md` (the reasoning record and the home of constructs 8's rulings), the build closed at **43/43** through the IS plan, and `BIO_System_Design.md` construct 8 reads **built** with *since designed and built through the IS plan*. **SO THE SENTENCE *CASE-MAKING IS UNDESIGNED* IS BOB'S FRAMING OF 2026-08-01 AND IS NO LONGER TRUE OF THE ESTATE.** It is kept because the doctrine it carries — that case-making is what the whole system is FOR, and sits at the TOP of the ladder — is permanent and is why this row stays open. **THE DEFECT THE SWEEP FOUND WAS NOT THIS ROW, IT WAS THE COPYING:** `BIO_Interaction_Constructs_v0_1.md`, `NOTIFICATIONS.md` and `BIO_System_Design.md` each quoted *undesigned* WITHOUT the status that sits beside it in the map, which is a contradiction IN THE RECORD in the exact sense ruled the same day — one part asserting what another denies, carrying a duty to resolve rather than a finding to keep. All three now name it as a 2026-08-01 quotation. **AND IT IS THE SELF-PRESERVING SHAPE: an *undesigned* invites readers to stay away, so the reading that would falsify it is the one nobody does.** |
| D-128 | design | 2026-08-01 | **The analytic product of progressions is the DELTA between declared and observed flow, and nothing names it as such.** Bob, 2026-08-01: "Obligations are the flows (edges) that go on in a living civic system. Understanding those flows, evolving that understanding, and differentiating between how things are supposed to flow and how they really flow (and the implications of those differences)." This corrects how the OBLIGATION class was first written in NOTIFICATIONS.md — as "a named person must act for the record to proceed", which is a MEMBER TASK and conflated two objects. The CIVIC obligation (the body's declared duty: minutes within N days, an award follows a solicitation) is the primary thing and is a model of how the institution is SUPPOSED to work; the member task is downstream plumbing that frequently arises BECAUSE a civic obligation was observed unmet. Naming them one thing models our inbox and calls it the institution. WHAT FOLLOWS: a progression definition is the DECLARED flow, its instances are the OBSERVED flow, and the divergence has three shapes — missing predecessor (D-73's sharper case), overdue successor (DEC-10), and out-of-order stage (named here for the first time; nothing models it). The delta is not a by-product of progressions, it is the reason to have them, and it needs somewhere to live: a flow model per institution that EVOLVES as understanding does, since a group's picture of how a body is supposed to work is itself a claim that gets refined and should carry its own basis. D-74's shared-identifier measurement is what makes cross-system flows observable at better than Grade C. | M4 · open |
| D-129 | design | 2026-08-01 | **`undetermined` conflates two different claims: WE DO NOT KNOW, and THERE IS POSITIVELY NONE.** Found by the practice survey (`PRACTICE-SURVEY.md`): Wikidata distinguishes `somevalue` (unknown) from `novalue` (positively no value) as separate ASSERTED statements, and BIO blurs them under one word. The conflation is real in our own code, not theoretical — a scanned PDF with no text layer is `novalue` (there is no text to extract, permanently, a property of the document) while a PDF over the Worker envelope is `somevalue` (there IS text and we did not get it, a limitation of our run that a later run could lift). Both currently surface as text-undetermined with the difference only in a reason string. The same split is latent elsewhere: an authority nobody can establish versus a document that genuinely has no issuing authority; a link whose verdict cannot be established versus one established to have no relationship. WHY IT MATTERS BEYOND TIDINESS: the standing rule is that `undetermined` is first-class and must be STATED, and its value to a reader is that it says what we do not know — but 'we could not read this' and 'there is nothing here to read' are different claims about the world, and a member deciding whether to re-capture, escalate or move on needs to tell them apart. One is worth retrying; the other never will be. Cheapest correct shape is probably a second field beside the reason rather than a new vocabulary, but that is a design call for whoever takes it. | M2 - open **WIDENED 2026-08-04 by the archival-systems survey (`STORE-AS-CACHE.md`): the useful state set is LARGER than two, and `partial` is the state BIO most obviously lacks.** Software Heritage stores crawl outcomes explicitly as `OriginVisitStatus.status` in {`created`, `not_found`, `ongoing`, `failed`, `partial`, `full`} — so *we went to this origin at time T and it was not there* is stored DATA, never an inference from missing rows (docs.softwareheritage.org/devel/apidoc/swh.storage.interface.html). Memento (RFC 7089) goes further and makes an archived 404 a CITABLE Memento, MANDATING that a replayed 4XX/5XX carry the same status code, with CDX indexing it in `statuscode` beside the 200s. **`partial` is not hypothetical for us: it is CPDF-5's measured Tier-1-at-88% case and every over-envelope document**, both of which today collapse into the same `undetermined` as a document we never read at all. The governing finding of that survey bears directly on this row — *systems built as ARCHIVES record absence; systems built as CACHES treat absence as a transient failure to be retried* — so this row is load-bearing for the read-through-cache framing rather than tidiness. **SHARPENED AGAIN 2026-08-04 by the caching-systems survey: the principled set is FOUR states with GRADED RETENTION, and there is an AUTHORITY rule BIO already believes in another vocabulary.** RFC 2308 (*Negative Caching of DNS Queries*) separates: the name does not exist (NXDOMAIN, SOA-derived retention, *"one to three hours… work well"*, over a day *"problematic"*); exists-but-not-this-record (NODATA); **we asked and could not tell (SERVFAIL — MUST NOT be cached longer than five minutes)**; and the server was unreachable (same five-minute bound). So: `NEVER_LOOKED` / `LOOKED_ABSENT` / `LOOKED_INDETERMINATE` / `PRESENT`, plus SWH's `partial`. **An indeterminate result must never inherit a definitive lifetime** — CloudFront's error floor is 10 s, Google Cloud CDN publishes 120 s for 404/410/451 and 60 s for 405/501. HTTP encodes the same confidence split more weakly and has NO wire representation for *we have not looked*, which is exactly this row's conflation. **AND RFC 2308 §5 IS THIS PROJECT'S OWN DOCTRINE, ARRIVED AT INDEPENDENTLY IN 1998: *"Negative responses without SOA records SHOULD NOT be cached"*** — the SOA is the proof of AUTHORITY that makes a negative answer a FINDING rather than an absence of finding, so an absence with no authority attached is not recordable at all. That is *an equality that costs nothing to produce is not evidence*, applied to absence. |

The member is shown a JavaScript error instead of the choice U8 was built to give them (record it as the unfinished capture it is, or write nothing). | D-135 | defect | 2026-08-01 | **The D-15 viewer gate is stamped onto compiled query paths only, so `op=list` shows an uninvited member every project's id, title and state.** VERIFIED at `bio-plane/src/index.mjs:2823-2826`: the `viewer` parameter is set for `search`, `select`, `selection`, `EDGE_ACTIONS` and `STATE_ACTIONS` and for nothing else. `viewerPredicate` exists only in `query.mjs` (121, 607) and is referenced from neither `store.mjs` nor `index.mjs`, so ops that read the `bundles` table DIRECTLY — `list`, `index`, `projection`, `image` — never meet it. `civicos-ui/app.html:952` builds the entire record view from `op=list`. That claim holds for the compiled paths and does not extend to direct reads. **THE SAFETY COMMENT IS THE SHARP PART.** `index.mjs:2820-2822` states that a viewer the compiler does not recognise compiles to a DENY predicate, 'so the failure mode of a missing stamp is an empty result rather than an unfiltered one'. That fail-closed property is real for ops that reach the compiler and is FALSE for ops that do not: for `list` the failure mode of a missing stamp is unfiltered, which is the opposite of what the comment promises the next reader. SCOPE, stated so this is not over-read: every affected op is member class or above, so this is not a public exposure and not a repeat of D-30 across the two-bucket fence; it is an INTRA-INSTANCE exposure of project existence to a member the project never invited. D-15 also deliberately leaves MACHINE credentials unfiltered, which is sound and is not this. | M7 - open |
| D-136 | defect | 2026-08-01 | **The three ops that implement the section 4.7 ballot and section 4.9 capability edit are unreachable from any administrator's browser.** VERIFIED against `SESSION_OPS` in `bio-plane/src/index.mjs`: `memberadd` and `memberset` are present; `membercaps`, `adminendorse` and `adminremove` are ABSENT. | D-138 | defect | 2026-08-01 | **The UI's state-machine drift guard does not read the authority it claims to check against.** VERIFIED: `civicos-ui/check-semantics.mjs` reads `app.html` and never reads `bio-plane/checks/bio-checks.mjs` (0 occurrences). | D-139 | gap | 2026-08-01 | **Nothing publishes what may be DONE to an object, so every act surface must keep a forbidden surface-side map.** VERIFIED: no `affordances` op and no `options` publication exist in `bio-plane/src/index.mjs`; `op=whoami` publishes capabilities and `op=searchfields` publishes the query vocabulary, and there it stops. | D-141 | defect | 2026-08-01 | **The UI rebuilds the project-visibility leak client-side, enumerating every project's projection.** VERIFIED at `civicos-ui/app.html:749-768`: `reverseRefs` calls `loadRecord()` (`op=list`), filters to every `focus`/`problem`/`project`, then calls `getProjection()` on EACH and parses `fm_json` to index their `references`.
