| D-142 | defect | 2026-08-01 | **Search silently degrades to a title-substring scan over an unfiltered list, and looks identical when it does.** VERIFIED at `civicos-ui/app.html:4013-4018`: if `op=search` returns no recognisable row shape, the surface falls back to `loadRecord()` — `op=list` — and filters client-side on `title`/`bundle_id` substring, with the comment 'so search always answers'. Three things are wrong and the third is the worst. (1) It bypasses the D-15 viewer gate, since `op=list` carries no viewer stamp (D-135). (2) It answers a WEAKER QUESTION: no full text, no frontmatter, no facets, no relevance — a member searching for a phrase inside a document gets nothing and is shown a confident empty state. (3) **A member cannot tell which engine answered.** The result list, the count line and the empty state are byte-identical either way, so a silent degradation presents as a finished search — which is the 'an outcome that costs nothing to produce is not evidence' family arriving in the UI: a result set that looks like a search result and is not one. A surface that cannot answer must SAY it cannot answer; `undetermined` is first-class here too. | M8 - open |
| D-143 | gap | 2026-08-01 | **A member of the public can verify a hash and list ids, and cannot READ a published case — the surface UI-PLAN calls 'the reason the rest exists' has no data path.** VERIFIED. The unauthenticated (`classes: null`) ops are exactly: `publishedmanifest`, `bootstrap`, `claim`, `login`, `enroll`, `invitelook`, `verify`, `knock`. **Not one of them returns a published bundle's bytes or prose.** And `publishedList` in `store.mjs` selects `bundle_id, bundle_sha, ratified_at, attestor_member, gate_version` — **no title** — which is why the published view renders a bundle id as its heading. So the public surface can today answer 'does this hash match' and 'what ids exist' and cannot answer 'what does it say'. U12 describes the case file as a narrative a member of the public reads start to finish, with print first-class and the sewer case as the proof piece, and names it as the reason the rest of the system exists; it is the one rung with no reachable data beneath it. NEEDED: a `classes: null` op serving a published bundle's prose and its registered bytes from the PUBLISHED bucket only (never the working corpus — the two-bucket fence is what makes an unauthenticated read safe), and a title on the published projection. Note the fence makes this SAFER to build than it looks: that bucket has never held unratified material, which is the same reasoning that already justifies `verify` being unauthenticated. | M6 - open |
| D-144 | defect | 2026-08-01 | **Re-ratifying a bundle DESTROYS the previous attestation — the record can still serve an old published hash and can no longer say who attested it.** VERIFIED at `bio-plane/src/store.mjs:5934-5940`: `published_bundles` is keyed on `bundle_id` alone and the write is `ON CONFLICT(bundle_id) DO UPDATE SET bundle_sha, ratified_at, attestor_key, attestor_member, gate_version, sig_armored` — every field replaced. **ONE CORRECTION TO HOW THIS WAS FIRST REPORTED, and it changes the fix rather than the severity.** The system's headline promise HOLDS: `published_shas` is keyed `(sha256, bundle_id, path)` and is genuinely append-only, so a hash once published stays answerable forever and `op=verify` on an old hash still works — the comment at 5941 saying exactly that is true. What is destroyed is the ATTESTATION PROVENANCE of the earlier publication: who signed it, when, with which key, under which catalogue version. So the record can answer 'yes, these bytes were published' and cannot answer 'by whom, and under what rules' — in a system whose entire product is that claims carry their author, and about the one act it calls irreversible. THE FIX IS NOT IDEMPOTENCE, which is what the first reading would have suggested: re-ratification is legitimate (a bundle revised and re-published SHOULD carry a new attestation). The fix is to make `published_bundles` accumulate like `published_shas` already does — key it `(bundle_id, ratified_at)` or chain each attestation to the one it supersedes — so the history of who stood behind which version is kept rather than replaced. Note a storyboard (`SB-OUTPUT` S16) designs a screen promising exactly that accumulation and cites `published_shas` as its justification without reading the row above it. | M6 - open |
| D-145 | design | 2026-08-01 | **Bundle ids are per-instance sequential, so nothing addressed by id survives leaving the instance that minted it.** VERIFIED: `allocId` (`store.mjs:4401`) mints per-instance sequence numbers, so every sovereign instance independently produces `INFO-2026-0001`. Hashes are global and ids are not. This is a hole under M6's whole promise — a record that can be mirrored, verified and outlived by tooling that already exists — because everything the record says ABOUT a document is addressed by id: `references[].target`, `refs`, `inquiry_basis`, `elevated_into`, every citation edge. An export can be verified byte for byte and its internal graph cannot be re-pointed at anything outside itself, and two instances citing each other would collide on the first id either had ever minted. The design pass asked what GRADE a cross-instance citation carries (`AUDIENCES` row 17) and nobody asked whether it can be ADDRESSED at all. Candidate shapes, none free: an instance-scoped prefix (cheap, changes every id and this record is append-only), a UUID beside the human id (no migration, two identifiers for one thing), or citation by content hash with the id as a comment — which is the shape the record's own doctrine already prefers, since identity is canonical and the address is a comment. | M6 - open |
| D-146 | gap | 2026-08-01 | **Six UI surfaces READ the entity/connection/progression layer and nothing can WRITE it — nine mutating ops landed with no caller anywhere, so the surfaces render empty by construction.** VERIFIED. The UI calls `entity`, `entitybyalias`, `connections`, `concerns`, `resolutions` and `captureprogressions` — all reads. Of the eleven mutating ops in that family, only `taskresolve` and `connect` have any caller: **`entitycreate`, `entityalias`, `relationdeclare`, `resolve`, `resolvetestify`, `progressiondefine`, `thread` and `discharge` have no caller in `civicos-ui/app.html` OR `bio-plane/src/setup.mjs`.** So an entire milestone's write path is unreachable while its read path is shipped and rendering. THIS CORRECTS AN EARLIER CLAIM OF MINE IN THE SHARPEST DIRECTION. I recorded that the A-D grades and the entity axis 'have no consumer until the claim layer exists', and used that to argue the claim layer should be built first so they would acquire one. The literal claim is false and the truth is worse: **they have consumers and no producers.** Six surfaces already depend on this layer and display nothing, and no amount of building above it changes that. The ordering consequence is real: the entity WRITE surface is not a later enrichment, it is what makes six shipped surfaces non-empty AND what produces the earned grades an inquiry's basis legs need (`resolutions` being the A/B/C source). Build the claim layer and the entity write surface together, not one behind the other. | M4 - open |
| D-147 | design | 2026-08-01 | **The `action` object models a records request as one round trip, and the real lifecycle has at least four decision points with their own clocks and outcomes.** `action` runs `planned -> active -> awaiting_response -> resolved / abandoned`, so `awaiting_response` collapses everything between asking and getting. The externally-sourced lifecycle (see `PROBLEM-DOMAIN.md`) has distinct stages that each produce a different fact about the body: the FEE ESTIMATE, the FEE-WAIVER decision, PARTIAL PRODUCTION, the ADMINISTRATIVE APPEAL, and litigation. Each has its own clock and its own outcome vocabulary, and collapsing them loses precisely what an accountability record wants. Federal appeal adjudication averaged 96.91 days in FY2024 [DOJ OIP, government's own statistics] and appeal outcomes vary by an order of magnitude between agencies — complete reversal 9.3% at State, 17.6% at DOJ, 31.4% at DHS [computed from the three agencies' own FY2024 annual reports] — so **a design must not assume a single reversal rate or a single response shape**. This is D-128's declared-versus-actual flow with real numbers attached: the declared flow is the statutory timeline, the actual flow is what these figures describe, and the delta is the finding. | M4 - open |
| D-148 | design | 2026-08-01 | **A fee quote is EVIDENCE about a public body, and the design treats it as an administrative obstacle.** Sourced examples: Colorado's Secretary of State quoted **$2.1M** (69,736 projected labor hours) for election contracts; Texas DCJ quoted **$1,132,024.30** for records the state PREA office supplied for **$551.39** — **a 2,000x spread for identical records**; Nevada DETR quoted $650,000, then **dropped a $1,083 charge entirely and released the records free once challenged**, which is direct evidence the quote was not cost-based; DoD quoted **~$660 million** [MuckRock survey Dec 2023; Las Vegas Review-Journal Sept 2023; Techdirt — press and NGO sources, labelled as such]. Against that, the federal fee system collects **0.34-0.38% of what FOIA costs to run**, about **$1.54 per request**, and the State Department collected **$0.00** in FY2024 [government's own reported statistics] — **fees are a deterrent instrument, not a cost-recovery one**, and the record should be able to say so with its own captured evidence. Consequence for the design: a fee quote should be a first-class captured artifact with a comparison basis, not correspondence prose. Two identical requests priced 2,000x apart is exactly the shape BIO exists to make checkable, and today it would be a paragraph in a `## Correspondence` heading. | M4 - open |
| D-149 | defect | 2026-08-01 | **The design is jurisdiction-blind, and BIO's own jurisdiction differs from federal FOIA on every axis that matters.** Nothing in the record models WHICH access regime an `action` runs under, and the assumptions differ sharply. Federal: fee-shifting is discretionary ('the court MAY assess... substantially prevailed', 5 USC 552(a)(4)(E)), search and review fees are chargeable by requester class, and plaintiffs substantially prevailed in only **112 of 1,672 lawsuits, 6.7%** [GAO-16-667, the government auditing itself]. **California, which is Oakland and therefore BIO's actual regime**: fee-shifting is MANDATORY ('the court SHALL award court costs and reasonable attorney's fees to the requester', Gov. Code 7923.115(a)); fees are **direct duplication cost only** with no search fee and no review fee (7922.530(a)); and **redaction time is not billable at all** — *National Lawyers Guild v. City of Hayward* (2020) 9 Cal.5th 488 threw out a $2,938.58 invoice for 40.2 hours of bodycam redaction, holding that 'extraction' means retrieving responsive data, not omitting unwanted data. So in California 'it will take us 200 hours to redact this' cannot lawfully become a bill — and a design carrying federal assumptions would model a cost that does not exist here and miss a violation when one is charged. A pending 2026 California bill would loosen the fee rules [CalMatters, Mar 2026]; flag as pending, not law. | M4 - open |
| D-150 | design | 2026-08-01 | **The completeness claim is VALIDATED by the strongest external evidence in the study — and the same evidence says an authored field is necessary and not sufficient.** From `PROBLEM-DOMAIN.md`: concealing exculpatory evidence appears in **44% of exonerations involving official misconduct**; Nifong scoped a report to exclude; Fujitsu edited out references to known defects; South Yorkshire amended 116 statements. **Every retained fact was true in every one of those cases** — which is precisely the failure the completeness claim targets and the reason a case is not merely a published finding. That is the clearest validation any BIO construct has received from outside the repo. BUT THE SAME SOURCES CARRY A CORRECTION: in both the SBI and Rolling Stone reviews the catch was **a second person with standing**, not a form field — and in each the person who found the gap was overruled inside the hierarchy. So the authored exclusion statement is necessary and insufficient, and the design should say so rather than let the field stand in for the check. What that implies is not settled here: candidates are a second-member acknowledgement on the exclusion statement before publication, or making the statement the thing a reviewer is specifically asked to attack rather than one field among many. Do not build the field and call the problem solved. | M10 - open · REC-14 (disposition corrected 2026-08-01 by session BOB: this row was written `M6` when the ladder had no rung above M8, and M6 is *the record can be left, mirrored and outlived* — nothing to do with a completeness claim at publication. M10 now exists and is where publication lives.) |


| D-152 | design | 2026-08-01 | **Image-only PDFs must be OCR'd and investigated, and OCR text must never be indistinguishable from text the publisher wrote.** RULED 2026-08-01 (DEC-4), overturning the recommendation that scanned PDFs stay honestly unreadable. Bob: *"Some PDFs will be released in the public record as image only. The textual contents of these images need to be extracted and investigated for meaningful content."* The refused argument was a volume argument — ~14% of a 14-document sample, skewing to design and scan artifacts — and coverage is not a volume question: a class the record can capture and never read is a hole, and it does not have to be large, only non-empty. The class is also not randomly assigned; scanned-only is what a signed order, an exhibit, a handwritten annotation and a deliberately-imaged release all look like. *"Investigated for meaningful content"* puts OCR output on the READING path (FW-15's L2→L3 wire), not merely in a text field, or the class is captured, readable and still ungraphed. **THE PROVENANCE RULE IS STRUCTURAL AND IS THE THING THAT COULD TURN THIS CAPABILITY INTO AN OVERCLAIMING DEFECT:** extracted text carries `text_source: 'layer'`; OCR carries `text_source: 'ocr'` with engine, version and per-region confidence, and they are distinguishable everywhere the text is shown, cited or indexed. A basis leg resting on OCR'd text carries its IMAGE REGION (page + rect) so a reader checks the claim against the pixels rather than against our transcription — the same discipline as answering by hash. OCR never raises a capture grade, and a low-confidence region reads `undetermined` rather than a best guess, which is the mojibake rule one layer up. **MEASUREMENT GATES DESIGN:** whether OCR runs in-plane at all is unknown — a WASM engine's bundle size against the Worker limit and its CPU against the isolate ceiling (D-36, D-56) — with the pdf-worker fleet member (I6) the first fallback and an external service the second. Nothing is designed before that number exists. | M2 - open · CPDF-9 (measure), CPDF-10 (build) |

| D-153 | design | 2026-08-01 | **`research/AUDIENCES.md` H4 and H6 are not hazards to be avoided; they are the required workflow — corrected by external evidence, and the pass is NOT edited.** RULED 2026-08-01 (DEC-13). AUDIENCES §7 runs `RATIFY -> CASE -> rendering -> action -> consequence` and §9 lists the pre-publication release of material among its HAZARDS — H4 (*the low-threshold rendering that escapes*) and H6 (*the embargo*). The practice literature says the ordering is inverted, and the sources are professional standards rather than opinion: the SPJ code (*"diligently seek out subjects of news stories to give them the opportunity to respond to allegations of wrongdoing"*); GAGAS/Yellow Book, where obtaining the views of responsible officials is a REPORTING REQUIREMENT and GAO's own protocols give an agency **7 to 30 calendar days** on a draft report, expect a single position including the rationale for any disagreement, and print the response beside the finding; and the Columbia Journalism School review of Rolling Stone's *A Rape on Campus*, which identified a comment request made **without specifics** as the central failure. So an unratified rendering carrying specific claims must be able to leave the building, addressed to the subject, under a deadline, before anything is ratified — which is exactly AUDIENCES row 14's *"delivery with no bucket"*, and the outside evidence says that row is the MAIN PATH for any case that names anybody rather than an edge case. **THE PASS IS DELIBERATELY NOT EDITED** — a research pass is a record of what it concluded, and correcting it in place would rewrite history and hide that the evidence arrived later and overturned it; same treatment `RECONCILED.md` gave the storyboard files, with the correction living in a live surface and a pointer going on the pass. What Bob's ruling adds beyond the sources: the GATE is not the contact but the group's DECLARED, JUSTIFIED POSITION on it, carried inside the case as declared bias — which is what makes a tipping-off refusal legitimate and visible instead of quiet. | M10 - open · REC-14, REC-24, UI-17 |

| D-154 | design | 2026-08-01 | **A HUNCH is temporary declared bias with a temporarily-high connection grade, and it is bias debt that must clear before publication.** RULED 2026-08-01 (DEC-15), and it resolves the D1(a)-versus-D1(b) fork by showing it was a false one: an authored connection grade and an earned one are not rivals, they are two phases of the same object. During `open` a member may author a connection grade high enough to make otherwise disconnected evidence traversable — *"the investigator thinks this is true"* — and at `published` no hunch may remain, so every leg is earned or is honest testimony. **THE GATE ALREADY EXISTED AND WAS BUILT FOR SOMETHING ELSE:** `BIO_Declared_Bias_v0_1.md`'s bias-debt section already ends *"a work product carrying bias debt cannot advance its workproduct_state or be ratified for publication until the debt is settled"*, written for a bias statement CHANGING and leaving old analysis owed a re-run. Registering a hunch as what it is reaches that rule with no new machinery. It also settles what CLEARING means without a further ruling: debt is *cleared by re-running the evaluation under the current set*, so the test is neither "delete the leg" nor "leave it in unrated" — **the case must still hold when the hunch is removed from the set**. `grade_source` gains `hunch` beside `resolution` and `testimony`; it is the ONLY authored grade permitted above D, permitted precisely because it cannot survive publication; it carries an author and a date; and it is **visible as a hunch on every surface from the moment it is made**, because the failure mode this construct invites is a hunch quietly ageing into a fact. A hunch is NOT an `undetermined` leg and must never be composed as one — R1 suspends an axis when a grade is ABSENT, while a hunch grade is present and asserted, and treating it as undetermined would destroy the traversability the ruling exists to preserve. `op=publishpreflight` refuses `UNCLEARED_HUNCH` naming every leg, before any signature exists. Manifest registration needs `object_type: bias` (D-84) and is sequenced behind it; the leg-level source and the refusal ship with the claim layer where they bite. | M9 - open · REC-11, REC-12, REC-15, REC-18, UI-11, UI-12 |

| D-155 | design | 2026-08-01 | **A project declares the evidentiary strength its work requires, and the published case carries that declaration beside the strength it reached.** RULED 2026-08-01 (DEC-17). `BIO_Content_Framework_v0_10.md` §12 has recorded since v0.1 that a claim *"needs a standard of proof attached and that is doctrine rather than architecture — the next design conversation"*; this is that conversation. The question was what makes an unsupported case harder to state, and both options this project generated put the standard in the wrong place: a global floor would refuse honest weak findings, and no floor leaves nothing to fall short of. **The standard belongs to the GROUP and to a particular piece of their work** — a project convened to refer something to an auditor needs a different bar from one convened to decide whether a thing is worth looking at, and both are legitimate, so a single system-wide answer was always going to be wrong for one of them. **This is the PROJECT axis and it is not the AUDIENCE axis**, which is why it does not contradict `research/AUDIENCES.md` §5's ruling that per-audience relaxation is *"a structural prior by role — the same defect as a suspicion flag, with the sign reversed"* and that a threshold belongs on a rendering and never on ratification. A project-declared bar is authored by the group, before the work, about its own intentions; nobody's standard is set by who they are. That file is not edited (see D-153's reasoning); its ruling stands and is about a different axis. SHAPE: `project` frontmatter gains `required_strength{capture, connection}` — **a PAIR per R2**, because a scalar would re-collapse the two axes in the one field a reader is most likely to quote. C-2.9 already requires a non-empty `objective` on the same object, so the check has a precedent beside it. `op=publishpreflight` refuses `BELOW_PROJECT_STRENGTH` naming the axis; the escape is amending the project's declared bar, which is an authored, dated, on-the-record act visible in the published case — you can lower your own bar, you cannot do it quietly. A project with no declared strength gates nothing and the case SAYS so rather than rendering a blank, since an absent bar is not a bar of zero. An inquiry outside any project has no bar and inherits none. | M10 - open · REC-14, REC-15, UI-18 |

| D-156 | design | 2026-08-02 | **"Audience" names two different things in this project, and they have different machinery attached.** Named by Bob 2026-08-02 (DEC-17 addendum): in requirements gathering the word refers to **user types** — the people who use BIO; when referring to a published case it means **the readers and consumers of that case** — elements of the public who use the information, analysis and conclusions it contains. VERIFIED in the corpus: `research/AUDIENCES.md` enumerates *"eight audiences"* mixing both senses in one list — an administrator or a lawyer READING A PUBLISHED CASE is the second sense, while *"the publisher's own future members"* and the primary archetype (*a member of a community accountability group*, from `JOURNEY-PRIMARY.md`) are BIO USERS and are the first; the word `archetype` occurs twice in the entire file. **THE CONFLATION IS WHY THAT FILE'S §5 READS AS A WARNING RATHER THAN A DEFINITION.** Its hard constraint — *"a per-audience relaxation is a structural prior by role… if administrators need a lower threshold, that is a threshold on a RENDERING, and it must never be a threshold on RATIFICATION"* — is correct and unchanged, but it had to be written defensively because "administrator" sat in a list mixing a user type with a case reader, which makes a per-role gate look like a natural extension of the list. Separated, the rule is definitional: **renderings and thresholds vary by AUDIENCE; capabilities, journeys and surfaces vary by USER TYPE; neither ever reaches ratification.** DISAMBIGUATION ADOPTED: `AUDIENCE` is reserved for readers/consumers of a published case; the requirements sense is `USER TYPE`, or `ARCHETYPE` where a document already uses that word. This is the D-68 / D-8 vocabulary-drift class caught BEFORE it reached the code — no surface, table or op names either concept today, so the correction costs documents and no migration, which is the whole reason to pay it now. The affected documents are `research/AUDIENCES.md`, `research/JOURNEY-PRIMARY.md`, `UI-PLAN.md` and `research/CAPABILITIES.md`; the research passes take a pointer rather than an edit (D-153's reasoning). **AMENDED 2026-08-02 by Bob, and the correction is larger than the vocabulary split:** the USER TYPE list is not one archetype. Bob: the community-accountability-group member is the PRIMARY archetype, but user types also include *"journalists, lawyers, city administrators, an oversight body, and any other role that involves collecting evidence, investigating, and any other group interested in improving government using the capabilities and processes of the CivicOS."* **The consequence D-156 did not see: the same person can be BOTH.** A lawyer may USE an instance to build a case and may also READ someone else's published case — the two senses are not two populations of people, they are two RELATIONSHIPS to a case, and which one applies depends on the case, not on the person. That is why the machinery differs (capabilities and surfaces versus renderings and thresholds) and why neither ever reaches ratification. | M10 - open · a documentation pass, no code |
| D-158 | defect | 2026-08-02 | **A signing key registered for a member who never ENROLLED reads `active` on `op=signerlist` and is refused by `op=ratify`.** MEASURED 2026-08-02 (session BOB, `MEASUREMENTS.md`, real `ssh-keygen` signatures through the real ratify path). `signerList()` (`store.mjs:5884`) reads the `signers` table alone; `gateFacts()` (`store.mjs:5920`) joins `members` and requires `s.status='active' AND m.status='active'`. The REVOCATION path agrees only because `memberSet` (`store.mjs:5857-5861`) cascades — it deletes the member's sessions and sets their signers to `revoked`. There is no cascade for the other direction: a member is `status='invited'` until enrolment (`store.mjs:5731`) and `signerAdd` checks only that the member EXISTS (`store.mjs:5874`), so an admin may register a key for someone who has never enrolled and `signerlist` reports it `active` while a signature from it returns `SIG_UNKNOWN_KEY`. **Not a blocker for RECONCILED §4 Q11**, which is settled YES on this measurement: the surface's *"you hold no active signing key"* is never a false alarm, because a key `signerlist` does not show active is one `ratify` will not accept. It is wrong only in the optimistic direction. **Two candidate fixes, and the second is preferable:** make `signerList()` join `members` so one predicate serves both, or make `signerAdd` refuse a member who has not enrolled — the join is the smaller change but leaves a row that means nothing; refusing at write keeps the table honest and is the shape `signerAdd`'s existing NO_SUCH_MEMBER check already reaches for. Whichever ships, the other view must be asserted against it. | M10 - open · REC-15/UI-17 pre-flight; scoped in the 2026-08-02 BOB INBOX entry |
| D-159 | design | 2026-08-02 | **An ungraded leg now costs a conclusion nothing, which makes leaving one ungraded the cheapest way to keep an inconvenient leg out of the reckoning.** Created by DEC-18: an ungraded leg is INERT — it does not contribute, does not floor and does not unrate — so a member holding a leg that cuts against their conclusion can decline to grade it and the conclusion stands on its load-bearing legs. This is a laundering path in the OPPOSITE direction from the one R1 closed, and it is named here rather than left for a build session to discover. **Three defences already stand and a fourth should not be invented before use shows one is needed:** every ungraded leg is NAMED, on the case and in the index row (DEC-18's plural clause); the completeness statement is authored, unprefilled and must say what the case leaves out; and invariant 7 makes a leg that cuts against travel the same path as one that supports. **What would settle it is USE, not argument** — whether real groups leave cuts-against legs ungraded is observable once M9/M10 ship, and inventing a counterweight now would be the same mistake as a global strength floor (§4 Q2). **RE-READ 2026-08-08 by PL-14 / IS-7 against a machine shaping legs at volume (the re-reading QUEUE IS-7 asked for), OUTCOME: THE RULE IS UNCHANGED AND NO FOURTH DEFENCE IS OWED — but ONE THING DID CHANGE and it is not the rule.** (a) **THE MACHINE HOLDS NO LEVER, and that is now enforced rather than intended.** D-159's hazard is a CHOICE — a member declining to grade a leg that cuts against them. A machine has no such choice: §5 rules that *"the grades arrive from the record"* and PL-14 enforces it at the READ, taking every effective grade from `earnedBasisRegistry` and leaving a leg ungraded only where the record earned nothing. So volume does not multiply a decision nobody can make, and the composed-at-volume case is not a wider version of this debt. (b) **BUT THE FIRST DEFENCE DEGRADES WITH VOLUME WHILE THE ARITHMETIC DOES NOT.** *"Every ungraded leg is NAMED"* is a defence only while somebody reads the names; one ungraded leg among four is seen, forty among two hundred in a machine-composed reading is a list nobody reads. The rule holds; its enforcement by a human reader thins. (c) **AND A SIBLING LEVER NOW EXISTS THAT D-159 COULD NOT HAVE NAMED:** under DEC-32's OR-max a leg can be put in a BRANCH OF ITS OWN, where it neither floors nor unrates — visibly present and contributing nothing, which is a stronger form of the same move. That is DEC-32's own named hazard with its own three containments (authored sufficiency, the compound falsifier, the reader), so it is a sibling and not an extension. **WHAT THE RE-READING CHANGES IS THE OBSERVABLE, not the rule:** this row says to watch whether groups leave cuts-against legs ungraded; against a machine the fact worth measuring is the RATIO AND ITS PROVENANCE — how many of a reading's legs are ungraded, and whether each is ungraded because THE RECORD EARNED NOTHING or because A MEMBER DID NOT GRADE IT. Those are one fact today and will not be. **PL-14 makes that measurement cheap rather than taking it:** `op=versionstrength` publishes `ungraded[]` with a per-leg reason distinguishing the two, so when M10 runs with a real group the number this row waits for can be read off the answer instead of reconstructed. RAISED, not acted on — the disposition below is unchanged. | M10 - open · WATCH, no task — re-raise when M10 has run with a real group |
| D-160 | drift | 2026-08-02 | **`RECONCILED.md` says `SUSPEND` 50 times and never says `UNRATED`, which is the canonical word.** `BIO_Case_Making_v0_1.md` R1 renamed it after the reconciliation pass was written and said the rename *"is not cosmetic"* — `SUSPEND` already meant the OPPOSITE thing in `SB-OUTPUT` §5.1, and `BUILD-ORDER`'s REC-12 says *"ship SUSPEND"* citing that file, so a worker following the build order in good faith would build the behaviour R1 forbids while every document appears to agree. That collision is what RECONCILED's own R1-h row identifies as *"the single most dangerous collision in the corpus"* — and RECONCILED then adopted the dangerous word as its canonical one. **Mitigated 2026-08-02** by a dated amendment block at the head of §1.1 declaring UNRATED canonical and every SUSPEND below it to be read as UNRATED; the 50 occurrences are NOT rewritten, because RECONCILED is a reconciliation record and rewriting it in place would hide that the collision happened. **What closing it takes:** REC-12 and any item quoting §1.1 must use UNRATED in the code, the copy and the test names. | M9 - open · REC-12 carries it |
| D-161 | gap | 2026-08-03 | **A connection is anchored to specific passages and then throws the anchor away.** `resolutions` is keyed `(capture_sha, ref, entity_id)` — a SPECIFIC reference inside a capture, with its own grade and method — and `reading_refs` gives each `ref` a kind, key and label. `deriveConnections` (`store.mjs:3499`) then *"collapses them to the STRONGEST grade each capture resolved to the entity at, and forms one connection per PAIR of distinct captures"*, so the connection row carries `a_capture_sha`/`b_capture_sha` and no passage. **Consequence:** following a connection lands a reader on a whole document — a 300-page budget PDF — rather than on the line that grounds it, and a case's citations cannot be checked at the point they are made. Raised by Bob 2026-08-03: *"a connection can also refer to specific content within a document, right?"* The data is not lost — it is in `resolutions` — it is simply not carried onto the connection. **Design question inside it, because one capture may resolve to the same entity at several refs: does a connection carry the ref pair that DETERMINED its grade, or all supporting mentions?** Recommendation is the determining pair, because that is the rule the record already applies everywhere else (name the leg that is why), with the full set reachable behind it. **NARROWED 2026-09-18 by FW-21, measured at the code rather than read off the rows.** (1) **The CARRY half is CLOSED by FW-17** (`5b46c43`): `connections` carries `a_ref`/`b_ref` and each end's position, `deriveConnections` writes them, `#connectionView` publishes `determining_pair`. The row was never told. (2) **The design question is ANSWERED by Bob and the answer was NOT BUILT.** His 2026-09-14 second pass on 5.4 (`b41db4fb`, 16:39, two and a half hours BEFORE FW-17 landed at 19:05) ruled that the connection pair is the ON-POINT pair, CHOSEN, not merely the strongest-graded mention. FW-17 built exactly the recommendation above instead: `Store.deriveConnections` keeps, per capture, the row that wins `GRADE_RANK[r.grade] > GRADE_RANK[cur.grade]`, so the pair is the STRONGEST-GRADED mention, a tie between equal grades goes to whichever row the scan returned first (the query orders by `capture_sha` only, so within a capture the order is unspecified), and `schema.mjs`'s own `connections` comment says so in words. No op, column or state lets anyone choose, and nothing on the row says the pair was not chosen. (3) **THE CONSEQUENCE IS IN THE OVERCLAIMING DIRECTION, DRIVEN 2026-09-18 (M-51, `node tools/fw21-onpoint-probe.mjs`) through `op=promote`/`op=resolve`/`op=connect`/`op=connections&content=`:** document A resolves to one ordinance TWICE at grade A (a reference read on p.2 and another read on p.9), document B once (p.5). The pair keeps p.2. A member's citation of PAGE 9 of A — where the ordinance genuinely IS, at grade A — answers `connection_grade: null`, `outside: 1` (`CONNECTION_PAIR_OUTSIDE_EXTENT`, C-49.1) and the sentence *"all 1 of this document's connections were established by references read outside page 9, so none of them reaches this citation"*. **A citation of page 7, which never mentions the ordinance, answers with byte-identical counts** — the record cannot tell the two apart. Flipping the collapse's `>` to `>=` (a tie-break carrying no relevance input) moves the pair to p.9 and the `outside` to p.2. **That is a definite NO the record cannot support**: the truth is that the record chose a different mention by grade and never asked which one is on point, which is UNDETERMINED. `reading_refs` is keyed `(capture_sha, ref)`, so a SINGLE reference string repeated on several pages is held at ONE position (`INSERT OR REPLACE`), so the same shape looks reachable with a single ref — READ from the schema and the writer, NOT driven. **What closing it takes, and it is not one owner's:** RECORD — `connectionGradeForContent` must not say `outside` while another resolution of the same capture to the same entity is placed inside the extent (that alone is small, a C-49 arm plus a suite ground, and it is the overclaim fix); the pair must say HOW it was chosen (`strongest-graded` today) so a reader never takes a grade collapse for a relevance judgement; and the member's act that CHOOSES the on-point pair (an I5/I3 change, its own IC) with a UI affordance on the connection. **REC-86 does NOT cover it**: REC-86 is NARROW of a basis LEG, and a connection is a derived edge between two captures that no leg names. Part II §14.5, §17 and §18 said "rowed (REC-86)" and were corrected by FW-21 in the same commit. **NARROWED AGAIN 2026-09-18 by REC-120 (IC-129), acts (1) and (2) of the three above — driven through `op=promote`/`op=resolve`/`op=connect`/`op=connections`, suite `bio-plane/test/rec120-onpoint-undetermined.test.mjs`.** (1) `connectionGradeForContent` reads EVERY resolution of the cited capture to the connection's entity (with its `reading_refs` position) and answers UNDETERMINED under a new C-49.4 `CONNECTION_PAIR_MENTION_UNCHOSEN`, naming the mentions, where a definite answer would rest on the pair alone: a pair OUTSIDE the part while another mention is inside or cannot be placed (M-51's page 9 now reads `undetermined: 1`, page 7 still `outside: 1` under C-49.1 with its pre-item sentence), and — required by REC-120's own negative control, which the row's act (1) wording did not cover — a pair INSIDE the part that was kept only on a TIE over an equal-grade mention not inside it (M-51's page 2, which FW-21's probe used as its ground, now reads UNDETERMINED: its "reach" flipped with a one-character tie-break and so carried no relevance). A weaker mention outside does not unsettle a reach; a one-mention document is byte-identical (golden taken on the pristine tree). (2) The pair says what it is: `connections.pair_rule` (I5) records `strongest-graded/first-reference-by-sort`, the scan now orders `capture_sha, ref` so the tie-break EXISTS rather than being SQLite's row order, the row's basis says "strongest-graded mention … not one chosen as on point", and `determining_pair.selection` (I3) publishes `{method, tie_break, chosen: false, says}` — `tie_break: null` with the reason stated on a row derived before REC-120. **What remains open is act (3) ONLY: the member's act of CHOOSING the on-point pair** (I5 + I3, RECORD + UI, its own IC), without which every multi-mention document's partial citations stay UNDETERMINED — honest, and the cost is that a member gets no definite answer where several mentions exist. Also still open and NOT driven: `reading_refs` holds ONE position per `(capture_sha, ref)`, so the same reference string read on several pages is one mention to this check (read from the schema, as FW-21 said). | M4 - open · carry half CLOSED by FW-17; acts (1)+(2) CLOSED by REC-120 (IC-129); act (3), the member's CHOICE of the on-point pair, is ruled by Bob, unbuilt, and has NO queue row — CONDUCT to row it |
| D-162 | gap | 2026-08-03 | **A connection runs through a NAMED thing, so two documents about the same IDEA cannot be connected.** `ENTITY_KINDS` (`store.mjs`) is `source, institution, office, movement, person, body, ordinance, parcel, contract, fund` — every one a named entity. There is no `concept`, `topic` or `theme`. So two staff reports both concerning *deferred maintenance*, naming no shared entity, have no connection available to them. Raised by Bob 2026-08-03, who described a connection as running through *"the same entity/idea"* — the second half of which is not built. **Not obviously a defect:** an idea has no identity criteria, and a registry of themes is how a record starts asserting relationships nobody can check, which is the failure mode `BIO_Declared_Bias_v0_1.md`'s malformedness rule exists to prevent. **What it needs is a decision, not a table** — whether a theme can be an entity while staying falsifiable. | M4 - open · DOCTRINE (Bob) — raise as a DEC when M4 reaches the entity axis |

| D-195 | design | 2026-08-04 | **DEC-32's OR-arithmetic takes the MAXIMUM over branches, which is only sound if the branches are INDEPENDENT — and nothing in the design tests independence, which three separate research strands identify as the exact failure that defeats every professional methodology.** DEC-32 (ruled 2026-08-04) sets strength as MIN over AND-related legs and MAX over OR-related branches, with *independently sufficient* an AUTHORED member judgment. **The research says an authored judgment is precisely what fails here, because correlation is invisible from where the author stands.** THREE INDEPENDENT DEMONSTRATIONS: (1) **The New York Times' Iraq WMD post-mortem** — reporters had defector testimony PLUS senior administration confirmation, two sources from different institutional categories, and the editors' note records that the accounts were *"often eagerly confirmed by United States officials"* who had themselves been fed by the same INC/Chalabi pipeline. Multiplicity was real; independence was not. (2) **Steve Buttry's Farragut case (Verification Handbook, p.15)** — twelve team members plus the opposing star and coach, fourteen honest non-colluding eyewitnesses, all consistent, all WRONG the same way; game video showed one foul where all fourteen remembered three or four. His conclusion: source count is not evidence when a shared narrative can generate correlated error. (3) **The Berkeley Protocol names CIRCULAR REPORTING as a hazard (para.187) and supplies no test for it**; the Verification Handbook prescribes triangulation while its own lead case study shows triangulation failing; Bellingcat's peer review is same-institution; ICIJ's journalists all work one corpus. **THE CONSEQUENCE FOR BIO IS EXACT: two OR-branches whose legs trace to captures sharing an origin are not two grounds, and taking the max publishes a strength the finding does not have — the Judith Miller error with arithmetic behind it.** THE FIX BIO IS UNUSUALLY WELL PLACED TO BUILD, and no newsroom could: provenance is CONTENT-ADDRESSED and every capture records its origin and chain, so the system can DERIVE whether two branches share an upstream source and surface it. Derived informs, authored binds (D-90) — the system flags *these two grounds share a source* and the member decides; it must not silently compute independence, and must not refuse the act. Also record what the member asserted: *independently sufficient* should mean independent in ORIGIN, not merely in reasoning, and the entry's own anti-gaming argument (visibility to a reader) only works if the shared origin is visible too. | M9 · open — pair with REC-12's strength derivation; DEC-32's arithmetic is unchanged, the INDEPENDENCE CHECK is what is missing |
| D-194 | gap | 2026-08-04 | **A member's LEAD has no home, and a search that came back EMPTY is not recorded at all — so the most evidentiary product of shoe leather is the one the record drops.** Named by Bob 2026-08-04: *"A reporter faces the same hazard — that corpus silence gets read as evidence of absence. That's why tips and shoe leather are so valuable."* MEASURED: the frontier today is the `deferred` link partition (`subresources.mjs:660`) — URLs DISCOVERED INSIDE DOCUMENTS. **A tip is not a URL**; it is a thing a member has reason to believe exists, sought. Nothing in the model holds *"there should be a contract between X and Y — look for it"*, and nothing holds the outcome when the looking fails. So the frontier is entirely DERIVED, and a derived frontier inherits the corpus's blind spots exactly — the machine analogue of a reporter who only reads clippings. **THE CONSEQUENCE THAT MAKES THIS EVIDENTIARY RATHER THAN ERGONOMIC:** RFC 2308's rule (see `STORE-AS-CACHE.md`) is that a negative answer with no proof of AUTHORITY attached is not recordable at all — the SOA is what makes *it does not exist* a finding rather than a shrug. **A member's authored lead IS that authority.** *We went looking for the document this tip described, across these sources, on this date, and there is none* is a finding with a name behind it; the same empty result with no lead behind it is silence that cost nothing to produce. Sibling to D-184 (a member's firsthand OBSERVATION has no home as a basis leg) — the same member knowledge is unrepresentable both BEFORE the search and AFTER it, and the two should be designed together. | M4 - open · the authored frontier; pair with D-184 **DESIGNED 2026-09-18 with D-184 in `docs/development/MEMBER-KNOWLEDGE-DESIGN.md` §5 (the LEAD as an authored row, never evidence, writing `observation_log` with `authority_kind = 'lead'`).** It had been recorded for the same reason as D-184 (designed together): no Program B design exists at the artifact.** Owed: the LEAD as an object, its writer, and how it becomes the frontier's internet level. | D-190 | gap | 2026-08-04 | **A Durable Object's SQLite is capped at 10 GB per object and this ceiling is recorded nowhere in the repository.** VENDOR CLAIM (developers.cloudflare.com/durable-objects/platform/limits, retrieved 2026-08-04): 10 GB per object on Workers Paid, 1 GB on Free; unlimited objects; a soft ~1,000 req/s per object. Today that is abstract because every instance is a development instance. **Under the read-through-cache framing (`STORE-AS-CACHE.md`) it stops being abstract, because the store then grows BY DESIGN rather than by member action.** At some corpus size the record must sharded across objects, and the single-threaded ~1,000 req/s per object is a second ceiling on the same decision. Deciding it now is cheap; deciding it after a cache model ships is the D-113/scheduler class — a shape question settled late, with consumers already built on the wrong assumption. NOT MEASURED BY US: the vendor figure is a claim, and what matters is the growth CURVE per captured document, which nothing has measured. | M6 · **PARTLY MEASURED 2026-08-08 (FL-1, riding D-218s measurement — same `MEASUREMENTS.md` entry).** Instrument: the planes own `op=stats` -> `dbBytes`, i.e. workerds `ctx.storage.sql.databaseSize` (`src/store.mjs:11209`), read live on two namespaces. **Empty-schema baseline 925,696 B; loaded store 6,402,048 B at 31 bundles / 142 files / 88 register / 244 history -> 176,657 B per bundle**, which against the vendors 10 GB claim is ~60,800 bundles and against 1 GB ~6,073 (numerator ours, denominator theirs). **ALSO MEASURED, and it is why this row stays OPEN: the platform has NO per-object storage surface at all** — `durableObjectsStorageGroups` carries only date dimensions and returned zero rows on this account, and `durableObjectsPeriodicGroups` is per-object but reports no stored bytes, so `dbBytes` off the object is the only handle there is. **AND THE MEASURED POINT HAS AN EMPTY MEANING LAYER — connections, entities and resolutions are all ZERO — so the slope prices CAPTURE ONLY and is a FLOOR on cost / an OVERSTATEMENT of capacity.** What remains is exactly D-224s measurement: the quadratic `connections` growth on the same object. Do not read 60,800 as headroom until that lands |
| D-191 | design | 2026-08-04 | **A capture assembled from reused subresources is a COMPOSITE with a temporal spread that the record does not state, so it may depict a moment that never existed.** From the archival-systems survey (`STORE-AS-CACHE.md`): Ainsworth, Nelson & Van de Sompel measured that embedded resources in an archived page routinely carry datetimes *"up to several years in the future or past"* relative to the embedding page, and the follow-up study is titled *"Only One Out of Five Archived Web Pages Existed as Presented"* (arxiv.org/abs/1402.0928; HT '15). **BIO builds exactly this shape on purpose**: `CAPTURE-SCALING.md`'s reuse policy fetches evidence and reuses furniture, and the manifest records `reused_from` and `reused_fetched_at` PER PART — so the data to compute the spread exists and nothing computes or states it. CAP-4's re-fetch at ratification NARROWS the spread and does not MEASURE it. The honest shape is the one the record uses everywhere else: state the fact rather than fix it — a composite capture carries its temporal extent (earliest and latest part fetch instant) and a reader weighs it, exactly as they weigh a grade. This matters most for a rendered capture offered as evidence of what a page LOOKED like, which is the class D-64/D-55 covers. | M2 - open · state the spread; the per-part data already exists |
| D-192 | defect | 2026-08-04 | **Integrity of stored bytes is not integrity of what a reader SEES, and archived-page replay is a documented attack surface.** Lerner, Kohno & Roesner, *Rewriting History: Changing the Archived Web from the Present* (ACM CCS 2017, adalerner.com/lerner-rewritingHistory-CCS17.pdf): four demonstrated attacks that alter a reader's view of an archived page — text, images, styling and behaviour — by injecting or abusing script that runs at VIEW time, and the paper's own summary is that *"attackers do not need to compromise the archives in order to compromise users' views of a stored page."* BIO stores captured HTML and renders it, and the whole product is trustworthiness of the record, so a reader shown an altered rendition of bytes we hold intact is the defect class this project cares about most — the hash stays correct while the claim the reader forms is wrong. NOT YET ASSESSED against our own viewer: what BIO renders, with what sandboxing, and whether any captured script executes at view time is UNMEASURED and is the first step. The mitigation vocabulary is standard (sandboxed iframes, CSP, stripping or neutering script at RENDER time while never altering the STORED bytes — the stored/served distinction is the load-bearing one, since altering stored bytes would break the hash and the premise). | M2 - open · measure what the viewer executes before designing the fix |

| D-187 | design | 2026-08-04 | **A published case was built as exactly ONE inquiry, and nobody ever chose that — it was assumed by every item in the chain.** MEASURED against source when Bob asked for a fact check of his own definition: `store.mjs:3539` refuses verbatim *"publishing publishes ONE case: pass target=<inquiry id>"*; `published` is a state of an INQUIRY (`STATES.inquiry`); `published_bundles` is keyed `(bundle_id, edition)`; `index.mjs:3667` builds the container as `case: body.bundleId` with its own comment *"the parts listed here ARE the bundle's files"*; `MILESTONES.md:428` says *"a concluded inquiry is published"*. Second measured consequence: a published child *NAMES its parent and siblings, serving neither* (UI-18), so even under composition a stranger holding the zip cannot read the findings it points at without returning to the instance — defeating S9's read-without-our-cooperation premise. | D-199 | design | 2026-08-04 | **The `ai` credential class and its declared task scope — DESIGN AHEAD, recorded here by CONDUCT enacting DEC-55 (Bob, 2026-08-04), because nothing reaches the plane today and a scope kept only in the decision register reaches only the person who already knows it.** Whoever builds the assistant (DEC-27) builds to these five. (1) **Mint the `ai` class in `classify()` on the DEC-37 precedent**, with a DECLARED TASK SCOPE enforced at the gate in `scopeFor`'s shape. (2) **The task scope is declared IN THE RECORD, never in a settings row** — DEC-17's reasoning transplanted: a settings row *"would be a way to change the standard with nothing to read afterwards"*, and what an AI credential may reach is exactly the thing that must be amendable only as an authored, dated, on-the-record act. (3) **Minting an AI token is a MEMBER act, never an AI act.** If an agent can request a broader token, the scoping is theatre — the never-prefill pattern applied to capability rather than to text. (4) **The record names the token IDENTITY and the PRINCIPAL behind it** — an organisation-scoped key acts for the group with nobody individual behind it; a member-scoped key is attributable. Both are legitimate, they carry different accountability, so an act must say which; never the token's value, which `tokens.mjs` already denylists on publication. (5) **THE EXISTING MACHINE FENCES GENERALISE TO THE NEW CLASS BY CONSTRUCTION — AND ONLY BECAUSE OF REC-46, WHICH LANDED THE SAME DAY.** Until then `store.mjs` answered *"is this a machine"* for itself ELEVEN times in two hand-typed shapes while the catalog answered it a third way with a word list that knew nothing of the prefix, and REC-45 had measured the cost: the gate accepted `asserted_by: token:member`. REC-46 collapsed them into one predicate over the `token:<class>` stamp, so **a token stamping `token:ai` is caught by every `MACHINE_CANNOT_*` refusal automatically**. Asked a day earlier this would have meant editing eleven sites and probably missing one. **The negative control is therefore cheap and MANDATORY, and ships WITH the class rather than after it:** mint an `ai`-class credential and assert that `MACHINE_CANNOT_PUBLISH`, `_CONCLUDE`, `_RELEASE`, `_REOPEN`, `_CORRESPOND`, `_MOVE_ACTION` and D-151's resolve/forward refusals each fire BY NAME — and that removing the predicate makes them all pass. **SEQUENCE THE CHECK ROLE FIRST**: DEC-24 calls it *"the role most worth building"* because the threat model is self-directed overclaiming, and it is also the safest, since a role that only reads and raises needs the narrowest scope of the four (EXTRACT, PURSUE, FIND, CHECK). | M9 · open — **NO LONGER ONLY WITH THE ASSISTANT (updated 2026-08-05, DEC-60).** The `ai` class now has a SECOND consumer and it is the first to hold a mutating op: the INVESTIGATIVE SESSION (`INVESTIGATIVE-SESSION.md`), whose scope reads across a project and writes only suggestions through one endpoint (IS-3/IS-4). **All five determinations above hold unchanged and IS-4 extends the scope vocabulary rather than revising it** — (2) in particular is what makes an investigative scope declarable in the record rather than in a settings row, and (5)'s negative control now has a second class of fence to fire against. DEC-24's rule still stands over all of it, and DEC-60 is its fullest expression rather than an exception: the machine proposes — now including the reasoned object itself — and the member authors by accepting. Nothing is owed before the assistant (DEC-27) OR before IS-1. Appended 2026-08-07, RESOLVED later the same day (session BOB, under Bob's tactical delegation — `IS-SWEEP-2026-08-07.md` §4b item 7): **CHECK is the investigative session's first DEPLOYED MODE** — the session run with its stated objective against an existing conclusion IS the CHECK role, so the enacted CHECK-first instruction is satisfied by one build, not a separate prior one. Milestone token corrected M4→M9 2026-08-07 per MILESTONES.md's M9 placement note (finding F8) |

| D-211 | defect | 2026-08-05 | **DIST's gate step 8 says `op=audit` clean before anything is called done, and on a record past 200 documents a clean FIRST PAGE satisfies that wording.** MEASURED 2026-08-05 by CONDUCT at REC-57's integration, establishing the consequence rather than filing the finding: in `auditPass()` the answered `ok` is the op's TRANSPORT verdict (it ran) while `checked` is **the size of one page** and `clean`/`withErrors` are counts **over that page** — REC-57's own comment at the site says it plainly: *"`checked` is the size of one page and `ok` is a verdict over it, so 'the audit is clean' and 'the first 200 are clean' published the same shape."* REC-57 made the two DISTINGUISHABLE (a non-null `cursor` means more, `total` is published, and `limit` now states the applied cap), so a careful reader can tell — **the remaining gap is the GATE's wording, which does not say to check them.** **D-200's own reading is UNAFFECTED and this was checked rather than assumed:** the live instance holds 31 documents, well under the 200 cap, so `cursor` was null and 31 checked / 21 clean / 10 with errors is a complete reading of the whole record. This is LATENT today and becomes real the day an instance passes 200 documents — which a group capturing an agenda packet a week reaches inside a year. NEEDS: `kickoffs/DIST.md` step 8 to require `cursor: null` (or `checked === total`) alongside clean, so "the audit is clean" is a claim about the RECORD rather than about a page. **DIST's file, so this is a delegation and not a CONDUCT edit** — raised here because CONDUCT met it, and because the gate is what stands between a known-unclean record and a release. | M0 · open — **DIST's gate wording**; latent until an instance passes 200 documents, and the fix is one clause |
| D-213 | design | 2026-08-05 | **ANSWERED 2026-08-06 by Bob: discovered evidence not related to the current inquiry is CAPTURED and recorded as an ACTIONABLE NOTIFICATION the member can act on later.** The problem, raised by DEC-60: the investigative session reads ALL of a project's inquiries for context but writes only to the SUBJECT one, so evidence it turns up bearing on inquiry B — the same vendor holding three other contracts, met while investigating whether one was competitively bid — had nowhere to go and was dropped by construction. This is a new kind in an existing catalogue. **And it composes correctly with the capture rule**: the document is CAPTURED — entry to the cache, not to a leg of any claim — while the OBSERVATION becomes the notification, so nothing enters a claim without a member. **Still open beside it: D-194/D-184**, the same gap for a member's own leads, which this does not close and should now be easier to close by the same route. | M9 · answered 2026-08-06 (DEC-60, Bob) — **close condition CORRECTED 2026-08-07: the code does not use N-ids.** Verified: no N-number exists anywhere in source; the live vocabulary is SLUGS in `queuestate.mjs` (`QUEUE_FINDING_KINDS` :118-134), `classOfKind` is the fence, and the store refuses unknown kinds at the mint. The build is: one FINDING-class slug (FINDING, not CONDITION — a CONDITION is personally mutable, and one member could silently mute a real lead), a producer, a real `basis`, and `options[]` from the producer — **whose natural options are inquiry-grain acts that do not yet exist, the D-222 grain problem one surface over.** `case` derives from inquiry B's ancestors, not A's. Closes when the slug is registered and the session emits it. **CLOSED 2026-08-08 by PL-15** (worktree agent-aa5e6711b4eb2c064): the slug is `out-of-inquiry-lead`, registered in `queuestate.mjs`'s `QUEUE_FINDING_KINDS` and in `NOTIFICATIONS.md`'s catalogue together; the producer is `store.mjs #findingsOutOfInquiryLead`, walking `capture_requests` rows that carry the new nullable `lead_inquiry` column and reached through the EXISTING `op=queue` — no new op, because D-213's own answer is that this is a new kind in an existing catalogue. `case` derives from inquiry B's ancestors (`#queueAncestors([r.lead_inquiry])`), NOT inquiry A's, and an arm is pointed at exactly that. The basis names both questions apart (`found_while_working` / `bears_on`), carries both principals through PL-4's one composer, and reports `basis_entry` as a MEASUREMENT over both basis projections — three-valued (`undetermined` / `absent` / `present`) so a reader can tell *no document holds these bytes yet* from *looked for and part of no case* from *a reading carries it*. `options[]` is real (document-grain acts on inquiry B) with the inquiry-grain gap DECLARED on the item as `options_grain` rather than hidden behind an empty array — D-222's grain problem, still open, now visible on the member-facing object instead of being a silence. **Two things the close condition did not predict, both recorded rather than absorbed:** (1) the mint fenced only CONDITION kinds, so PL-15 SWEPT the class — every item's kind is now checked against the catalogue whatever its class, split into C-31.2 `NO_SUCH_KIND` and C-31.3 `KIND_MISCLASSED` because unknown is not the same as misfiled; and (2) the mint ran AFTER the personal mute loop, so a FINDING minted under a kind the catalogue files as a CONDITION — the one misfiling `queueMute`'s write fence accepts — could be muted away with the mint blind to it. The mint now runs first. Both found by RUNNING the control, not by reading. **D-194/D-184 remain open beside it** (the same gap for a member's OWN leads) and are unchanged by this |
| D-218 | design | 2026-08-05 | **UNMEASURED: whether a fleet-member Worker can hold a WHOLE investigative run inside the paid CPU ceiling.** Raised by DEC-60's integration architecture (`INVESTIGATIVE-SESSION.md` §14a), where Bob's Claude-account cascade (member, else project, else instance) settles that the agent loop runs in the GROUP'S OWN infrastructure — which preserves the installer's sovereignty promise and points at the fleet-member shape `pdf-worker` already established (I6). **THE REASONING THAT MUST NOT BE TRUSTED UNTIL MEASURED:** an agent loop spends most of its time WAITING on API responses, and Workers bill CPU rather than wall time, so a run of many minutes may sit well inside the ceiling. That is an argument, not a measurement, and this project has a standing rule against exactly this move — three claims the archive design rested on were wrong when finally measured, every one having sat in the design document for weeks. **WHAT IT DECIDES:** whether a fleet member can hold an entire run, or whether a run must be structured as many short invocations with its state carried between them, which is a materially different build. **RELATED MEASURED FACT:** Workers Paid was confirmed on this account 2026-08-04 (`MEASUREMENTS.md`), moving the CPU line from 10 ms to 30 s — so the question is not whether there is headroom but whether a whole run fits in it. NEEDS: a probe running a representative agent loop in a Worker and reporting CPU consumed against wall time, recorded in MEASUREMENTS.md before IS work that assumes either shape. | M9 · **MEASURED 2026-08-08 (FL-1) — the CPU question is ANSWERED and it is not the binding one.** `MEASUREMENTS.md`, FL-1 entry; instrument the Cloudflare GraphQL Analytics API (`workersInvocationsAdaptive.sum.cpuTimeUs`), read off a DEPLOYED probe, never the Workers own clock. **The reasoning this row said not to trust is CONFIRMED: 50.0 s of wall time waiting on subrequests cost 4.29 ms of billed CPU (~0.16 ms per awaited subrequest, independent of wait length).** A 25-turn run-shaped loop over REAL plane payloads costs 24.29 ms; 100 turns 189.28 ms; 400 turns 4,488.9 ms — CPU grows as ~n^1.9 (the transcript re-serialised every turn), putting ~1,100 turns inside the 30 s default and ~3,700 inside the 5-minute maximum (extrapolated from measured points, labelled as such). **BUT MEMORY BINDS FIRST BY AN ORDER OF MAGNITUDE: memoryUsageBytesP99 reaches 120.4 MB of the 128 MB isolate at 200 turns while CPU is still 2.5% of the ceiling.** So a fleet member CAN hold a long run as far as CPU is concerned, and CANNOT hold an unbounded one because of memory — which lands where §14a already put it: **the resumable shape is the one to build, and the number sizes the segment rather than deciding the shape.** OPEN only for FL-3 to consume; nothing further to measure here |
| D-219 | defect | 2026-08-06 | **A stored, member-facing method string says "with no captured basis" and reads as "with no basis", which is FALSE — the basis is REQUIRED.** Found 2026-08-06 while answering Bob's question *"What is testimony with no basis? Where did it come from then? Isn't there always context for how something got into the system?"* — and his instinct was right. `testifyResolution` (`store.mjs:8398`) REFUSES without one: `NO_BASIS` — *"grade D is recorded testimony: it carries the member's stated basis, with an author and a date"*. So a grade-D testimony always carries the member's stated reason, an author and a date. What it lacks is a CAPTURED DOCUMENT supporting the link, which is why it grades weakest. **But the method string written into the record reads** `testimony -- asserted by <who> with no captured basis (framework 8.1 grade D)` **, and a reader meeting "no captured basis" hears "no basis at all" — the record describing its own contents more weakly than they are.** This is the inverse of the usual defect class here (a comment claiming a stronger property than the code enforces); it is a stored string claiming a WEAKER one, and it is worse than a comment because it is member-facing and persisted on every grade-D resolution. NEEDS: the string to say what is actually missing — no captured document — while keeping the grade and the framework reference. Also worth a pass for siblings: any other place where "no basis" is used to mean "no captured basis". | M8 · open — one string plus its assertions; it rides whatever next touches the resolution path |
| D-220 | design | 2026-08-06 | **VERSIONS OF ONE DOCUMENT ARE ALREADY RECORDED AND NOTHING EXPOSES THEM, so the code that needs the version chain reconstructs it by full-text search and gets it wrong.** RULED by Bob 2026-08-06: *"Versions of the same document should be linked so that the system recognizes that reality. Each version should also be indexed by the same url (or id that refers back to the url.) These capabilities should be used in code that would benefit to know about this versioning reality."* **THE INDEX HE IS DESCRIBING ALREADY EXISTS** — verified in `schema.mjs` 2026-08-06: `captured_locators` is keyed `(address_norm, capture_sha, via)` with an index on `(address_norm, first_retrieved)`, carrying `first_retrieved`, `last_retrieved` and `observations`; `register` maps `capture_sha` to `bundle_id` and is indexed both ways. **So "every version at this address, in date order, with its bundle" is ONE INDEXED JOIN over two existing tables.** The versioning reality is recorded; no op answers it. **THEREFORE: DO NOT ADD AN EDGE BETWEEN VERSIONS.** An explicit `supersedes` relation would be a SECOND COPY of a fact the record already holds, which is the mirror-and-drift class this project refuses (D-164's solve-it-once; D-138's guard that guarded nothing). The link Bob asks for is the join, EXPOSED. **WHAT THE UNCHANGED CASE ALREADY DOES RIGHT, so it is not re-litigated:** a re-capture of identical content creates no bundle and no second register entry — it advances `last_retrieved` and increments `observations` on the existing row (Bob's own ruling, 2026-07-30, `LINK-FIDELITY.md`). What has no structural form is the CHANGED case, where a new bundle is created and its only tie to its predecessor is a SENTENCE OF PROSE in the body naming the earlier bundle id. **CODE THAT WOULD BENEFIT (Bob's third clause), in the order the benefit is largest:** (1) `heldMatch` in `civicos-ui/app.html`, which today searches `locator:"<url>"` and is the site of D-221; the join replaces the search and removes that defect by construction rather than patching it. (2) The **investigative session** (DEC-60): a run that reads sixty versions of one calendar as sixty documents has a distorted picture of what the record holds, which is the false-coverage hazard `STORE-AS-CACHE.md` names, arriving at the document level. (3) **Search result rendering** — sixty bundles at one address are sixty versions of one document, and a result list presenting them as sixty documents misrepresents the record. (4) **Monitoring** and (5) **`op=audit`'s cross-bundle duplicate sweep**, both of which D-60 records as comparing raw hashes; that line is partly stale (see D-60's correction) and the join gives either one its candidate set directly. (6) **A reader of a citation** — a case cites a specific bundle and that citation must never drift, but the LIVE record can show that later versions exist (a published case cannot be affected, and must not be). **RESEARCHED IN DEPTH 2026-08-06 (sub-session), and the fix is SMALLER than this row assumed — both halves already exist in code.** (i) **The read method exists and was deliberately left unwired:** `Store#capturedLocators({ addressNorm })` (`store.mjs:14779`, REC-26), whose own docstring says *"Deliberately not wired to a control-plane op."* **Its only caller anywhere in the repo is `test/monitor-cadence.test.mjs:415`** — in production nothing reads it. (ii) **The join already exists:** `#conditionBundlesForHost` (`store.mjs:9991`) already runs `captured_locators JOIN register ON r.capture_sha = cl.capture_sha`, scoped by GLOB to a host prefix and used to pick queue-condition subjects. So D-220 is *generalise an existing join and wire an existing method to an op* — not new machinery. (iii) **One write site, and it is unconditional:** `recordCapturedLocator` (`store.mjs:14747`), called only from `op=acquire` (`index.mjs:2938`) for every capture. New bytes at a known address INSERT a row; the same bytes seen again bump `observations` and widen the `MIN`/`MAX` interval — *"Seeing the same bytes again later is not a duplicate, it is the observation that proves the target held still in between."* `via` is in the key deliberately (D-96): an archive sighting of the same bytes is a different fact. Address normalisation is `normalizeAddress` (`subresources.mjs:520`) and is deliberately conservative — it does NOT strip trailing slashes or case-fold the path, because *"a normalisation MISS looks exactly like 'not captured'."* **CORRECTION TO THIS ROW'S OWN CONSUMER LIST:** consumer (5) named `op=audit`'s *cross-bundle duplicate sweep*. **No such sweep exists.** `auditPass` iterates bundles and calls `checkBundle` per bundle; `checkRegisterIntegrity` (`bio-checks.mjs:3536`) buckets within one bundle's `provenance.json` only. The phrase was inherited from D-60 and is a category error — there is nothing cross-bundle to move onto the join, which makes the join the thing that would CREATE that capability rather than improve it. **A CONSUMER THE ROW MISSED, and it is the largest one:** **MONITORING SELECTS PER BUNDLE, NEVER PER ADDRESS** (`store.mjs:15983` `#monitorCadencePlan`, `:16023` `#monitorCadenceTick`, keyed on `bundles.monitor_enabled/monitor_frequency/monitor_last_checked`). So sixty versions of one calendar are **sixty independent monitor subjects** — the same address fetched sixty times on sixty schedules. Monitoring an ADDRESS is what a member means; monitoring a bundle is what the code does. | M3 · open (Bob 2026-08-06) — design settled, no schema change, and the implementation is smaller than assumed: **generalise `#conditionBundlesForHost`'s join, wire `capturedLocators` to an op**, then consumers — (1) `heldMatch` (closes D-221), (2) monitoring per address, (3) the investigative session, (4) search rendering |
| D-222 | design | 2026-08-06 | **THE MEANING LAYER HAS NO QUERY SURFACE, and the investigative session is the first consumer that genuinely needs one — so this is a PRECONDITION of DEC-60, not an enhancement.** Raised 2026-08-06 by the rigorous architecture pass Bob asked for. **THE MEASUREMENT, from `STORE-AS-CACHE.md` and re-verified:** BIO has two retrieval routes. **Route 1**, the query compiler (`query.mjs`), projects **34 filterable fields and exactly 5 FTS columns** (`title`, `body`, `meta`, `locator`, `authority`). **Route 2**, the meaning tables — `readings`, `reading_refs`, `resolutions`, `connections`, `inquiry_basis` — is **not reachable by the query compiler at all.** **WHY THE HOLE IS INVISIBLE, which is worse than the hole:** route 1 carries SCALAR SUMMARIES of the meaning layer onto the bundle row (`capture` → `inquiry_capture_strength`, `legs` → `inquiry_basis_count`, `connection` → `inquiry_connection_strength`, `resolution` → `action_resolution`), so a caller can filter by a finding's STRENGTH and never reach the legs that produced it. `STORE-AS-CACHE.md`'s own words: *the projection creates a false sense of coverage — the meaning layer is visible as a number and unreachable as a structure.* **WHY IT BLOCKS THE SESSION:** forming a version of a claim's legs (DEC-60) is meaning-layer work end to end. As `INVESTIGATIVE-SESSION.md` §3 is written, the session's read scope is NOT ACHIEVABLE — it can see that a claim scores 0.7 and cannot see what it rests on, which is precisely the work it exists to do. **RELATED AND SAME-SHAPED: D-220**, where sixty versions of one document read as sixty documents is the identical false-coverage failure one axis over. **RESEARCHED IN DEPTH 2026-08-06 (sub-session), and the gap is wider than this row stated.** **(i) THIRTEEN TABLES, and about TWENTY-FOUR ops read them — NONE of which is a query surface.** Every one is a fixed-shape lookup keyed on exactly one of `capture_sha`, `ref`, `entity_id`, `relation_id`, `bundle_id`, `target_id` or `(progression_key, entity_id)`, or an unkeyed whole-table walk with a fixed output shape. **No predicate, no projection choice, no sort, no paging** (the one exception is `documentsNamingEntity`'s clamped limit). So the layer is not under-queried; it is UNQUERIED BY CONSTRUCTION. **(ii) GRADE IS A FILTER NOWHERE.** No op filters on grade at all, and **no grade column is indexed** — there is no `resolutions(grade)` or `connections(grade)`. The consequence with teeth: the C tier is explicitly *"FLAGGED for a member to confirm"* (`schema.mjs:876`) and **nothing can enumerate the flagged set** — `connectionsFor` refuses without a key (`store.mjs:8618`). A queue of work the system creates and cannot list. **(iii) NO CROSS-AXIS JOIN.** *"Every claim whose legs cite documents from authority X"* is two indexed columns and no join. **(iv) TIME IS A PREDICATE NOWHERE** — `at` is on nearly every table and filterable on none, so *"what resolved this week"* is unaskable. **(v) `readings.reading` IS AN OPAQUE JSON BLOB**, so nothing inside a reading is reachable except what `reading_refs`/`reading_ref_terms` already project out. **(vi) SEE D-223** — the sharpest single instance: hunch debt is publication-disqualifying and cannot be listed. NEEDS: a meaning-layer read surface. **Whether it extends the query compiler or is a second addressable surface is NOT a free choice — `query.mjs:701-705` states that a second query path is what the compiler's design exists to prevent, and D-15 gives visibility exactly ONE compilation point, enforced by a throw rather than a convention.** Options are being graded; D-223's question is acceptance on whichever wins. **Stage C DESIGNED 2026-09-14 (BOB #11): `docs/development/CONTENT-SEARCH-DESIGN.md` §4.2 — the `content:` and `passage:` arms and their row shapes on the one compiler; D-225's caps are its item 1, the arms items 2 and 5.** | M3 · open (DEC-60) — **BLOCKS the investigative session's read scope**; nothing else in the IS decomposition depends on it. Appended 2026-08-07: the options were GRADED 2026-08-06 in `INVESTIGATIVE-SESSION.md` §14c — recommendation D, staged as A then C; do not re-grade — **ROUTED 2026-09-14 by CONDUCT #11 at the BOB #11 drain: stage C (content-grain search) is DESIGNED in `CONTENT-SEARCH-DESIGN.md` and rowed as REC-90 (the `content:` arm) → REC-91 (the text index) → REC-92 (the `passage:` arm), behind M0-31's measurement; D-225's caps are REC-89.** |
| D-223 | defect | 2026-08-06 | **HUNCH DEBT CANNOT BE ENUMERATED, and the schema itself calls it the one declared bias that DISQUALIFIES PUBLICATION.** Found 2026-08-06 by the meaning-layer research sub-session. `inquiry_basis` (`schema.mjs:1221`) carries `grade_source`, and `schema.mjs:1187` names a `hunch` leg as publication-disqualifying. **No op can answer *"every leg whose `grade_source` is hunch"*, corpus-wide or per project.** It is checked only PER BUNDLE at the two gates (`checkInquiryBasis`), which means the rule is enforced one document at a time and **the outstanding debt across a project is unknowable to the group that carries it**. The same is true of `role`, `ground` and `grade_axis` — so *"every inquiry with a `cuts_against` leg"* and *"every multi-ground basis"* (REC-42's OR branches) are equally unaskable. This is a case of the record enforcing a rule it cannot report on, and it bears directly on doctrine: a group cannot see its own exposure before it publishes, only be refused at the moment it tries. Bob's own vocabulary correction (D-188, *say HUNCH DEBT*) presumes the debt is visible as a quantity; it is not. | D-224 | design | 2026-08-06 | **`connections` grows QUADRATICALLY in the documents concerning one entity, and nothing caps, comments on, or measures it.** Found 2026-08-06 by the meaning-layer research sub-session. `deriveConnections` (`store.mjs:8548`) collapses `resolutions` to distinct captures per entity and then emits **every unordered pair** — a nested loop at `store.mjs:8566-8568`, so **k documents concerning one entity produce k(k−1)/2 rows**. At 100 documents that is 4,950 rows for one entity; at 1,000 it is 499,500. **There is no cap, no comment acknowledging the growth, and no measurement anywhere in the repo.** Three things make it worse rather than academic: (i) a heavily-covered subject — the entity a group's whole project is about — is exactly where k is largest, so the cost lands on the most important entity rather than a marginal one; (ii) the derivation is idempotent and alarm-driven (`connection-derive`, batch 100 entities per tick), so the write amplification arrives as sustained background work on a Durable Object whose storage ceiling is itself unrecorded debt (D-190); (iii) `stats()` counts `connections` but NOT `readings`, `reading_refs`, `reading_ref_terms` or `inquiry_basis`, so an operator watching for this has one number and no denominator. NEEDS: a measurement first — derive against a synthetic entity at k = 10/100/1000 and record rows, bytes and tick duration in `MEASUREMENTS.md` — then a decision about whether pairs are materialised at all above some k, or derived on read like progression instance grades already are (`#assembleInstance`, which stores nothing). **Do not cap it before measuring; the point of the row is that nobody knows the curve.** | M3 · **MEASURED 2026-08-08 (REC-66) — the measurement this row asked for, taken before anything was capped, and it is in `MEASUREMENTS.md` with its instrument (`bio-plane/test/connections-growth.measure.mjs`, re-runnable).** The curve is EXACTLY k(k-1)/2 at every level driven and the cost per row is FLAT at ~798 bytes: k=100 -> 4,950 rows / 3.9 MB / 53-65 ms; k=200 -> 19,900 / 15.9 MB / 268 ms; k=500 -> 124,750 / 100 MB / 2.0 s; **k=1,000 -> 499,500 rows / 398 MB / 10.3 s inside ONE synchronous transaction — about 4% of the 10 GB per-object ceiling D-190 records as a vendor claim, for ONE subject.** **THE NUMBERS SAID SO, so the cap landed with them:** REC-66 bounds the DERIVATION (not the answer) at 500 pairs by default and 5,000 at the ceiling, with the DOCUMENT bound derived as the inverse of the quadratic (32 and 100 documents), both figures the plane's own `#MEANING_LIMIT_*` pair. **STILL OPEN, and this is now the whole of the row:** whether pairs are MATERIALISED at all above some k, or derived on read like progression instance grades already are. The bound makes the cost knowable, refusable and stated; it does not decide the model. Also unmeasured: the sweep's cost across many dirty entities in ONE tick, and the read side over a large connection table |

NEEDS: either the derivation's extent recorded where the read can see it (a per-entity stamp of the document bound the last derivation applied, which is one additive column and a write already in the path), or `op=connections` computing the same fact on read (a second scan, which is the cost this item exists to avoid).
