## Dispositions, added 2026-07-31

Every open row now carries a leading DISPOSITION token in its Status column: a
milestone from `MILESTONES.md`, or `DOCTRINE (Bob)`, `ACCEPTED`, `WATCH, no task`, or
`NOT OURS TO MEASURE`. The reason is that this file had become the de facto backlog
while `QUEUE.md` held six items, so forward work was invisible inside a ledger nobody
could sort. **A row with no disposition is invisible work** — which is how Bob's
ruling that reused parts must be re-fetched at ratification went two design revisions
with nothing scheduling it and no row recording it.

The token says where the work is placed, not that it is scheduled. Only `QUEUE.md`
schedules. A new row takes a disposition at the moment it is written.

Two ledger corrections this pass found, both stale rather than wrong when written:

- **D-48** said `open, S-12 step 6`. It was closed by 0.28.0 (`PLAN.md` S-12 item 6
  names it explicitly) and the residue is D-50, the catalog half.
- **D-53** said it blocks S-11 step 5. That block is STALE: bulk release shipped in
  0.34.0 under Bob's 2026-07-27 ruling, which decoupled the operation from trust
  inheritance entirely. D-53 remains open as doctrine and blocks nothing today.

| D-242 | gap | 2026-08-08 | **THE MINTED-ID LEDGER (M0-17) IS SCOPED TO ONE CLONE ON ONE MACHINE, AND NOTHING SAYS SO AT THE MOMENT IT STOPS BEING TRUE.** `tools/mintid.mjs` takes an id by an exclusive create under `git rev-parse --git-common-dir` — the ONE `.git` every worktree of this checkout shares. That is deliberately the exact scope the collisions have: **every colliding pair on 2026-08-08 was two worktrees of one clone**, which is why this is the right mechanism today rather than a network allocator nobody would run. **What it does NOT cover: two clones, a CI runner, a second machine, or a network filesystem whose `O_EXCL` is not atomic (NFSv2 was not; APFS and ext4 are).** In any of those the ledgers are independent and two allocators can take the same number — back to the read-the-file convention, which is the graceful floor and not a silent failure, because the corpus floor still holds. **THE REAL COST IS NOT THE COLLISION, IT IS THE CONFIDENCE**: a worker that ran the tool believes its id is safe, and in a two-clone world it would be wrong for a reason no output mentions. NEEDS: either the tool DETECTS that its ledger is not the only one (there is no cheap local test for that, which is why it is a debt and not a fix), or the day workers run on more than one clone the allocator moves to something with a single writer — the plane's own `Store.allocId` already is one, under `ctx.storage.transactionSync`, and is the obvious destination. Relation: M0-17, D-238 (the stash defect, the same shared-common-gitdir fact from the painful side). **NARROWED 2026-08-08 (D-243's item), and the row's own sentence is what got tested: "there is no cheap local test for that" is TRUE of the two-clone half and was NOT true of the non-atomic-filesystem half.** `mint` now PROBES the real ledger directory before it trusts it — an exclusive create, a second exclusive create that must be refused `EEXIST`, and a re-read proving the first writer's bytes did not move — and **REFUSES `EXCL_NOT_HONOURED` rather than minting** if any of the three fails. That closes the NFSv2 line by measurement instead of accepting it on report. **The two-clone line is NOT closed and cannot be locally: nothing on this machine can see a second ledger.** What changed for it is that **the confidence is no longer silent** — every mint, every `--list` and every `--audit` now prints a SCOPE block naming what the take IS exclusive against (every worktree of this clone) and what it is NOT (any allocator on a different ledger), with a WARN when the ledger has been used from more than one host (shared storage, where a one-process probe cannot speak for cross-host atomicity) or when `BIO_IDALLOC_DIR` overrides the git-derived scope. **The decision, recorded: REFUSE when exclusivity is demonstrably absent, WARN and still mint when the scope is wider than what was tested, STATE always — because the happy path is where a false belief is formed.** **Still open:** two clones cannot be detected, and `--audit` section 2 reports an allocation above the ledger's high-water as a QUESTION precisely because a second ledger is one of its two explanations. | M0 · open — **WATCH, no task, and NARROWED rather than closed.** The trigger is unchanged and concrete: the first time a worker runs anywhere but a worktree of `/Users/sparky/ClaudeCodeBIO/bio`. |
| D-244 | gap | 2026-08-08 | **A JPEG PAGE THAT NEEDS TURNING CANNOT BE TURNED WITHOUT DESTROYING THE ONE THING THE PASS-THROUGH ROUTE IS FOR.** `pdf-worker/src/pagepixels.mjs` renders an image-only page by EXTRACTING its single embedded image (CPDF-12; DEC-42's observation verified at 24 of 24 image-only pages over 622 real Oakland pages). For `CCITTFaxDecode` the page's own `/Rotate` is applied exactly — a quarter turn lands a bit on a bit — and the result is `upright: true`. For `DCTDecode` the route hands on the publisher's own JPEG bytes UNTOUCHED, which is both the cheapest path and the strongest provenance position available (no transform of ours sits between the record and the pixels a reader checks a claim against); rotating it means decoding and re-encoding, which throws that away and is lossy besides. **MEASURED SIZE OF THE GAP: 3 of the 24 image-only pages are `DCTDecode` at `/Rotate 270`** (14 are `DCTDecode` at `/Rotate 0` and need nothing; 7 are `CCITTFaxDecode` at `/Rotate 270` and are handled). Those three come back `upright: false` with `rotate_deg` STATED — never silently sideways. **WHY IT MATTERS RATHER THAN BEING TIDINESS: a sideways page fed to an OCR engine does not fail, it INVENTS.** The same exhibit at `/Rotate 270`, un-rotated, scored **8.67% character accuracy with 355 MINTED DIGITS** in fluent confident prose and the engine announced nothing (MEASUREMENTS 2026-08-08); upright it scored 99.93% / 90-of-90 / 1. NEEDS one of: (a) a baseline JPEG decoder in the member (~600 lines, and it makes the route lossy — the honest cost is that the record then holds OUR re-encoding, so `text_source` would have to say so); (b) a LOSSLESS JPEG transform (a quarter turn is expressible as an MCU-block permutation for dimensions that are multiples of the MCU size, which these are not guaranteed to be); (c) the CONSUMER rotates, which is the cheapest and is what `upright: false` is for — CPDF-10 is the first consumer and this is a real input to its design. Relation: CPDF-12, CPDF-10, DEC-42. | M2 · open — **decide it in CPDF-10, where the consumer is.** Nothing is silently wrong today: the field says so. |

Stated as a decision in `kickoffs/CONTENT-PDF.md` with a provisional and its reversal cost. | D-250 | gap | 2026-08-08 | **THE RESIDUE REPORT'S TWO STATED BLIND SPOTS, WRITTEN DOWN SO THEY ARE A KNOWN LIMIT RATHER THAN A LATER SURPRISE.** Raised by D-237's reporting half in the same turn as the fix, over its own instrument. **(1) THE STANDING SCAN RECOGNISES ONE ARTIFACT.** It finds workerd persistence by miniflare's OWN directory naming (`miniflare-<Object>`, the vendor's spelling, the one `battery.mjs` already counts sandboxes by) at any depth under any shared root. **An ordinary fixture tree with an ordinary name is invisible to it** — `migrate.test.mjs`'s `/tmp/civicos-fixture` would have matched nothing. Such a directory is seen only while it is ARRIVING (the before/after diff) or being HELD (the pid chain), so **a suite that leaked one on some PREVIOUS run and no longer touches it is invisible to every arm**, which is the same eight-day silence D-237 is about, one artifact over. It is not closed by widening the recogniser: the alternative is a census of `/tmp`, measured at **861 top-level entries and 236 MB** on this machine, and a report whose first rows are always other people's work is a report nobody reads. **(2) THE PID-CHAIN ARM IS A SAMPLER.** `lsof` is called at 60 ms and 700 ms into each suite (17 ms scoped to node+workerd, against 118 ms unscoped — the cadence is a cost decision, measured). Suite durations run 32 ms to ~1.6 s with a MEDIAN OF 505 ms, so **10 of 133 suites finish before the first sample can be taken and the only conclusive arm never covers them** — and a file opened and closed between two samples is missed in any suite. **NEITHER IS SILENT, WHICH IS WHY THIS IS A GAP AND NOT A DEFECT:** the report prints `HELD arm: N lsof sample(s) covering M of K suite(s)` on every run and names the shortfall, so *we looked and found none* can never read as *we never looked*; both limits are stated in `scripts/residue.mjs`'s header; and the suite PINS them (arm (e) asserts the write-then-clean case produces nothing, arm (j) asserts the sampler actually sampled). WHAT WOULD CLOSE (1): a marker this estate's own sandboxes carry, so a ground is recognised by something we mint rather than by a name a vendor could rename. WHAT WOULD CLOSE (2): an in-process `fs` hook preloaded into every suite, which is conclusive and cheap but changes what every suite runs, and workerd writes its persistence from a separate binary that no node hook can see. | D-276 | defect | 2026-08-09 | **`agent-worker` ASKS `op=meaningrows` FOR AN ARM THAT DOES NOT EXIST, AND TURNS THE PLANES REFUSAL INTO A CONFIDENT ZERO.** Found by D-258 while measuring who actually consumes that op, and **DRIVEN through the real op rather than read**: `agent-worker/src/index.mjs` passes `rows: "legs"` at all three call sites (the `meaningRead` default at :453, `collect` at :543, `compose` at :562), and the compilers arms are `leg`, `resolves`, `concerns`. (2) **exhaustion is REACHABLE — `EADDRNOTAVAIL` at 14,459 simultaneous loopback connections**, so the range and not the file-descriptor limit is the binding constraint, and the probe says which bit rather than assuming; (3) **TIME_WAIT CONSUMES that pool — 0 of 400 TIME_WAIT ports were re-handed-out while 3,000 fresh ones were allocated**, and the arm carries an over-strictness check that the allocator was LIVE (3,000 fresh, 3,000 outside the minted set) so that "withheld" cannot be reported by a probe that simply allocated nothing. `net.inet.tcp.msl` is 15000 ms, so a port is held out for **30 s** after use. **THE CONSUMPTION TRACKS THE BATTERY COUNT:** peak TIME_WAIT was **6,895 (42.1% of the whole range) with 4 concurrent batteries** and **6,565 (40.1%) with 3**, falling to 458–2,955 (2.8%–18%) when none ran. **131 of 151 suites construct a Miniflare and each binds; the entry socket belongs to workerd, a CHILD process.** WHY IT MATTERS HERE SPECIFICALLY: ~100 worktrees of one clone share one machine and one kernel, and the stated ceiling is eight concurrent batteries — this is a MACHINE-WIDE resource, so it is the one hazard a per-worktree sandbox (D-186) structurally cannot fence. **WHAT IS NOT ESTABLISHED, AND IT IS THE HEADLINE:** extrapolating the 4-battery figure to 8 lands at **119%–174% of the range**, i.e. exhaustion — **but EIGHT BATTERIES WERE NEVER RUN AT ONCE, so this is NARROWED, NOT PROVEN, and an extrapolation is not a measurement.** The confirming experiment was **deliberately not run**: the `headroom` ramp exhausts the range machine-wide and would have inflicted `EADDRNOTAVAIL` on the four other worktrees' batteries measurably running at the time, corrupting their baselines. **The instrument IS the hazard, which is corroboration of a kind and not proof.** RELATION TO THE UNEXPLAINED FAILURES: other sessions have hit `SocketError: other side closed`, `ECONNREFUSED` and a suite hanging past 300 s, each attributed to "load" without investigation. **This row does NOT claim those were port exhaustion** — no failure was captured with a socket-level errno beside it, and saying otherwise would be inventing an attribution. It claims only that a mechanism which would produce exactly those three signatures is measurably present and unmeasured. WHAT CLOSING IT TAKES: a sampler in `scripts/battery.mjs` recording peak TIME_WAIT and the concurrent battery count per run (report-only, like `residue.mjs`), so the correlation stops being anecdotal; and one honest 8-battery run on a quiet machine to settle the extrapolation. Relation: D-249 (the pinned-port half, closed empty), D-237, D-186, D-231. | M0 · OPEN — the mechanism is measured and real; its reach at the stated concurrency ceiling is NARROWED and not established, and that is stated rather than rounded up. |

FOUR live exposures, in THREE shapes: never-pushed, PUSHED-THEN-BEHIND, and committed-nothing-with-11-files-live.** The middle one is new: item 1 protects a worker that pushes ONCE at the end, and REC-100 and M0-40 each pushed in the MIDDLE and committed after, stranding a *"release the claim"* commit — **so the loss would have been the DURABLE REPORT itself.** **AND THE HEADLINE: M0-48, THIS ROW'S OWN ITEM 2, WAS ITSELF A STRANDED WORKER BRANCH AT THE MOMENT OF THE SWEEP** — two commits on no remote, one of them titled *plancheck WARNS on a local worker branch whose commits reach nobody*. Nothing reported it; it was found by looking, which is the argument that put the item in the cohort. **Item 2's arm therefore needs THREE words and not one warning** — the reader's next act differs per shape (push it · push it · ask the worker to COMMIT, which a push cannot do) — **and it must not name a FRESH worktree**, whose tip sits at `origin/main` with nothing committed and no exposure; an arm that cries about a healthy state is tuned out within a day. | D-291 | gap | 2026-08-10 | **TWO MEMBER-FACING SETS OF DECISIONS CAN BE REACHED ONLY ONE ITEM AT A TIME, AND THE PLANE IS WHAT STOPS THE OTHER MODE.** Found 2026-08-10 by UI-55 sweeping the member-facing flows for DEC-69's four shapes. **DEC-69 as amended, Bob, same day: *"they shouldn't be forced to make decisions in bulk. But they should be enabled to when appropriate"* — the operative word is FORCED and it cuts both ways**, so a set a member can reach only by clicking N times is the flaw exactly as a bulk-only control is. **THE TWO SITES, with the op that bounds each:** (a) **`loadResolveCandidates` / `resolveGo` (`op=resolve`)** — the subject-candidate list maps N documents to N identical `resolvePick` buttons, the list is bounded at 500 and is routinely long, and the commit sends ONE `captureSha`; `Store#resolveReferences({ captureSha, ref })` takes one document (it IS plural WITHIN that document — omit `ref` and every reference resolves — so the member's unit of decision is the document, and that is the unit with no bulk path). (b) **`queueEntryControlsHtml` (`op=proposedispose`, `op=taskresolve`, `op=taskforward`)** — every queue FINDING draws Adopt / Defer / Dismiss and every OBLIGATION draws Forward / Mark resolved; `Store#proposeDispose` is keyed on ONE (`progression_key`, `stage_key`) and the two task ops take one `id` each, and `TASKS_BUSY` serialises them besides. The queue's own header already names selection scoping as an unbuilt follow-on and correctly refuses to stub a control for it. **WHY THIS IS NOT FIXABLE IN THE SURFACE, and the reason is doctrine rather than effort:** a bulk path built as a client-side loop is **N motions over N items**, which is the forty-dialogs shape wearing a bulk control's clothes — DEC-52's own words, and `aiConnectionsReviewMotion` states the same rule one screen over and composes ONE motion instead. So the bulk path is **the plane accepting a set**, which is an interface change on I3 and not a UI edit. **CONTRAST, MEASURED, because it is what makes the two above a gap rather than a policy:** `op=release` takes a selection HANDLE and `op=queuemute` takes a `kinds` SET, and both of those surfaces offer the member either mode — the mute was bulk-only until UI-55 corrected it in place, which cost one parameter precisely because the op already accepted a subset. **WHAT CLOSING IT TAKES:** a decision on shape first (a set-accepting parameter on each op, or one selection-handle path the way `op=release` already works — the second is the established pattern and would unify three ops), then the plane change through `INTERFACE-CHANGES.md`, then the surface control. **NOT SILENT MEANWHILE:** `civicos-ui/test/member-respect.test.mjs` ARM 4c carries both rows with their reasons and prints them every run, and **ARM 4d RE-MEASURES each carried op's signature against `store.mjs` on every run, so the day an op learns to accept a set the arm goes RED** and the mode gets built rather than the carry being inherited. Relation: DEC-69 (and its same-day amendment), DEC-52, DEC-47, I3. | M8 · OPEN — the two sites measured with their op signatures; the fix needs an I3 DECISION on shape before it needs code, and the carry is self-expiring rather than an exemption |
| D-292 | defect | 2026-08-10 | **EVERY SERVICE BINDING IN THE REPOSITORY NAMES A WORKER THAT DOES NOT EXIST, AND CLOUDFLARE REFUSES THE DEPLOY THAT NEEDS IT.** MEASURED by deploying the fleet: `pdf-worker` went up clean (it declares no service binding) and **`agent-worker` was REFUSED outright** — *"Service binding 'PLANE' references Worker 'bio-plane' which was not found"* (code 10143). So `agent-worker`'s `PLANE -> bio-plane` and the plane's own `SELF -> bio-plane` both name a worker that exists in no account. **THE FLEET IS NOT INSTALLABLE AS CONFIGURED, and nothing said so because nothing had ever tried** — both members carry suites and FL-2..FL-5, D-262 and D-276 were built against them, all inside test harnesses. **THIS CORRECTS D-202's STATED REMAINDER, which is mine and was wrong**: I wrote that `deploy.mjs` should DERIVE its bindings from `wrangler.jsonc`. Measured, that alone would make every plane deploy FAIL the same way, because the declared target does not resolve. The fix is derivation WITH THE INSTANCE SLUG SUBSTITUTED — a binding must point at the instance being installed, not at a name in a file. **That is DS-1's real content** (the installer wires the fleet to THIS instance) and it is why DS-2's version-authority row and DS-4's gated deploy could not have worked first. So the fleet's uninstallability was written down twice, by the item that built it, and enacted zero times. **That is the queue's own "a note is not an item" class** — the lesson recorded on 2026-08-05 and again on 2026-08-10 — and the Cloudflare refusal above is its first MECHANICAL proof: until something tried to deploy, two accurate notes and a live delegation produced exactly the same result as knowing nothing. **The corroborating evidence was also already live and nobody had read it as evidence:** the `civicos` worker's one binding is `service PLANE -> biosmoke7` — the only worker in the account wired to the plane points at the INSTANCE, not at `bio-plane`, which is the rule this row prescribes, already working in production since 2026-08-05. | M1 · **DEPLOY HALF CLOSED 2026-09-10, INSTALLER HALF IS D-297** — `tools/deploy-fleet.mjs` templates every service target from the instance slug and `agent-worker` IS NOW DEPLOYED: the account read back `service PLANE -> biosmoke7`, and it serves `{"ok":true,"name":"agent-worker","version":"0.1.0"}`. **THE FLEET IS FULLY DEPLOYED FOR THE FIRST TIME** — agent-worker, biosmoke7, civicos, newgroup, pdf-worker. The tracked configs still say `bio-plane` and that is CORRECT: the phantom is substituted at deploy, never written down. The refusal is now OURS and pre-flight — `BINDING_TARGET_MISSING` names the missing worker instead of Cloudflare's code 10143 |

So each of the two sessions has now silently taken the other's work, neither noticed at the time, and it was recorded in a COMMIT MESSAGE where no later session would look for it — which is the same "a note is not an item" class as D-292. | D-298 | defect | 2026-09-10 | **THE PLANE'S COMMITTED BUNDLE HAS NO FRESHNESS GUARD AND IS CURRENTLY STALE BY 114 COMMITS — SO A RELEASE ASSEMBLED TODAY WOULD SHIP TWO DIFFERENT VERSIONS OF THE SAME PLANE SOURCE.** Measured 2026-09-10 while scoping DS-1's release-format half. FL-9 gave the FLEET members a build guard (`bio-plane/test/fleetbundles.test.mjs`: a stale artifact FAILS instead of shipping) and both members are covered. **The plane itself is not.** `bio-plane/dist/bio-plane.bundled.mjs` is tracked, is byte-identical to the released 0.56.0 asset (`da67aa4d…`, cut 2026-08-05), and `bio-plane/src` has had **114 commits and +19,027 lines** since — `store.mjs` alone gained 11,796. Nothing fails: `bio-plane/test/bundle.test.mjs` loads that artifact under miniflare and livefires it, which proves the artifact WORKS and says nothing about whether it matches `src`. **So the battery is simultaneously green on today's source and on a month-old build of it, and cannot tell you they differ.** THE CONSEQUENCE THAT MAKES THIS DS-1's PROBLEM RATHER THAN A TIDINESS ONE, and it is measured: FL-9 found that **three of `pdf-worker`'s six build inputs live in `bio-plane/src/`** (`cpu.mjs`, `pdfstructure.mjs`, `subresources.mjs`), and all three hash EQUAL to the current tree — the member bundles are fresh against TODAY's plane source while the released plane asset is a month behind it. Adding a `fleet` array to `RELEASE.json` right now would therefore publish, inside ONE signed release, `pdfstructure.mjs` as of today (inside `pdf-worker`) and as of 2026-08-05 (inside the plane). A release that is internally inconsistent about its own source is the record claiming more than it can support. NEEDS: (1) the plane gets FL-9's guard — the same input-hash manifest and byte arm, which is a straight extension of `fleetbundles.test.mjs` and NOT a new design, since FL-9 already measured why byte-identity alone is insufficient (esbuild tree-shakes an unused export out of a non-entry module, so a real committed-source change passed a byte check) and why it is cwd-sensitive (identical source built from two directories differed by 30 bytes); (2) the release assembler REFUSES to assemble unless every asset — plane and members — derives from ONE tree. **The cut itself is a separate gated act**: making the release coherent means cutting a new plane release from today's tree, which is 114 commits of unreleased plane work, a version bump, the baton, and a deploy — not a side effect of a format change. **INCIDENTALLY CHECKED AND CLEAN, recorded so nobody re-raises it as an alarm:** `bio-plane/dist/SECRETS.txt` is tracked and holds ADMIN_TOKEN / MEMBER_TOKEN / PROBE_TOKEN since 2026-07-24, and all three values hash into `tokens.mjs`'s denylist, which treats a published value as NOT SET. The mitigation this project designed for exactly this worked; the file is inert, not a live credential. | M1 · open — **DIST's (2), and (1) is FL-9's guard extended to the plane**; blocks a COHERENT fleet release, does not block the format or the installer half |
| D-300 | defect | 2026-09-10 | **A TEST ESTATE THAT READS THE WALL CLOCK CAN GO RED WITHOUT ANYONE TOUCHING IT, AND THE CENSUS THAT SAYS IT WON'T IS A FLOOR RATHER THAN A CLEARANCE.** Opened by M0-22, which fixed the one LIVE instance: `action-loop.test.mjs` pinned `DUE = "2026-09-10"`, the calendar reached it, and C-11.1 began reporting every action fixture in the file silently past-due — **73 pass / 6 FAIL on a tree nobody had touched**. The shape of that failure is the reason for this row: **CONDUCT measured `168/168 · 10,351` that morning and a later worker measured `167/168 · 10,345` on the SAME COMMIT, and both were honest.** A figure falsified by TIME is indistinguishable from one falsified by a CHANGE, so re-measurement — this project's standing defence against stale numbers — cannot detect it, and points the wrong way exactly as the missing-`node_modules` trap does. **WHAT WAS MEASURED, DRIVEN RATHER THAN GREPPED.** `bio-plane/test/clockshift.preload.mjs` advances node's `Date.now()`/`new Date()` and is inherited by spawned children (proved by probe, not assumed: child 2027, parent 2026). **215 suites were run under an advanced clock — 168 battery suites at +1, +5 and +20 years, plus the 47 `civicos-ui` harness suites at +1 year, which the battery never runs.** Result: **ZERO further live instances at any horizon.** Four suites (`governor`, `scheduler`, `selection`, `task-drain-alarm`) fail under any node-side shift and were **cleared by measurement, not by judgement — they fail identically at +1 HOUR**, a shift that cannot overtake a calendar date; they compare a node-computed now to a workerd-written stamp, so they are sensitive to the instrument's own node/workerd skew. **WHY THE ZERO IS A FLOOR AND MUST NEVER BE QUOTED AS A CLEARANCE, stated as the instrument's limits:** (1) **it does not reach workerd at all** — miniflare runs the plane in a child process whose clock is the true wall, so `store.mjs`'s `#nowMs()` fallback is invisible to it, and **only 3 of 260 `bio-plane/test` files bind `BIO_NOW_MS`** (`overdue-successor`, `capture-progressions`, and now `action-loop`) while **only 5 inject `nowMs` into `checkBundle`** — the catalog holds five `ctx.nowMs ?? Date.now()` sites (reeval age, two monitor-staleness, package age, C-11.1 past-due) and 255 files consult them through the wall; (2) a fixture dated beyond +20 years is not reached; (3) `newgroup/test` is DIST's and was not run; (4) **the instrument sees FAILURES, not FINDINGS** — an assertion shaped as `.some(...)` over a message list absorbs a new finding without failing, so a suite can acquire the defect and stay green under this sweep. **WHAT CLOSING IT TAKES:** bind `BIO_NOW_MS` in every suite that constructs a Miniflare and thread `nowMs` into every `checkBundle` call in a suite — mechanical, ~160 sites, and it makes the class unrepresentable estate-wide the way M0-22 made it unrepresentable in one file. The cheaper partial: a battery arm that runs the clock-advanced sweep on a cadence so a latent fixture is found by the instrument rather than by the calendar. **Do NOT close it by moving dates further out** — M0-22 measured that fix directly and it is green today and RED at +1 year, reproducing the original signature exactly, with a viable window of NINE DAYS before it needs doing again. Relation: C-11.1, REC-8's injectable clock, REC-24. | M0 · open — **the one live instance is CLOSED by M0-22**; this row is the estate-wide residue, its instrument (`node bio-plane/test/clockadvance.control.mjs`) and its stated blind spots |

So the anchor page carries true character/digit ACCURACY and every other page carries AGREEMENT WITH LOCAL TESSERACT reading the same crop, which is a different claim wearing similar digits: where both engines are wrong the same way, the agreement figure reads high and nothing notices. CPDF-14 keeps the two apart by naming its columns differently (`agree w/ floor`, `digits DIVERGING from the floor`) and never mixing them, which is the right handling of the gap and not a substitute for closing it. WHAT IT COSTS: every future engine measurement — CPDF-10's eventual external tier, any CPDF-13 calibration re-probe, any tesseract fleet-member score — is either n=1 on accuracy or n>1 on agreement, and a GO/NO-GO that needs both at once cannot be had. WHAT CLOSING IT TAKES: hand-transcribed ground truth for a handful more image-only pages, adjudicated at zoom the way the first one was, committed beside `GT_PAGE2` and read by the same guard. Roughly an hour per page and it is the only thing that buys corpus-scale ACCURACY. | ACCEPTED · open — RAISED by CPDF-14, which handled it by naming its columns apart (accuracy vs agreement-with-the-floor) and stating it beside every figure it affects rather than smoothing it. Closing it is hand-transcribed ground truth for more pages; nothing else buys corpus-scale ACCURACY. |
| D-311 | gap | 2026-09-10 | **THE SEVEN ROSTER ACTS STAY `NON_ACTS` AND THE DECISION IS MADE HERE RATHER THAN DEFERRED AGAIN — but the reason their row has carried since REC-19 (*"folding them into affordances is a later item"*) was a NOTE and not an item, so this row is the item.** `affordances.mjs` lists `projectinvite`, `projectjoin`, `projectleave`, `projectremove`, `projectowneradd`, `projectownerremove`, `projectownerrescue` as *"participation: roster act, position-enforced by the store"* — seven acts on a PROJECT bundle that `op=affordances` publishes nothing about. **D-310 gated `publish` on the caller's position and DID NOT move these, on an argument stated at their own table**: (1) `publish` was already IN `ACTS` with an INCOMPLETE derivation — an act offered that the store refuses is an OVERCLAIM and this record ranks that above a missing feature; these seven are offered at all by nothing, so no pre-flight is lying about them. (2) **They need a DIFFERENT fact.** D-310's `project_owner` answers *"does this viewer own SOME project"*, which is right for `publish` because the project is a PARAMETER of that act; for these seven the project IS the target, so their fact is `#isProjectOwner(target, viewer)` — the PAIR question `caseproduction.test.mjs` §3 already measures the store refusing (*"pilar owns one project and publishes as another she merely joined"*). Deriving them from D-310's fact would offer `projectinvite` on every project to anyone who owns any, which is that confusion shipped. (3) **They are not one position rule but seven**: `projectjoin` is the INVITEE's act and an invitee is not an owner; `projectleave` is a joined participant's; `projectremove` is an administrator's (Membership Architecture v2 7.7); `projectownerrescue` has its own condition. (4) Adding them is an ADDITION to the published act set — a separate I3 change with its own consumers to measure — where D-310 was a narrowing. **CARRIED HERE SO IT IS NOT LOST: the machine fence is the same class one rule over.** `publishCase()` refuses a machine credential `MACHINE_CANNOT_PUBLISH` (DEC-49, fires first) and `op=affordances` still offers `publish` to a `class:` credential — deliberately, because D-310's over-strictness arm requires a machine credential's answer to be BYTE-UNCHANGED and because closing it is an opposite behaviour change no consumer has been asked about. COST WHILE OPEN: narration only for the seven (no surface renders a roster control off `op=affordances`; the roster is published by `op=projectparticipants` and `op=projectownerarith`, which answer the position question directly), and for the machine fence a pre-flight that offers publication to a credential the store refuses by name — which matters when an automated worker is written against the act set rather than against the refusal. CLOSING IT TAKES: one per-pair fact on `#affordanceFacts` (`#isProjectOwner(target, viewer)`, the D-310 pattern), seven `applies` predicates derived one at a time from the refusal each op actually raises, an IC row for the ADDITION, and a decision on whether a machine credential's act set narrows at all. | ACCEPTED · open — **the seven are DECIDED (they stay), and what is open is folding them in**; no deadline, and explicitly NOT a blocker for DEC-33 the way D-310 was |
| D-312 | gap | 2026-09-10 | **THE PLATFORM'S `memoryUsageBytes` IS NOT A FRACTION OF THE 128 MB ISOLATE, AND EVERY DOCUMENT IN THIS REPOSITORY THAT SAYS "AGAINST A 128 MB ISOLATE" INVITES THE WRONG ARITHMETIC.** MEASURED 2026-09-10 by CPDF-15 on a deployed scratch Worker running wasm tesseract, read off `workersInvocationsAdaptive`: invocations the platform itself marks `success` report **132.4–240.2 MB**, and the invocation it marks `exceededMemory` reports **278.7 MB**. A metric that reads 240 MB on a SUCCESSFUL invocation cannot be the isolate's 128 MB budget, so **no percentage of 128 may be computed from it** and "mem P99 140.9 MB" does not mean "110% of the ceiling". What the surface CAN do is locate a ceiling by refusal, and CPDF-15 did: the RGBA frame is the transferable number — **61.3 MB completes, 75.7 MB is killed**, with a third shape at 134.6 MB where the allocation is refused in-isolate as a catchable `RangeError` instead. RELATION: DEC-42's entry and D-245 both state the 33.6 MB-frame-against-128 MB framing, which was the right worry and the wrong unit; FL-1 (`fl1-cpu-probe.mjs`) reads the same surface and its `memoryUsageBytesP50` figures (9.8 MB at 25 turns, 51.2 MB at 100) carry the same caveat unstated. WHAT CLOSING IT TAKES: one sentence at each site saying the metric's units are the platform's and not the isolate's, and a rule that a memory claim is expressed as the WORKLOAD SIZE that survives rather than as a share of 128. | M2 · open — RAISED by CPDF-15, which handled it by reporting the frame size beside every memory figure and never dividing by 128. Closing it is a wording fix at three sites; nothing is broken, but a reader doing the obvious division gets a wrong answer. |

| D-320 | gap | 2026-09-12 | **THE PASS-THROUGH JPEG ROUTE CANNOT BE TRANSCRIBED IN-ISOLATE, SO THE ONE ROUTE WITH THE STRONGEST PROVENANCE IS THE ONE THE OCR MEMBER MUST DECLINE.** MEASURED 2026-09-12 by CPDF-10. CPDF-12's renderer has four decoded routes and one pass-through: `passthrough-dct` hands back the publisher's OWN JPEG bytes untouched, which is deliberately the strongest position the record can hold — no transform of ours sits between the record and the pixels a reader checks a claim against. But `tesseract-wasm` needs RGBA samples (`loadImage` refuses anything shorter than width*height*4, CPDF-15 measured that refusal), workerd has neither a canvas nor `createImageBitmap`, and no JPEG decoder is built. So `ocr-worker` REFUSES that route by name (`PIXELS_UNREADABLE`, with the route and the media type in the refusal) and the page stays honestly unread. The decoded routes — `decoded-ccitt-g4`, `raw-samples-1bit`, and the 8-bit ones — go through this member's own PNG reader and transcribe normally. WHAT THIS COSTS, AND IT IS UNMEASURED: nobody has counted what share of the image-only class is DCT versus CCITT. CPDF-12's corpus probe measured the routes and that figure is in `MEASUREMENTS.md`; it has never been read as "what fraction of scans this member can serve", which is the question this row asks. WHAT CLOSING IT TAKES: either a baseline JPEG decoder inside the member (a real capability, ~700 lines for baseline DCT, and it would have to be measured against an independent decoder the way the CCITT decoder was — `pagepixels.test.mjs`'s Pillow-derived digests are the model), or the escalation tier DEC-74 leaves open with Bob. THE ATTRIBUTIONS, each with its commit: (1) the probe's two bar arms and arm E(6) — `ce2fe34` (CASE-2, DEC-72/IC-65) removed the cross-citer composition and `#requiredStrengthFor` with it, so `op=strengthbarof&target=` now refuses by name and the E(6) anchor matched nothing, silently handing `slice(-1401,-1)` the last 1,400 bytes of the file; corrected to the per-project shape that replaced it, and re-aimed rather than re-anchored because **the replacement site QUOTES the removed rule verbatim inside the table explaining that it is gone** — re-anchoring the old assertion there would have gone green over prose asserting the opposite. | D-344 | defect | 2026-09-14 | **THE CONTROL REGISTER READS `NEGATIVE CONTROL` ONLY WHEN THE PHRASE IS FOLLOWED IMMEDIATELY BY A COLON OR DASH, AND A PARENTHETICAL BEFORE THE COLON SILENTLY SCORES THE SUITE AS DECLARING NO CONTROL.** Found by COFF-9 (2026-09-14): `bio-plane/scripts/control-register.mjs` matches `NEGATIVE CONTROL` followed by `:`, `—`, `–` or `-`; writing `NEGATIVE CONTROL (four arms): …` made both declarations invisible, `coverage.mjs --strict` exited 1 and `owed-controls.test.mjs` went red with nothing anywhere naming punctuation as the cause — the SECOND instance of D-233's class in that register's own history. COSTS: a worker loses time to a red whose cause is unnamed, and a suite with real controls can be scored as having none. CLOSING IT TAKES: either the detector tolerates a bracketed qualifier before the separator (with its own arm proving a bare `NEGATIVE CONTROL` with no separator still FAILS), or the failure message NAMES the punctuation rule — one M0 item, the choice priced at the site. **INSTANCE FOUND 2026-09-14 at M0-28's landing (its chat report, relayed by CONDUCT #10; recorded by CONDUCT #11):** `bio-plane/test/corpuscheck.test.mjs` carries a SECOND declaration at its line 29 spelled `NEGATIVE CONTROL, THE OTHER DIRECTION —` — a COMMA before the dash — so the register reads only the first block (line 13) and the arms the second block states are invisible to it; latent (the suite still counts as declaring one), and it is the class this row names, not a new one. Corrected when this row closes, by the grammar fix rather than by re-spelling one file. | M0 · open — one M0 item; interim law: the marker is followed immediately by `:` |
| D-346 | gap | 2026-09-14 | **THE THREE OPENDOCUMENT ENTRIES EMIT NO `core-properties` ITEM AND NO `intra` LINK, SO THE SAME I2 SHAPE CARRIES LESS EVIDENCE FROM AN ODF EXPORT THAN FROM ITS OOXML EQUIVALENT — AN ASYMMETRY BETWEEN TWO FORMATS A CONSUMER IS TOLD ARE INTERCHANGEABLE.** Opened by COFF-10 (2026-09-14) about its own scope, not found in someone else's: the item reads ONE `content.xml` per the row, and OpenDocument keeps the core properties (creator, title, created/modified, revision) in `meta.xml` and embedded objects and images in separate members listed by `META-INF/manifest.xml`. `bio-plane/src/docx.mjs`, `formats-xlsx.mjs` and `pptx.mjs` all emit a `core-properties` item from `docProps/core.xml` and content-address their embeddings into the `intra` partition; `bio-plane/src/odf.mjs` emits neither. **THIS IS NOT A SILENT GAP AND THAT IS WHY IT IS A `gap` AND NOT A `defect`:** both absences are carried on every successful read as `{part, why:"outside_content_xml_not_read", detail}` in `evidentiary.undetermined`, and the same fact is repeated in `notes[]`, so a consumer is told `intra: 0` means NOT LOOKED rather than NONE PRESENT — the suite drives it against a fixture whose `meta.xml` really does carry a creator the entries do not surface. COSTS: Part II §16 says OpenDocument and OOXML "preserve the same evidence", and from these entries they do not — FRAMEWORK gets no producer metadata and no content-addressed embedding from a Drive export, which is precisely the harvest path Bob's 2026-09-14 ruling makes the normal one, so the shortfall lands on the common case rather than an edge. A reader who trusts the envelope without reading its `undetermined` list draws a weaker conclusion than the bytes support (the safe direction, but still wrong). CLOSING IT TAKES: two reads already available at the same seam — `meta.xml` through `readPart` into the existing `core-properties` kind (ODF's `<office:meta>` carries `dc:creator`, `dc:title`, `dc:date`, `meta:creation-date`, `meta:editing-cycles`, which map onto the kind's existing fields with `lastModifiedBy` honestly null), and a `META-INF/manifest.xml` walk emitting `intra` links content-addressed by sha256 exactly as the OOXML entries do — plus DELETING the two markers in the same turn, because a marker left standing over a gap that closed is the stale-claim defect this project meets most. The negative control is the one already written: the fixture's planted creator must then APPEAR, and a package with no `meta.xml` must still state an absence rather than emit an empty item. | M2 · open — one item on CONTENT-OFFICE's own ground, sized at roughly COFF-10's own `odfParts` plus one manifest walk; CAP-8 does not depend on it (an export is readable without it) but is the first consumer that will feel it |
| D-351 | gap | 2026-09-14 | **A GOOGLE DRIVE EXPORT IS NOT BYTE-STABLE, SO `capture_sha` — THE TRUST ROOT — DIFFERS ON EVERY RE-FETCH OF A DOCUMENT THAT DID NOT CHANGE, AND THREE MECHANISMS THAT KEY ON IT GO QUIET OR CRY WOLF.** MEASURED by CAP-8 (2026-09-14, MEASUREMENTS.md §4): three consecutive exports of one unchanged document, ~1.5 s apart, through the plane's egress, produced **three distinct `capture_sha` in three takes** for both `.odt` and `.ods`. Every export is Google's CONVERSION AT FETCH TIME and the conversion is not reproducible — the ZIP envelope differs every request in both kinds, and for `.odt` even `content.xml` differs by one character (per-request automatic-style names). For `.ods` the envelope alone moves and `content.xml` is byte-identical across all three, which is the shape of the fix. COSTS, all three in the direction of a record that says LESS than it could rather than more, which is the safe direction and is why this is a gap and not a defect: (1) **C-18.3's corroboration fold can never fire on a Drive document** — its RAW arm compares `capture_sha` and two captures of one Doc will never match, so the record holds two review items where there is one; (2) **the NORMALISED arm cannot rescue it either**, because `profile.digests.determined` requires the bytes to have been read AS TEXT with a CERTAIN stack and an ODF container is never read as text, so a Drive capture records `evidentiary: null` and the sweep correctly refuses to treat two nulls as equal; (3) **`resolveLinks`' identity BRACKET can never fire** — its strongest arm is one set of bytes observed on both sides of an interval hashing equal, which is unreachable here, so every Drive link falls to `undetermined` and a monitoring tick would report a CHANGE on every single re-fetch. CLOSING IT TAKES: an evidentiary digest for the office/ODF path that normalises over the CONTAINER rather than over decoded text — `content.xml` is the obvious substrate and the `.ods` measurement shows it is stable — which is an I1 §4c change (`digests.determined` currently gated on `profiled_from_text`) plus one producer in the acquire path; the `.odt` per-request style names need normalising out and that is a measurement before it is a build. | M2 · open — CAPTURE's, and NOT in CAP-8's scope (its row rules on recognition and the hop, not on dedup) and deliberately not smuggled in; sized as one item for CAPTURE once CAP-7's census says how much Drive material there actually is |
| D-353 | gap | 2026-09-14 | **D-333's DECAY MODE (c) IS MEASURED ACROSS THE ESTATE FOR THE FIRST TIME AND READS ZERO OPEN INSTANCES — AND NOTHING WILL EVER MEASURE IT AGAIN, WHICH IS THE GAP THIS ROW IS FOR.** Mode (c) is *"an arm whose SUBJECT stopped existing as a thing to compare against"*; D-333 closed without it, naming it rather than folding it in, because it is *"a judgement at attribution time, not a number"*. M0-29 measured the half that IS mechanical, over all **89 drivers** (85 by convention plus the same 4 off-convention files the census enumerates) and **408 path literals** held against the working tree and `git ls-tree -r HEAD` (683 tracked paths): **6 candidates, 0 unadjudicated**, each of the six checked BY HAND against `e9ba393` and recorded in a DATED ADJUDICATION TABLE inside the instrument, so the next reader inherits the judgement instead of re-deriving it. Three real mode-(c) instances DO exist in the estate and **all three are already retired with the loss stated at the site**, which is the handling D-330 established: `dec65-strength-reach.control.mjs` arm (5) (the other tree became the mainline), `d280-strengthbar.control.mjs` arms (A), (C) and (E) (*"RETIRED BECAUSE THEIR SUBJECT WAS DELETED"*), and `civicos-ui/test/case6.control.mjs`'s arm for `FINDING_IN_ANOTHER_CASE` (deleted by D-309 enacting DEC-72 clause 6, announced-and-skipped rather than removed). WHAT THE INSTRUMENT CAN AND CANNOT SEE, and the sentence is load-bearing: it sees a named subject that resolves NOWHERE under any package root or at HEAD, and prose that retires an arm in words; it CANNOT see an arm whose subject file still exists while the construct inside it is gone (mode (a) when the anchor dies, a judgement when it does not), an arm whose subject is a process, URL, account or credential rather than a path, a path built at runtime from a variable, or — because the resolver tries every package root to kill 11 false positives — a subject that MOVED between packages. WHAT IT COSTS WHILE OPEN: the figure is a SNAPSHOT of `e9ba393` and nothing re-takes it, so the estate can grow a mode-(c) arm tomorrow and nobody will know; this is exactly the D-330 condition (an instrument nothing runs) one level out from the census that closed D-333. WHAT CLOSING IT TAKES: decide whether `tools/modec-sweep.mjs` joins the periodic census as a third shape beside anchor-liveness and declared-vs-measured — it is read-only, ~3 s, and already carries a both-directions `--self-test` — or whether mode (c) stays a judgement somebody takes at attribution time, which is a defensible answer and must then be WRITTEN DOWN rather than left as silence. **NOT A DEFECT: nothing in the estate is wrong today.** RELATED: D-333 (the three modes), D-330 (the instrument nothing runs), D-343 (the tally half, closed by the same item). | M0 · open |
| D-355 | defect | 2026-09-14 | **TWO CONTROL DRIVERS ARE RED ON A GREEN `main` FOR REASONS OUTSIDE ANY OPEN ITEM, AND ONE OF THEM LEAKS A 4 MB PEN ON EVERY NON-ZERO EXIT.** Found by M0-29 (2026-09-14) while striking two of D-343's candidates, both reproduced on a PRISTINE `e9ba393` worktree: (1) `civicos-ui/test/refusal-partition.control.mjs` exits 1 with 2 of 18 sub-checks NOT AS DECLARED (arm 2/2b declared 1, actual 0) — D-333's mode (b), a declared pass/fail pair gone false while every anchor stays live — AND on that non-zero exit it leaves `.rec79-control-pristine/` (~4 MB) behind, measured twice, so every red run grows the untracked estate the battery's provenance line then names; (2) `bio-plane/test/provenance-floor.control.mjs` exits 1 at 55 of 58 as declared (arms 2a, 3 and 6-stage-1), mode (b) again. WHAT CLOSING IT TAKES: attribute each red arm to a behaviour move or a suite growth with the dated reason at the site (the D-343 precedent), never exempt; make the pristine pen's removal unconditional on exit (a `finally`, or the driver removes what it wrote by name — UI-59's driver is the precedent); teach the census `refselectivity`'s announcement shape or re-spell the one driver, and say which. | M0 · open — raised at M0-29's integration by CONDUCT #11 from the worker's act 4; owners of the drivers are REC-79's and M0-18's ground, both landed, so the fix is an M0 background-lane item when a slot-free worker is available |
| D-357 | defect | 2026-09-14 | **THE DEC-49 GUARD'S REGION MATCHER ENDS IN `\\b`, AND A HYPHEN SATISFIES A WORD BOUNDARY, SO ONE REGION'S OPENER COUNTS AS ANOTHER'S WHENEVER THE FIRST NAME IS A HYPHEN-PREFIX OF THE SECOND.** Found by CONDUCT #11 at FW-17's integration (2026-09-14): `civicos-ui/check-refusal-codes.mjs`'s `REGION_START(name)` is `/DEC-49 REGION\\s+(name)\\b/` — for REC-84's region `is-content-row` it matched FW-17's new region `is-content-row-present` (`\\b` holds between `w` and `-`), the guard reported *found 2 opening marker(s), expected exactly 1* and FAILED THE HARNESS on a correct tree; neither worker could have seen it (each branch had one of the two names), so it is the class of defect only the integrator meets. RESOLVED AT THE INSTANCE by renaming FW-17's region to `pair-content-row-present` at its three sites (two markers, one `where`). WHAT IT COSTS WHILE OPEN: any future region whose name extends an existing one by a hyphenated suffix fails the harness at merge time with a message that says DUPLICATE, and the rename is the only cure the message suggests. WHAT CLOSING IT TAKES: anchor the matcher to the WHOLE name (a lookahead for whitespace, `*/` or end of comment after the name, not `\\b`), with a negative control that plants a hyphen-extended sibling region and proves the guard counts ONE opener per name; `regionLines` floors unmoved. | M0 · open — the instrument's own defect; an M0 background-lane item when a slot-free worker is available; UI owns the file |
| D-352 | gap | 2026-09-14 | **A READING'S POSITION EXISTS ONLY WHERE THE CONTAINER ITEMISED ITS OWN TEXT, SO THE HTML PATH AND TWO OF IC-1's FOUR ARMS CARRY NONE — AND THE ABSENCE IS INDISTINGUISHABLE AT THE WIRE FROM A READER THAT DID NOT TRY.** Opened by FW-17 while landing IC-86, which makes `reading_refs` carry WHERE a reference was read. The mechanism is a segment map built at `docprofile/readtext.mjs`'s flatten, so it can only place a reference the PRODUCER itemised: `text.pages[]` gives IC-1's `pdf-page` arm and `text.paragraphs[]` gives `doc-para`, and that is the whole of it. THREE CONSEQUENCES, each a different gap and none of them a defect in this landing. (1) THE HTML PATH CARRIES NOTHING: a calendar reaches its reader through `op=acquire`'s own text read-back, which is one decoded string with no container structure, and IC-1's `dom` arm has no producer anywhere in the tree and is refused by name. Closing it needs CONTENT-HTML to produce `dom` AND an offset-preserving `unescapeHtml` — the calendar reader unescapes and then slices to the inside of `<main>`, displacing every offset by an amount that depends on how many entities preceded, so handing either coordinate to the locator would return a confidently WRONG page, which is worse than none. (2) `sheet-cell` AND `slide-shape` HAVE NO PER-CELL OR PER-SHAPE TEXT in the I2 text shape at all, so a reading over an xlsx or a pptx honestly answers null for every reference; the arms exist in the vocabulary and no producer fills them. (3) `rect` IS ALWAYS NULL on a reading's `pdf-page` position, because Tier-1 and Tier-2 text is a flat per-page string with no geometry — the page is in the bytes and the rectangle is not — which means a REGION-grained content extent can never be reached by a reading-derived connection, only a PAGE-grained one. WHAT IT COSTS: a portion leg on any non-PDF, non-DOCX document answers UNDETERMINED on the connection axis forever, and so does one on a region of a PDF. That is honest and it is also a ceiling nobody may mistake for a floor. WHAT CLOSING IT TAKES: for (1) CONTENT-HTML's `dom` producer plus an offset-preserving unescape; for (2) an ADDITIVE I2 change giving the office text shape a per-cell and per-shape itemisation, which is CONTENT-OFFICE's; for (3) positional text extraction, which I2's own Status section already names as a FUTURE doctype-driven ASK and not a gap. MITIGATED MEANWHILE, and this is why it is a gap rather than a defect: every one of these absences is STATED rather than silent — each reader declares in its own header whether it can say where and why not, the reading's basis carries the producer's own reason, and the column is NULL rather than defaulted to `document`. | M4 · open — three producers, three owners, and NONE of them is FRAMEWORK's to close: (1) CONTENT-HTML owes IC-1's `dom` producer and an offset-preserving `unescapeHtml`; (2) CONTENT-OFFICE owes a per-cell and per-shape itemisation of the I2 text shape, an ADDITIVE I2 change with its own IC; (3) positional PDF text is CONTENT-PDF's and I2's own Status section already names it a FUTURE doctype-driven ASK rather than a gap, so it is carried here for completeness and is not owed. Interim law, and it is what makes this a gap rather than a defect: a reading that cannot say where writes anyway with `pos_kind`/`pos`/`pos_ref` NULL, the reader declares in its own header why it cannot, and a portion leg over such a reading answers UNDETERMINED and STATED on the connection axis — never `document`, and never a silent nothing. |
| D-359 | gap | 2026-09-14 | **NO FORMAT ENTRY EMITS A SHEET'S ROW AND COLUMN EXTENT OR A SLIDE'S SHAPE COUNT, SO THE INNER HALF OF TWO OF THE THREE OFFICE ARMS' OUT-OF-RANGE REFUSAL STILL HAS NOTHING TO READ.** D-354's residue, found by CAP-12 while closing it, and MEASURED against all six entries' returns rather than assumed: `xlsxText`/`odsText` return `sheets: [{sheet, name, hidden, text, undetermined}]` — names and no dimensions; `pptxText`/`odpText` return `slides: [{slide, ref, part, hidden, text}]` — no shape count; `docxText`/`odtText` return `paragraphs[]`, whose LENGTH is the count, which is why the paragraph arm is fully fed and the other two are not. | D-412 | gap | 2026-09-17 | **THE ESTATE AUDITS EXPOSURE AND NOBODY AUDITS RESIDUE — a worktree can be perfectly healthy, correctly reported `0 EXPOSED`, and never reclaimed by anything.** Raised by CONDUCT #3 from REC-113, which built a pristine checkout in `/tmp` to measure a clean baseline — **correct practice, and the very run that caught the `npm ci` symlink trap** — and it stayed REGISTERED as a git worktree after its agent was gone. **`plancheck` saw it and reported `0 EXPOSED`, which was TRUE: its work was merged, nothing was at risk, nothing was wrong. And nothing was ever going to reclaim it either.** **THE TWO QUESTIONS ARE DIFFERENT AND ONLY ONE IS INSTRUMENTED.** `strandedwork.mjs` asks *does this work reach anybody* — an EXPOSURE question, pointed at loss. Nothing asks *is this still needed* — a RESIDUE question, pointed at cost. A tree that is clean, merged and abandoned scores perfectly on the first and is invisible to the second, **so the healthier a residue is the more certainly it is ignored.** **AND THE POPULATION IS WIDER THAN THE INSTRUMENTS LOOK:** `retirable.mjs` keys on SESSIONS and their worktrees; this one had no live session. `strandedwork.mjs` enumerates `git worktree list`, which DID see it — the enumeration was right and no arm asked the second question of it. **A scratch checkout OUTSIDE `.claude/worktrees/` belongs to no lane, no session and no sweep.** | M0 · **OPEN, and NOT fixed here.** It is adjacent to `retirable.mjs` (D-402) and to `strandedwork.mjs` but is neither: the predicate would key on a worktree being REGISTERED, CLEAN, MERGED and owned by no live session — the same three conditions D-398 already names, asked of a tree rather than of a session. **The acceptance test is the one this estate keeps needing: it must NOT name a tree a live worker is using**, so its over-strictness arm is the item, exactly as it was for stranded work. **Do not fold it into `strandedwork`** — that arm's whole design argument is that it warns about LOSS, and mixing a cost signal into a loss signal is how both get tuned out. |

| D-389 | defect | 2026-09-16 | **`truncated: false` RESTS ON THE OVER-FETCH BEING WIDE ENOUGH TO ABSORB THE FENCE, AND ON A FULL RAW FETCH IT IS A COVERAGE CLAIM THE READER CANNOT SUPPORT. All THREE arms of `op=frontier` share it.** Raised by REC-109 while closing D-385, and raised rather than fixed for a stated reason: it is a property of the OVER-FETCH MECHANISM, which the document arm (REC-103), the content arm (REC-109) and the meaning arm (REC-95/CONDUCT #11) all use, so fixing it in one arm of three is the mirror-and-drift class in the one place it would be most dangerous. **THE SHAPE.** Each arm fetches `R` raw rows (`(cap + 1) * 2` at document and content, `(cap + 1) * 3` at meaning), gates them, then cuts at `cap`. When the raw fetch comes back SHORT of `R` the supply is exhausted, the gated list is complete, and `truncated` is sound in both directions. **When it comes back FULL, rows beyond it were never fetched and their visibility is unknown — so a gated list of `cap` or fewer is published with `truncated: false`, which claims a completeness the method did not establish.** It is unsound only when the fence drops more than half a full over-fetched page (a third, at meaning), which is why the factor is doing real work and not decoration. **WHAT CLOSING IT TAKES, and the analysis is done so the next owner does not re-derive it.** The sound form adds one disjunct: `raw.length === R`, i.e. *the supply was not exhausted, so do not read this as complete*. **AND IT LEAKS NOTHING, which was checked rather than assumed** — on a full raw fetch a viewer entitled to every row ALSO has `page.length > cap` and so ALSO reads `true`, so the new disjunct never distinguishes the withheld viewer from the entitled one; on an exhausted fetch it is false for everybody and the flag falls back to each caller's own list. The cost is that `truncated` reads `true` on any instance whose supply exceeds the over-fetch, which is the fail-safe direction for a coverage claim and is what a cursor-less paged read is entitled to say. **Whether that is acceptable across all three arms at once is the decision, and it is a disclosure-adjacent read change (I3) rather than an implementation choice.** | M3 · RECORD · open — raised by REC-109 2026-09-16, region-bounded and reported rather than absorbed. PINNED so the next reader meets the decision rather than the defect: `observation-content.test.mjs` arm G5 names this row. Wants a queue row spanning all three arms. |

That is the trap this project has paid for repeatedly: a bench at real size finds what a suite at small scale cannot. **REC-109 did NOT widen its claim to fix it, but did refuse to make it worse**, which is the half that belonged to this item: the over-fetch to `(cap + 1) * 2` would have doubled the worst case to 402, so the `IN` list was narrowed from the RAW page to the PUBLISHED cut (`pageCut`), which both holds the worst case at `cap` — strictly better than the 201 it inherited — and stops computing an index state for rows the answer then discards. **WHAT CLOSING IT TAKES:** chunk the subject list at 64 the way D-36's other guarded sites do, merging the per-chunk results into the one `Map`, and a bench at 200+ subjects to prove the ceiling is where D-36 says it is rather than inheriting a number measured on 2026-07-25. **The same question should be asked of `#frontierMeaning` and of every other `IN (${marks})` built from a `limit`-bounded page** — not swept here, and the sweep is named rather than claimed. | M3 · RECORD · open — raised by REC-109 2026-09-16 and NOT hit; the exposure was reduced in the same turn rather than enlarged. | D-393 | gap | 2026-09-17 | **THE HEARTBEAT THAT DRIVES THE QUEUE HAS NEVER BEEN SEEN TO DO THE ONE THING IT EXISTS FOR.** `conduct-heartbeat` (`~/.claude/scheduled-tasks/conduct-heartbeat/SKILL.md`, every 20 min in a session of its OWN, which is why it survives a session ending and is not blocked while CONDUCT integrates) closes the defect measured 2026-09-17: CONDUCT closed a wave at 23:18 and sat **IDLE 9 h 20 m with 23 runnable rows** while every board read green — *a loop that depends on a session continuing is not a loop*, and GREEN AND STOPPED LOOK IDENTICAL. **THREE of its five behaviours are DRIVEN on real scheduled runs:** it executes from disk in a fresh session; it IDENTIFIES the integrator by rule (newest session matching `CONDUCT` / `CONDUCT #<n>`, older matches are retired predecessors, never itself) — the last run enumerated all four candidates with a verdict for each; and it STANDS OFF a busy integrator, sending nothing and writing no state. **TWO ARE NOT DRIVEN, and they are the two that matter: (a) IDLE-WITH-WORK -> sends a turn, the behaviour the task exists for, and (b) NO-INTEGRATOR -> PushNotification to Bob.** Neither can be manufactured without breaking live work — (a) needs the integrator idle while the queue is non-empty, (b) needs NO session matching the pattern — so **this row exists because the states are real, routine, and will occur on their own; the gap is that nobody is watching for them.** **WHY IT IS A ROW AND NOT A NOTE: a green run is not evidence a conditioned arm fires, and this estate has paid for that three times in two days** — an arm that cannot fire, a spec that would have blinded the arm to its own window, and a predicate that fired on a healthy state. **A REPORTED RETIREMENT IS THE CHEAPEST WAY TO CLOSE (b)**: the gap between a CONDUCT standing down and its successor being clicked is exactly the no-integrator state, it happened once on 2026-09-17, and nobody was watching the heartbeat during it. **WHAT CLOSING IT TAKES:** read the heartbeat run that lands in each state (`list_task_runs` then `list_events` on the run's session) and confirm from the run's OWN OUTPUT that (a) sent exactly one message naming the tip sha and wrote `last-poke.json`, and (b) sent the push notification and named the absence — **the positive artifact, never the exit status.** Also unproven and closable in the same pass: the SECOND-POKE ESCALATION, which fires when a poke leaves `origin/main` unmoved and exists because a stood-down session and an available one are indistinguishable from outside. Relation: `kickoffs/CONDUCT.md`'s paragraph on the session-only cron, which predicted this failure and whose durable half was never built; D-288, the same *nobody is watching* shape one altitude down. | M0 · OPEN — the mechanism is LIVE and 3/5 driven; BOB #12 armed an idle-notice on CONDUCT #2 so (a) is checked when it next goes idle, and (b) is owed at the next retirement. **Do not close on a green run — close on the two run transcripts.** **AND THE STATE THAT DRIVES THE FIRST ARM IS NOW SET UP AND WAITING, 2026-09-17 17:xx: CONDUCT #2 IS IDLE WITH 18 QUEUED ROWS AND ZERO LIVE WORKERS** — the exact idle-with-work condition the poke arm has never met, because every run so far has found the integrator BUSY (three times) or has not run. **The heartbeat is DISABLED right now** (paused deliberately for a network outage), so **THE VERY FIRST RUN AFTER IT IS RE-ENABLED WILL LAND ON THIS STATE.** Whoever re-enables it should read that run's own transcript — `list_task_runs` then `list_events` on its session — and confirm from the run's OWN OUTPUT that it sent exactly one message naming the tip sha and wrote `last-poke.json`. **Do not let that run pass unread: it is the only arm of this instrument that has never fired, it is the one the instrument exists for, and the condition for driving it is rare enough that three scheduled runs missed it.** |
| D-394 | gap | 2026-09-17 | **A REFRESHED DOCUMENT PRODUCES NEW CONTENT ROWS THAT ARE RELATED TO THE OLD ONES BY NOTHING, SO A MEMBER WHOSE CASE RESTS ON A PASSAGE IS NEVER TOLD A NEWER VERSION OF IT EXISTS.** Raised by Bob's question of 2026-09-17 — *is content updated when a refreshed version of a document is obtained* — and the answer separates into two cases that look like one. **CASE 1, RE-EXTRACTION (the SAME bytes under a better engine): BUILT and correct.** The content row's identity includes the extraction CHAIN, so a re-read yields a new row for the same extent; `REC-82`'s writer carries the prior referent forward when target and extent match, marks it `stale`, and leaves the move to a member's act, with the control that proves it can fail in the suite. D-252 records what that narrowly avoided: `inquiry_basis` is delete-then-insert re-projected on every promotion, so a naive projection would have SILENTLY RE-POINTED every authored citation the first time anything re-read the document — the move Bob ruled the record never makes (5.8). **CASE 2, A REFRESHED DOCUMENT (NEW bytes, a new `capture_sha`): NOT BUILT, AND NOT ROWED UNTIL NOW.** The new capture IS extracted and indexed — `promote` writes its `capture_text` and its content rows — so content is CREATED for the new version. **What does not exist is any relation between the new rows and the old.** `BIO_Content_Framework_v0_10.md` §18 rules the doctrine: an authored edge is never re-pointed to a newer capture without a member's act, the content row's identity includes the capture, a new capture is a new row, and *"the same passage across versions" is a derived relation between rows, PROPOSED AND NEVER APPLIED*. **The never-applied half is ruled and right; the PROPOSED half is designed nowhere and built nowhere** — driven 2026-09-17: ZERO occurrences of any cross-capture content relation in `store.mjs`, and no row or debt entry in the queue or this file (the only `re-point` hits are D-252's chain case). **THE CONSEQUENCE IS A TRUSTWORTHINESS GAP RATHER THAN AN OVERCLAIM, WHICH IS WHY NO EXISTING INSTRUMENT CATCHES IT:** the member's citation stays honestly pointed at the capture it was made against, every grade stays earned, nothing claims more than it can support — and a case may rest on a passage the publisher has since revised while the record holds BOTH captures and says nothing. Every gate here is pointed at the record claiming too much; this is the record knowing something and not telling anyone. **THE SUBSTRATE EXISTS**, which is what makes this a gap and not a research question: `op=versionchain` already answers for one document address, and captures carry their shas. **WHAT CLOSING IT TAKES:** a design decision first, and it is doctrine-adjacent enough to name here rather than assume — WHEN the correspondence is derived (at capture of the newer version, or lazily at read), WHAT it is attached to (the content row, the edge, or a read-time answer), and HOW it is shown so that a proposal is never mistaken for a re-pointing. The extent-match test `REC-82` already uses for the chain case is the obvious candidate and is NOT obviously right across versions, where a revision may move a passage between pages. Relation: `BIO_Content_Framework_v0_10.md` §18 piece 1, D-164, D-252, `REC-82`. | M4 · **DESIGNED 2026-09-17 — `BIO_Content_Framework_v0_10.md` §18.1, which is the home that already ruled the doctrine. The three decisions this row named are made.** **WHEN: lazily at READ, never at capture** — a correspondence derived at capture is a STORED CLAIM and goes stale the moment either extraction improves, which is this corpus's most-repeated failure; a read-time answer cannot go stale and costs nothing for the refreshed captures nobody cites. **WHAT: a read-time answer about a PAIR, attached to nothing persistent.** Not the content row (§14.4 puts the capture inside its identity) and not the edge (an edge gaining a pointer to a newer capture IS the re-pointing). **Attaching it to nothing is the MECHANISM rather than an omission: the strongest guarantee a proposal is never mistaken for a re-pointing is to make it impossible to persist one** — it cannot then be followed by accident, re-projected on promotion, or inherited as fact. **HOW: a notice on the citation in three states, and the third is the design.** This row was right that `REC-82`'s extent test is not obviously right across versions, so extent-match is admitted as a signal for a CANDIDATE and never as evidence of identity; where it fails the answer is **UNDETERMINED**, not a guess. **THE DECOMPOSITION THAT MADE IT TRACTABLE: the valuable half does not need the hard half.** The trustworthiness gap is *never told a newer version exists*, which `op=versionchain` can answer TODAY with certainty; WHICH PASSAGE survives is separate, harder and worth much less. **Collapsing the two is what kept both unbuilt.** And undetermined is nearly the whole value here — *a newer version exists and I cannot tell you whether your passage is in it* is exactly what sends a member to look, which is the act the doctrine reserves to them. **STILL OPEN:** nothing is BUILT and no item is rowed — the design is deliberately handed over without a decomposition, because the split above changes what the items are. **And one question is explicitly NOT settled: whether the notice reaches a member whose case is already PUBLISHED, and through what surface, which is a publication question rather than a content one.** |

The asymmetry is measured on both sides: a false suppress costs a stopped board nobody can see (9h20m on 2026-09-16, twelve silent runs here), a false poke costs one message CONDUCT answers in a sentence, and the poke's own text already says *sequencing is yours, this is a turn, not an instruction*. **CLASS: a question asked about the wrong unit — the third instance on 2026-09-17** (D-399's branch name for reachability; a LOCAL-HEAD plancheck arm read as ESTATE-WIDE; this). That is the residue and it is named rather than left. |
| D-401 | defect | 2026-09-17 | **THE STAND-DOWN PROTOCOL RESERVED SESSION CLOSURE TO BOB BY HAND ON A CAPABILITY CLAIM NOBODY HAD TESTED, AND THE CLAIM WAS FALSE.** `kickoffs/BOB.md` and `kickoffs/CONDUCT.md` both said *a session cannot close itself; closing it is the operator's*, and CONDUCT.md went further — **a peer must not do it even having verified it is safe.** Falsified 2026-09-17 by BOB #13 by CALLING THE TOOL instead of re-reading the sentence: `archive_session` accepts a peer's session id, accepts the literal `"self"`, stops the process, and under `bypassPermissions` — this project's default per `CLAUDE.md` — asks nobody. **THE CAPABILITY WAS THERE THE WHOLE TIME; THE PROHIBITION WAS INVENTED.** It is `CLAUDE.md`'s *a blocker is a claim, and nothing here audits one*, and it is **BOB.md's own rule 6 landing on BOB.md a second time** — the *there is no message channel to a running worker* claim was falsified in exactly this way three days earlier, in the same file, and the stand-down step was then written with the same untested premise. **THE MEASURED COST: BOB #12 wrote *this session is ready to be closed* and ran on for hours, holding a 635 MB worktree and LANDING TWO COMMITS TO `main` DURING ITS SUCCESSOR'S SESSION** — two sessions in one lane (DEC-3), and the reason one of them ROWED D-399 while the other FIXED it. **RULED BY BOB THE SAME DAY:** *"BOB #12 being alive is not my problem. The protocol was supposed to be updated so that a lane's retired session was archived without my involvement."* | M0 · **FIXED 2026-09-17 in both kickoffs, and DRIVEN on BOB #12 rather than described.** The act is the SUCCESSOR'S first step, because a retiring session cannot verify its own deadness and the successor already owns the checkout from the click. REC-134 did not, because it is a SIGHT defect and the item was scoped to authority. | D-429 | design-gap | 2026-09-18 | **`op=ratify` OF A PROJECT'S OWN BUNDLE MEETS NEITHER HALF OF §7'S CASE-RATIFICATION RULE, AND THE DESIGN DOES NOT SAY WHETHER A PROJECT BUNDLE MAY BE PUBLISHED AT ALL.** Membership v2 §7's bullet *"A CASE RATIFICATION: who AUTHORISES it and who may DELIVER it"* (BOB #15) decides a CASE: an owner's signature, delivered by a member with a role or the founder. REC-137 built that for `op=caseratify` and, as its row asked, drove `op=ratify` of a PROJECT bundle against the same rule. **Measured, not inferred:** ruth, an enrolled administrator with no role in the project, carried the signature of gus, who is neither an owner nor a participant of it, and the project's own document was PUBLISHED under gus's name (`case-authority.test.mjs` §7 pins it, so the pin fails when this is closed). Also observed in the same probe and NOT pinned: a member who cannot see the project, delivering, is refused `GATE_REFUSED` C-13.1 *"bundle.md is missing"* — the ratifier-scoped image read of a hidden bundle reported as an empty document (REC-53's class, a claim about the record made from a failure to see it). **What it costs:** a project is the group's THINKING, which §7.9 hides even from the invited; publishing its own document puts it in the public corpus on the authority of anybody holding a registered key, delivered by an administrator who directs nothing. **Closing it needs a ruling first (BOB):** (a) a project bundle is never published through `op=ratify` (refuse by type), or (b) it is, under the case rule (an owner's signature; a joined participant or the founder delivering). Recommendation: (a) — a project publishes through its CASES (DEC-72), and nothing in the design asks for the project document itself to cross; reversing it later costs one refusal.
